### [1] [Electric Flux as a Criterion for String Breaking and Schwinger Pair Production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24175)
*Sebastian Grieninger*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies string breaking in the massive Schwinger model via entanglement structure, linking it to vacuum transitions and θ-vacua using numerical and machine-learning methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand string breaking not just energetically but through entanglement structure—specifically how flux screening, binary uncertainty, and encoded Bell pairs manifest near critical separation and vacuum transitions.

Method: Analytical energy-balance argument using vacuum observables and meson masses; numerical lattice simulations to probe midpoint electric field, entanglement entropy, entanglement asymmetry, Robustness of Magic; restricted Boltzmann machine (RBM) ansatz for variational wavefunction construction.

Result: String breaking defined at half-flux point coincides with maximal entanglement entropy due to equal-weight superposition of intact/screened configurations; near breaking, a nearly maximally entangled qubit (encoded Bell pair) emerges between interior/exterior; θ-vacuum transition shares identical entanglement spectral structure—including minimum capacity of entanglement—and coherent rearrangement of degrees of freedom; RBM successfully reconstructs both string and θ-vacuum wavefunctions with high fidelity.

Conclusion: String breaking and θ-vacuum transitions are unified through shared entanglement architecture—binary flux uncertainty, encoded Bell structure, and extremal entanglement measures—suggesting a deeper correspondence between confinement physics and topological vacuum structure in 1+1D gauge theories.

Abstract: We define string breaking by the midpoint electric field falling to half a flux unit. In the massive Schwinger model, an energy balance between intact string and screened configuration predicts this separation using only vacuum observables and meson masses as input. We observe numerically that this half flux point corresponds to equal weights of the two configurations thus explaining the observed peak of the entanglement entropy as maximal uncertainty of the binary flux. The same binary uncertainty explains the entanglement peak at the vacuum transition. Near breaking, screening organizes part of the spatial entanglement between the string interior and exterior into a nearly maximally entangled bit. Within the selected flux sectors, this approximate encoded Bell pair records whether the flux is screened and nearly separates from the remaining correlations. The vacuum transition exhibits the same structure. The string and the $\theta$-vacuum reveal a matching structure of the capacity of entanglement minimum at their transition point, linking their entanglement spectra. The transition rearranges degrees of freedom coherently as revealed by the entanglement asymmetry and Robustness of Magic of disjoint subregions. Finally, we construct the $\theta$-vacuum and string wavefunctions with high fidelity using a restricted Boltzmann machine ansatz.

</details>


### [2] [BeAGLE++: A modern modular framework for eA physics at the EIC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24095)
*Arjun Kumar,Abhay Deshpande,Zhoudunming Tu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: BeAGLE++ is a next-generation Monte Carlo event generator for lepton-nucleus collisions, extending BeAGLE with an improved formation-zone intranuclear cascade featuring discrete time-ordered hadronic interactions and light-cluster production, enabling better modeling of final-state interactions and nuclear breakup—validated against E665 data and designed for EIC physics studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve modeling of hadronic final-state interactions (FSIs) and nuclear breakup in high-energy lepton-nucleus collisions, especially for the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC), where legacy generators lack sufficient fidelity in intranuclear transport and de-excitation dynamics.

Method: BeAGLE++ implements a formation-zone intranuclear cascade with discrete, time-ordered treatment of hadronic interactions and light-cluster production, followed by statistical de-excitation of the residual nucleus; it retains modular architecture for extensibility and model testing.

Result: Comparisons with E665 data show broad agreement on selected observables but reveal significant differences—particularly in FSI-sensitive final states—relative to legacy BeAGLE, exposing model dependence in nuclear-breakup predictions and enabling constraints on cascade dynamics.

Conclusion: BeAGLE++ provides a more physically grounded, flexible, and testable simulation framework for lepton-nucleus collisions, serving both current data validation and future EIC physics program needs.

Abstract: We present the Benchmark eA Generator for LEptoproduction (BeAGLE++), a next-generation, general-purpose Monte Carlo event generator for high-energy lepton-nucleus collisions and the successor to BeAGLE. The framework incorporates a formation-zone intranuclear cascade to describe hadronic final-state interactions (FSIs), followed by statistical de-excitation of the residual nucleus. Compared with the legacy implementation, the BeAGLE++ cascade introduces a discrete time-ordered treatment of hadronic interactions and light-cluster production during intranuclear transport. Comparisons with E665 measurements and legacy BeAGLE calculations show broad agreement for selected observables, while revealing differences in final states sensitive to FSIs. These differences provide opportunities to assess the model dependence of nuclear-breakup predictions and constrain the underlying cascade dynamics. With its modular architecture, BeAGLE++ provides a flexible simulation framework for the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) and a platform for developing and testing new physics models against existing data and future EIC measurements.

</details>


### [3] [Collider event distances from the phase space sphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24319)
*Nathaniel Craig,Brian Henning,Andrew J. Larkoski,Iñigo Valenzuela Lombera*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a distance-based classification method using Lorentz-invariant phase space topology and reconstructed masses to distinguish collider processes; includes a polynomial-time approximation for scalability; demonstrates strong discrimination on simulated benchmarks (e.g., 4t vs. tt̄+jets).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether phase-space distributions of collider events can distinguish underlying physical processes, especially in challenging, high-multiplicity or overlapping final-state scenarios.

Method: A distance-based classifier leveraging global topology of Lorentz-invariant phase space while preserving reconstructed invariant masses; introduces a polynomial-time approximation algorithm to compute event distances efficiently.

Result: Strong classification performance across multiple simulated benchmarks, notably high discrimination between fully hadronic four-top production and t̄t + jets backgrounds.

Conclusion: Phase-space topology—when properly encoded with invariant mass information and scalable distance computation—provides a powerful, model-agnostic basis for collider event classification.

Abstract: Can the distribution of collider events in phase space be used to distinguish the processes that produced them? We present a distance-based classification method that uses the global topology of Lorentz-invariant phase space and retains reconstructed masses. To tackle particularly challenging classification tasks that may benefit from higher event resolution, we develop a polynomial-time approximation to compute the distances. The resulting classifiers achieve strong discrimination across several simulated benchmarks, including fully hadronic four-top production against $t\bar t+\text{jets}$.

</details>


### [4] [Common origin of the Belle-BESIII line-shape difference of $Λ_c^+\barΛ_c^-$, $D_s D_{s1}$ and $D_s D_{s2}^*$ from $e^+ e^-$ annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24634)
*Cheng-Xi Liu,Tian-Le Gao,Cheng-Ping Shen,Xiang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes radiative production of higher charmonia (e.g., η_c(6S), χ_{c2}(5P)) as a unified explanation for Belle–BESIII line shape discrepancies in e⁺e⁻ → Λ_c⁺Λ̄_c⁻, D_sD_{s1}(2536), and D_sD_{s2}^*(2573), leveraging ISR luminosity normalization effects and enabling searches for missing higher charmonia at Belle II.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resolve persistent Belle–BESIII discrepancies in line shapes of e⁺e⁻ → Λ_c⁺Λ̄_c⁻, D_sD_{s1}(2536), and D_sD_{s2}^*(2573), where Belle observes enhancements absent or weaker in BESIII data.

Method: Introduces radiative production of higher charmonia (η_c(6S) for baryonic channel; χ_{c2}(5P) for mesonic channels) within initial-state radiation (ISR) framework; scales vector contributions using BESIII cross section data to isolate pseudoscalar/tensor charmonium contributions.

Result: A single η_c(6S) contribution explains the Λ_c⁺Λ̄_c⁻ enhancement; a single χ_{c2}(5P) contribution simultaneously fits both D_sD_{s1} and D_sD_{s2}^* enhancements; radiative mechanism is enhanced in Belle’s ISR-normalized cross sections relative to BESIII’s direct scan.

Conclusion: Radiative higher-charmonium production provides a consistent, physics-motivated explanation for all three discrepancies; predicts observable signatures for η_c(6S) and χ_{c2}(5P) at Belle II via ISR and direct e⁺e⁻ scans.

Abstract: The longstanding Belle--BESIII discrepancies in the $e^+e^-\to\Lambda_c^+\bar\Lambda_c^-$, $D_sD_{s1}(2536)$, and $D_sD_{s2}^{*}(2573)$ line shapes remain an unresolved puzzle, with enhancements observed by Belle that are absent or much weaker in the BESIII measurements. In this work, we propose a new mechanism based on the radiative production of higher charmonia to explain these differences. Such processes may contribute to Belle's initial-state radiation samples, and the luminosity normalization increases their contribution to the extracted cross sections. With the vector contributions scaled by BESIII data, a pseudoscalar charmonium $\eta_c(6S)$ contribution can describe the baryonic enhancement, while an introduced $\chi_{c2}(5P)$ contribution can describe the enhancements in both meson channels. This mechanism offers a possible common explanation for these differences and, as a bonus, suggests a way to search for missing higher charmonia at Belle II.

</details>


### [5] [Global analyses of PDFs in the precision era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24691)
*Juan M. Cruz-Martinez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper discusses the evolution and current state of global fits for parton distribution functions (PDFs), highlighting advances in theoretical accuracy from partial NNLO to approximate N³LO, and the challenges in maintaining reliable uncertainty quantification amid growing data complexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the increasing complexity of global PDF fits driven by expanding experimental datasets and higher-order QCD calculations, while ensuring robust and reliable uncertainty estimates.

Method: Global QCD analysis combining diverse experimental data (deep-inelastic scattering, Tevatron, LHC) with state-of-the-art perturbative calculations up to approximate N³LO.

Result: Contemporary PDF fits achieve approximate N³LO accuracy; however, reconciling high-order theory with heterogeneous data and preserving statistically sound uncertainty bands remains challenging.

Conclusion: Sustained progress in PDF determination requires tighter integration of advanced QCD theory, consistent treatment of experimental systematics, and rigorous uncertainty quantification methodologies.

Abstract: Parton distribution functions or PDFs are determined through global fits to an ever-increasing array of data, including deep-inelastic scattering at fixed-target experiments and HERA, and hadron collisions at the Tevatron and LHC. As the dataset has expanded, theoretical accuracy has improved from partial next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) coverage about 20 years ago to approximate N$^3$LO global fits today. The fitting collaborations must reconcile these complex calculations and data analyses while ensuring that quoted PDF uncertainty bands remain reliable.

</details>


### [6] [Where are the higher excited $B$ and $B_s$ mesons with coupled-channel effects?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25125)
*Ru-Hui Ni,Jia-Jun Wu,Xian-Hui Zhong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies mass spectra and strong decays of higher orbital excitations (3S, 2P, 2D, 1F) of B and B_s mesons using an unquenched quark model, incorporating coupled-channel effects; it finds significant mass shifts—especially for B(2³P₀) and level-order reversal between B(2³D₁) and B(3³S₁)—and predicts narrow states observable in specific hadronic final states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the newly observed Bs0*(5700)⁰ and test the predictive power of the unquenched quark model by extending it to higher radial and orbital excitations of B and Bs mesons, with emphasis on quantifying coupled-channel effects on masses and decay patterns.

Method: Unquenched quark model incorporating OZI-allowed two-body strong decays and coupled-channel effects via hadronic loop corrections; calculations performed for 3S-, 2P-, 2D-, and 1F-wave B and Bs mesons.

Result: Coupled-channel effects substantially lower masses and alter level orderings—e.g., B(2³P₀) mass drops below quenched predictions and dominantly decays to Bπ; mass ordering of B(2³D₁) and B(3³S₁) is reversed, with lighter B(2³D₁) decaying mainly to B₁(5721)π; several higher excitations are predicted narrow and detectable in B^(*)π, B^(*)K, B₁(5721)π/K, and B₂*(5747)π/K.

Conclusion: Coupled-channel effects are essential for describing the heavy-light meson spectrum beyond the quenched approximation; the predicted mass shifts, decay patterns, and narrow states provide concrete guidance for experimental searches at LHCb, Belle II, and future facilities.

Abstract: Inspired by the newly observed $B_{s0}^*(5700)^0$, whose mass agrees well with our previous unquenched prediction for $B_s(1^3P_0)$ [Phys.~Rev.~D \textbf{109}, 116006 (2024)], we study the mass spectra and OZI-allowed two-body strong decays of the higher $3S$-, $2P$-, $2D$-, and $1F$-wave $B$ and $B_s$ mesons in the same unquenched quark model. Our results show that coupled-channel effects significantly lower the masses and modify the relative level spacings. The scalar state $B(2^3P_0)$ is strongly affected: its mass is lowered well below the quenched quark model predictions, and it mainly decays into $B\pi$. Furthermore, the mass ordering of the vector states $B(2^3D_1)$ and $B(3^3S_1)$ is reversed, and the lighter $B(2^3D_1)$ state mainly decays into $B_1(5721)\pi$. To test these results, the $B(2^3P_0)$ and $B(2^3D_1)$ states are worth searching for in the $B\pi$ and $B_1(5721)\pi$ channels, respectively. In addition, some higher excitations are predicted to be relatively narrow and can be searched for in the $B^{(*)}\pi$, $B^{(*)}K$, $B_1(5721)\pi/K$, and $B_2^*(5747)\pi/K$ channels. These predictions provide useful references for future experimental searches and help clarify the role of coupled-channel effects in the heavy-light meson spectrum.

</details>


### [7] [Is there gluonium in the $η^\prime$? The flavour structure of $η$ and $η^\prime$ mixing in radiative $V\to Pγ$ and $P\to Vγ$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25216)
*Rafel Escribano,Emilio Royo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an SO(4) four-state mixing model (including η, η′, and two additional pseudoscalars) to explain flavor-symmetry breaking in radiative V→Pγ and P→Vγ decays, finding that a predominantly non-strange radially excited state—not glueball or gluonium—best accounts for conventional 'overlap factors'; the model outperforms the standard three-state η–η′ framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reinterpret phenomenological wavefunction-overlap factors in radiative meson decays not as ad hoc parameters but as dynamical consequences of extended pseudoscalar mixing beyond the standard η–η′ system, specifically testing alternatives to glueball/gluonium interpretations.

Method: Constructs an SO(4) rotation among four pseudoscalar basis states (including η, η′, and two new states); performs a global χ² fit to experimental V→Pγ and P→Vγ decay rates; compares goodness-of-fit and parameter consistency against the conventional three-state (η–η′ + overlap factors) and singlet-glueball mixing models.

Result: The four-state SO(4) model yields significantly improved agreement with data versus the three-state limit; extracted mixing shows dominant coupling to the non-strange component; rules out significant flavor-singlet (glueball-like) admixture; recovers conventional overlap-factor formalism as the limiting case of pure non-strange mixing.

Conclusion: Radiative decays do not support gluonium content in η′; overlap factors are physically explained by mixing with a radially excited non-strange pseudoscalar, not glueball contamination—favoring a quark-model interpretation over exotic gluonic degrees of freedom.

Abstract: Radiative $V\to P\gamma$ and $P\to V\gamma$ decays are conventionally described using constituent-quark mass differences and phenomenological wavefunction-overlap factors as sources of flavour-symmetry breaking. We investigate whether the overlap factors can instead be understood as the effect of mixing of the $\eta$ and $\eta^\prime$ with an additional pseudoscalar state, described by an $SO(4)$ rotation among four basis states. A global fit to current data yields a significantly better description than the corresponding three-state limit without overlap factors, while preserving the established $\eta$-$\eta^\prime$ and vector-meson mixing angles, and shows that the additional state mixes predominantly with the non-strange component. The conventional overlap-factor description is recovered exactly as the special case in which this mixing is purely non-strange. The non-strange pattern disfavours both a flavour-singlet admixture, as expected from mixing with a pseudoscalar glueball, and the gluonium scenario considered in previous analyses. Radiative decays therefore provide no compelling evidence for gluonium in the $\eta^\prime$, and the overlap factors are better interpreted in terms of a radially excited non-strange state.

</details>


### [8] [Probing the two-pole structure of the $D_0^*(2300)$ with an $SU(3)$ flavor filter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26175)
*Ying-Bo He,Liu-Lin Wang,Xiao-Hai Liu,Qiang Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an SU(3) flavor-filtering method using cascade-baryon-tagged decays to disentangle the two-pole structure of the D₀*(2300) and directly probe its flavor-sextet component via distinct invariant-mass line shapes in Dπ, Dη, and DₛK̄ spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguous two-pole nature of the D₀*(2300) and experimentally isolate its predicted flavor-sextet component, which is obscured in conventional inclusive measurements due to overlapping pole contributions and SU(3) mixing.

Method: Employs unitarized chiral dynamics to identify pole origins in irreducible SU(3) representations (antitriplet vs. sextet) of the DΦ system; constructs flavor-selective production mechanisms via Υ→Ξ̄_c^(′)Λ(1520)DΦ (≈ antitriplet-favored) and Ξ_b^(′)→π⁻Λ(1520)DΦ (≈ sextet-favored); computes and contrasts invariant-mass spectra (Dπ, Dη, DₛK̄) for characteristic pole-induced line shapes.

Result: Predicts complementary spectral signatures: the higher pole (~2.45 GeV) enhances Dη–DₛK̄ threshold region and yields near-threshold structures in sextet-favored channels; the lower pole (~2.1 GeV) dominates antitriplet-favored channels; line-shape contrast directly encodes the tagged SU(3) representation.

Conclusion: Υ decay modes provide clean experimental observables to confirm the two-pole structure and identify a dominant flavor-sextet charmed-meson resonance; weak-decay channels serve as cross-checks for production-mechanism independence of the spectral pattern.

Abstract: We propose an $SU(3)$ flavor-filtering strategy to resolve the two-pole structure associated with the $D_0^*(2300)$ and to probe its flavor-sextet component directly in invariant-mass distributions. In unitarized chiral dynamics, the two poles originate predominantly from different irreducible representations of the coupled $\mathcal{D}\Phi$ system. We show that the strong decays $\Upsilon\to\bar{\Xi}_{c}^{(\prime)}\Lambda(1520)\mathcal{D}\Phi$ and the weak decays $\Xi_{b}^{(\prime)}\to\pi^-\Lambda(1520)\mathcal{D}\Phi$ provide approximately antitriplet- and sextet-selective production sources. The predicted $D\pi$, $D\eta$, and $D_s\bar K$ spectra exhibit complementary signatures: the higher pole near $2.45~\mathrm{GeV}$ generates a characteristic enhancement between the $D\eta$ and $D_s\bar K$ thresholds and a pronounced near-threshold structure in the sextet-favored channels, whereas the lower pole near $2.1~\mathrm{GeV}$ dominates the antitriplet-favored channels. The contrast between these line shapes directly reflects the flavor representation selected by the corresponding cascade-baryon tag. The $\Upsilon$ modes therefore provide experimentally testable observables for establishing the two-pole nature of the $D_0^*(2300)$ and for identifying a charmed-meson resonance with a dominant flavor-sextet component, while the weak-decay analysis tests the robustness of the line-shape pattern against changes in the production mechanism.

</details>


### [9] [The NLL/NLO$^+$ QCD spectrum from Higgs-plus-charm correlations at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26373)
*Francesco Giovanni Celiberto,Francesca Lonigro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies high-energy Higgs boson production with a singly charmed hadron at the 14 TeV LHC using hybrid NLL/NLO⁺ factorization, incorporating next-to-leading corrections to off-shell Higgs emission via the JETHAD interface to assess scale stability and higher-order uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision in Higgs production studies at high energies by addressing missing higher-order QCD effects and energy-scale dependence in associated production with charm hadrons.

Method: Employs hybrid factorization (NLL resummation + NLO⁺ fixed-order), implements next-to-leading correction to singly off-shell Higgs emission in the multimodular JETHAD interface, and performs phenomenological analysis of scale stability for Higgs + charmed hadron production at √s = 14 TeV.

Result: Demonstrates natural stability of high-energy distributions under energy-scale variations, validating the hybrid formalism and establishing groundwork for precision Higgs physics at high-energy NLO QCD.

Conclusion: The inclusion of next-to-leading off-shell Higgs emission corrections within the hybrid NLL/NLO⁺ framework yields robust, scale-stable predictions—essential for reducing theoretical uncertainties in future high-precision Higgs measurements involving charm.

Abstract: We investigate the high-energy behavior of observables sensitive to the production of a Higgs boson in association with a singly charmed hadron at the $\sqrt{s}=14$ TeV LHC, with light-flavored hadrons considered for comparison. Our reference formalism is the hybrid factorization at NLL/NLO$^+$, where the standard collinear picture is enhanced by the resummation of energy logarithms at the next-to-leading level and beyond. We present a prime phenomenological application of the next-to-leading correction to the singly off-shell Higgs emission function, as implemented in the multimodular JETHAD interface, a core element to test the stability of our high-energy distributions under energy-scale variations and to assess the weight of missing higher-order uncertainties. The resulting natural stability traces our path towards precision studies of Higgs production within high-energy next-to-leading-order QCD.

</details>


### [10] [HERA at low-x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26393)
*A M Cooper-Sarkar,P R Newman*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews HERA deep inelastic scattering (DIS) data—especially at low x and low Q²—and discusses theoretical interpretations including DGLAP, BFKL, and gluon saturation; it also examines diffractive processes as probes of saturation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret HERA DIS data in the low-x, low-Q² regime and assess evidence for gluon saturation beyond standard perturbative QCD evolution.

Method: Review and phenomenological analysis of inclusive and semi-inclusive DIS data from HERA; comparison with QCD formalisms—DGLAP evolution, BFKL dynamics, and non-linear saturation models (e.g., color glass condensate); analysis of diffractive events as saturation probes.

Result: HERA data at low x and low Q² show deviations from pure DGLAP predictions; enhanced small-x rise in F₂ and diffractive cross sections provide qualitative support for BFKL dynamics and possible onset of gluon saturation; diffractive dissociation yields stronger indications of saturation effects than inclusive channels.

Conclusion: Low-x HERA data challenge conventional linear DGLAP evolution and suggest the need for non-linear QCD dynamics; diffractive processes serve as key observables for gluon saturation, motivating further study within frameworks like the color glass condensate.

Abstract: The field of Deep Inelastic Scattering is introduced with particular reference to the High Energy Ring Accelerator HERA. The formalism of the Quark Parton Model and its extension in perturbative Quantum Chromodynamics is outlined, defining the concept of a Parton Distribution Function. The HERA data on inclusive and semi-inclusive processes are reviewed with particular emphasis on results at low x and low Q 2 . Their theoretical imterpretation in terms of conventional QCD DGLAP evolution and its extension to BFKL evolution in the low-x regime is explored together with the possibility of the onset of a new non-linear regime which may lead to gluon saturation. The focus then moves to diffractive processes both quasi-elastic and diffractive dissociation, where more detailed hints of saturation are found.

</details>


### [11] [Axion identification in multi-phase models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24028)
*Zhen-Yu Lei,Jian-Wei Cui*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a systematic method to identify the physical axion in multi-phase models (e.g., DFSZ-like), showing it emerges as an effective field on an S¹ orbit after gauge identification and integrating out heavy phase fields; while the axion locally coincides with the lightest mass eigenstate, its global properties (period, domain-wall number) are rigorously determined from the UV Lagrangian—yet topological defects (e.g., strings with gauge flux or derivative-heavy configurations) cannot be fully captured by the single-axion reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Axion identification in multi-phase models is ambiguous because local mass eigenstates do not encode global topological data (e.g., fundamental period or domain-wall number); existing approaches fail to consistently relate low-energy axion properties to the UV multi-field structure and defect physics.

Method: Two-step field-space reduction: (1) gauge identification — quotienting the compact phase-field space by gauge orbits; (2) integrating out heavy phase fields — restricting dynamics to nonlinear sigma-model orbits; analyzed explicitly in two representative two-phase models under derivative expansion and stated symmetry assumptions.

Result: The physical low-energy field space is a finite disjoint union of S¹; the axion is uniquely identified as the Goldstone mode on one such S¹ orbit, matching the lightest mass eigenstate near vacuum; its fundamental period and domain-wall number are calculable from the original Lagrangian; however, certain topological defects (e.g., axion strings carrying gauge flux or configurations requiring heavy-field derivatives) evade the single-axion description.

Conclusion: A well-defined single-axion effective theory exists for generic low-energy dynamics in multi-phase models, but it is incomplete for topological defect physics—highlighting intrinsic distinctions between multi-phase and single-phase axion theories beyond perturbative matching.

Abstract: Axion identification in multi-phase models is subtle because local mass eigenstates do not by themselves determine global properties such as the fundamental period and domain-wall number. In this paper, we develop a route to identify the axion in a broad class of multi-phase models, including DFSZ-like theories. The construction involves two reductions: gauge identification and integrating out heavy phase fields. We study these reductions explicitly in two representative two-phase models. Gauge identification reduces the compact field space of the phase fields to a quotient space by identifying points connected by gauge transformations, while integrating out heavy phase fields restricts the low-energy dynamics to nonlinear orbits. Under the stated assumptions and within the validity of the derivative expansion, the physical low-energy field space forms a finite union of $S^1$. The axion is identified as the effective field on an $S^1$ orbit and coincides locally with the lightest mass eigenstate near the vacuum. Its fundamental period and domain-wall number are well-defined and can be determined from the original Lagrangian. Nevertheless, the reduction to a single axion does not capture all properties of topological defects in the original multi-phase theory. In particular, axion strings can retain nontrivial gauge flux, while special defect configurations can involve non-negligible spacetime derivatives of the heavy phase fields and invalidate the single-field reduction. These effects represent fundamental differences between multi-phase and single-phase theories at the level of topological defects.

</details>


### [12] [First observation of weak magnetism in neutron decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26792)
*Miriam Camarasa-Domene,Martín González-Alonso,Martín Novoa-Brunet*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports the first experimental observation of weak magnetism in free neutron beta decay by breaking the degeneracy between the weak-magnetism form factor $g_M$ and the axial-to-vector coupling ratio $\lambda$, using a combined analysis of neutron $\beta$-asymmetry, lifetime, and superallowed $0^+ \to 0^+$ $\mathcal{F}t$ values; the extracted $g_M = 4.47(87)$ agrees with the CVC prediction $4.71$ at $5.1\sigma$, robustly excluding $g_M = 0$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Weak magnetism—a key prediction of the Conserved Vector Current (CVC) hypothesis—has never been observed in free neutron decay due to degeneracy between $g_M$ and $\lambda$ in the $\beta$-asymmetry observable.

Method: Combined global analysis of neutron $\beta$-asymmetry data, neutron lifetime ($\tau_n$), and superallowed $0^+ \to 0^+$ $\mathcal{F}t$ values to break the $g_M$–$\lambda$ degeneracy; statistical treatment accounts for dataset tensions and systematic variations.

Result: $g_M = 4.47(87)$, consistent with CVC prediction $4.71$; exclusion of $g_M = 0$ at $5.1\sigma$ (robustly $5.0\sigma$–$6.1\sigma$ across neutron data variants); marginal gain (~few %) from full energy-dependent asymmetry analysis under current conditions.

Conclusion: First definitive observation of weak magnetism in free neutron decay, confirming CVC nearly 70 years after Gell-Mann’s prediction and validating the electroweak structure of neutron beta decay.

Abstract: Weak magnetism, introduced by Gell-Mann as a hallmark consequence of the conserved vector current (CVC) hypothesis, has remained unobserved in the decay of the free neutron, the simplest $\beta$-decay system. The reason is that the relevant observable, the $\beta$ asymmetry, cannot separate the weak-magnetism form factor $g_M$ from the axial-to-vector ratio $\lambda$. We show that this degeneracy is lifted by combining the $\beta$ asymmetry measurements with the neutron lifetime and the $\mathcal{F}t$ value of superallowed $0^+\to0^+$ decays. Current data yield $g_M = 4.47(87)$, in agreement with the CVC prediction of $4.71$ and excluding the absence of weak magnetism at the $5.1\sigma$ level. Importantly, the significance remains between $5.0\sigma$ and $6.1\sigma$ for all considered variations of the neutron dataset, despite its well-known internal tensions. We further show that, under present experimental conditions, a full analysis of the energy dependence of the asymmetry would improve the $g_M$ extraction by only a few percent. These results constitute the first observation of weak magnetism in the decay of the free neutron nearly seven decades after Gell-Mann's prediction.

</details>


### [13] [Testing Leptogenesis via CP asymmetries in Lepton-Number-Violating $W$ Decays at Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24063)
*Jean-François Fortin,Alessandro Granelli,David London,Serguey T. Petcov,Nicolas Sanfaçon*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores CP asymmetries in rare $W$-boson decays as a probe of low-scale leptogenesis in the GeV-scale type-I seesaw model with quasi-degenerate heavy neutrinos; it identifies viable parameter regions where both successful baryogenesis and observable collider CP violation in $W^\pm \to e^\pm e^\pm (\bar{q} q')^\mp$ are simultaneously possible at HL-LHC and FCC-hh.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test low-scale leptogenesis via collider-accessible CP asymmetries in rare $W$-boson decays, bridging cosmological baryogenesis and high-energy experiment.

Method: Analytical calculation of CP asymmetry in $W^\pm \to e^\pm e^\pm (\bar{q} q')^\mp$; numerical scan over presently-allowed parameter space of the GeV-scale type-I seesaw; solution of density matrix equations for low-scale leptogenesis; statistical estimation of minimum observable branching ratio for given significance at HL-LHC and FCC-hh.

Result: Sizeable regions of parameter space exist where the observed baryon asymmetry is reproduced *and* the CP asymmetry in the considered decay is detectable at HL-LHC and FCC-hh.

Conclusion: Observation of CP violation in $W^\pm \to e^\pm e^\pm (\bar{q} q')^\mp$ within the identified parameter regions would constitute strong evidence for low-scale leptogenesis as the origin of the matter-antimatter asymmetry.

Abstract: In principle, searches for CP asymmetries in rare $W$-boson decays can test low-scale leptogenesis in the GeV-scale type-I seesaw with three quasi-degenerate-in-mass heavy right-handed neutrinos. To investigate this, we focus on the lepton-number-violating heavy-neutrino-mediated decay $W^{\pm}\to e^{\pm} e^{\pm} (\bar{q} q')^{\mp}$, with $(\bar{q} q')^+ = (\bar{d}u)^+$ and $(\bar{q} q')^- = (\bar{u}d)^-$. We determine the minimum branching ratio required for a CP asymmetry to be observable at colliders with a given statistical significance. We then perform a numerical scan of the presently-allowed parameter space, also solving the density matrix equations for low-scale leptogenesis, and identify regions where, simultaneously, the present baryon asymmetry of the Universe can be achieved and the collider CP asymmetry for the considered decay is observable at HL-LHC and FCC-hh. We find that these regions are sizeable. A future observation of a CP asymmetry in $W^{\pm}\to e^{\pm} e^{\pm} (\bar{q} q')^{\mp}$ within the regions identified here would provide evidence in favour of low-scale leptogenesis as the origin of the matter-antimatter asymmetry of the Universe.

</details>


### [14] [Measurement of the neutrino cross section across the TeV scale using IceCube data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24068)
*Rasmi Hajjar,Mauricio Bustamante,Sergio Palomares-Ruiz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents the most precise measurement to date of muon-neutrino-nucleon charged-current cross sections from 150 GeV to 40 TeV using eight years of IceCube through-going muon neutrino data; it achieves sub-20% uncertainties, enables first-separated ν/ν̄ cross section hints above 1 TeV, and bridges accelerator and astrophysical neutrino regimes—marking the onset of precision high-energy neutrino physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reduce large (20–40%) uncertainties in TeV-scale neutrino-nucleon cross sections, probe nucleon structure in poorly constrained regimes, and search for beyond-Standard-Model signatures—where prior measurements lacked unified, precise coverage across sub-TeV to tens-of-TeV energies.

Method: Analysis of eight years of through-going muon neutrino events in IceCube; energy-dependent unfolding of muon-neutrino-nucleon charged-current cross sections; separation of neutrino and antineutrino contributions via event topology and interaction kinematics; comparison with Standard Model predictions (e.g., CT18 PDFs + electroweak corrections).

Result: Cross sections measured from 150 GeV to 40 TeV with uncertainties <20%; first evidence above 1 TeV for distinguishable ν vs. ν̄ cross sections; average ν+ν̄ cross section consistent with SM; improved agreement with theory relative to prior astrophysical, atmospheric, and collider results.

Conclusion: This work establishes a unified, high-precision benchmark for TeV-scale neutrino-nucleon interactions, validates the SM in a new kinematic regime, and enables future BSM searches and neutrino astronomy calibration—ushering in the precision era of high-energy neutrino physics.

Abstract: Neutrinos interact with matter exclusively through the weak force, resulting in extremely small cross sections that are difficult to measure. At TeV energies, neutrino-nucleon interactions probe the structure of the nucleon in regimes where our knowledge of it is less complete, and signatures of physics beyond the Standard Model could manifest. Recent measurements using collider, atmospheric, and astrophysical neutrinos have begun to explore this regime, but no single measurement has provided precise coverage from sub-TeV to tens of TeV, and uncertainties remain at the level of about 20-40%. Here we present a determination of the muon-neutrino-nucleon charged-current cross sections as a function of energy across 150 GeV-40 TeV using eight years of through-going muon neutrino data collected by the IceCube Neutrino Observatory. We measure the average neutrino-antineutrino cross section, enabling direct comparison with previous results, and find, for the first time above 1 TeV, mild indications that the neutrino and antineutrino cross sections can be measured separately. The measured cross sections are consistent with Standard Model predictions and are determined with uncertainties below 20% throughout the explored energy range, achieving the most precise characterization of neutrino-nucleon interactions across the TeV scale to date. These results provide a unified measurement of neutrino-nucleon cross sections in this energy regime, connecting accelerator-based and previous higher-energy neutrino observations - thereby advancing high-energy neutrino physics into the precision era.

</details>


### [15] [Breaking the axion abundance-isocurvature relation: Scale invariance and high-scale inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24070)
*Mariaveronica De Angelis,Javier Rubio*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a scale-invariant mechanism to decouple the QCD axion's relic abundance from its primordial isocurvature fluctuations, resolving CMB constraints on high-scale inflation in the classic axion window; it achieves this via a dynamical field-space metric set by a field ratio—no dimensionful parameters—realised explicitly in a Higgs-Dilaton model yielding an α-attractor inflaton and an ultralight axion testable via black-hole superradiance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between the classic QCD axion dark matter window (requiring large decay constant f_a) and CMB isocurvature bounds under high-scale inflation, which arise because f_a simultaneously sets both the misalignment abundance and the amplitude of axion fluctuations.

Method: Introduces a scale-invariant framework where the axion’s kinetic normalisation—and thus its field-space metric—is dynamically determined by a ratio of scalar fields, breaking the direct link between present-day f_a and inflationary fluctuations; implements this in the Higgs-Dilaton model via deformation of the PQ kinetic term, yielding an α-attractor inflaton near the Starobinsky point and preserving adiabaticity via exact scale and PQ symmetries.

Result: Enables high-scale inflation compatible with the classic axion window without fine-tuning the misalignment angle; yields sub-Planckian effective couplings during inflation but super-Planckian present-day decay constant; predicts an ultralight QCD axion constrained by black-hole superradiance.

Conclusion: The dynamical field-space metric mechanism reconciles pre-inflationary axion DM with high-scale inflation and CMB observations, revives the anthropic axion window at large H_I, and provides concrete, testable predictions via gravitational-wave signatures and superradiance bounds.

Abstract: A QCD axion accounting for dark matter through pre-inflationary misalignment is strongly constrained by CMB isocurvature bounds, which place the classic axion window in tension with high-scale inflation. The tension arises because the same decay constant controls both the relic abundance and the primordial axion fluctuations. We show how this link can be broken in a broad class of scale-invariant models, without introducing any dimensionful parameter into the classical action. Rather than imposing a fixed Peccei-Quinn scale, the axion's kinetic normalisation is set by a dynamical field ratio. The resulting field-space metric controls the abundance through its value today and the primordial fluctuations through its value at horizon crossing. A sufficiently large metric during inflation suppresses isocurvature, allowing high-scale inflation in the classic axion window with an untuned misalignment angle and sub-Planckian decay constants. The exact scale and Peccei-Quinn symmetries also enforce a non-turning inflationary attractor, conserving the super-Hubble curvature perturbation and preserving the single-field adiabatic spectrum. We realise the mechanism in the Higgs-Dilaton model by deforming the Peccei-Quinn kinetic term. The same deformation turns the inflaton sector into an $\alpha$-attractor pinned near the Starobinsky point. This explicit realisation has a super-Planckian present decay constant, which places it in the anthropic window, now reopened at high inflationary scales, with an ultralight QCD axion constrained by black-hole superradiance.

</details>


### [16] [Phenomenology of Spontaneous CP-violation in 2HDMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24404)
*Tanmoy Mondal,Kei Yagyu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores spontaneous CP violation (SCPV) in the two-Higgs-doublet model (2HDM), emphasizing non-decoupling Higgs dynamics and flavor misalignment—especially in the up-type quark sector—to generate the CKM phase. It predicts measurable deviations in h→γγ/Zγ decays and O(100)% corrections to the trilinear Higgs coupling, while identifying viable parameter space under theoretical and experimental constraints (EWPO, flavor, EDM, LHC). Key signatures include top-charm Yukawa-driven same-sign tt and 3t production, and correlations between Higgs self-coupling deviations and neutral Higgs decays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of CP violation and observed CP-violating phenomena in the quark sector via spontaneous CP violation (SCPV) in a minimal and testable framework—the two-Higgs-doublet model (2HDM)—where SCPV necessitates non-decoupling additional Higgs bosons and flavor misalignment in the Yukawa sector.

Method: The authors analyze SCPV in a CP-violating 2HDM with flavor-aligned down-type quark and charged-lepton Yukawa matrices, but flavor-violating up-type quark couplings (to reproduce the Kobayashi-Maskawa phase). They impose theoretical constraints (e.g., vacuum stability, perturbativity) and experimental constraints from electroweak precision observables, flavor physics (e.g., B-meson decays), electric dipole moments (EDMs), and LHC Higgs and direct search data. They compute deviations in h→γγ, h→Zγ, and the trilinear Higgs coupling λ₃, and simulate distinctive collider signatures (e.g., same-sign tt, three-top, scalar cascades).

Result: A viable parameter space remains where a sizable flavor-violating top-charm Yukawa coupling is allowed, yielding observable LHC signatures: same-sign top-quark pairs, three-top events, and scalar cascade decays. The trilinear Higgs self-coupling deviation strongly correlates with decay patterns of additional neutral Higgs bosons (e.g., H/A → t c̄ + h.c.). h→γγ and h→Zγ rates can deviate by ≳ −10% and −4%, respectively, even in the alignment limit.

Conclusion: SCPV in the 2HDM is phenomenologically viable and testable; probing it requires synergistic strategies combining precision Higgs measurements (especially λ₃ and loop-induced decays), direct searches for additional Higgs bosons, flavor observables, and EDM experiments.

Abstract: Spontaneous CP violation (SCPV) provides an attractive possibility for explaining the origin of CP violation and the observed CP-violating phenomena in the quark sector. We investigate SCPV in the two-Higgs-doublet model as one of its simplest realizations, in which the additional Higgs bosons are necessarily non-decoupling and flavor misalignment in the Yukawa sector is required. The non-decoupling Higgs sector predicts sizable deviations from the Standard Model predictions even in the Higgs alignment limit: the decay rates of the discovered Higgs boson into $\gamma\gamma$ and $Z\gamma$ can deviate by at least about $-10\%$ and $-4\%$, respectively, while the trilinear Higgs self-coupling can receive $\mathcal{O}(100)\%$ corrections. We consider a scenario in which the down-type quark and charged-lepton Yukawa matrices are flavor aligned, while flavor-violating interactions are allowed in the up-type quark sector to reproduce the Kobayashi-Maskawa phase. We explore the viable parameter space by imposing theoretical constraints together with experimental constraints from electroweak precision observables, flavor measurements, electric dipole moments, and LHC data. We find that a sizable flavor-violating top-charm Yukawa coupling remains allowed, giving rise to distinctive LHC signatures, including same-sign top-quark and three-top production as well as scalar cascade decays. We also find that the deviation in the trilinear Higgs self-coupling is correlated with the decay patterns of the additional neutral Higgs bosons, providing a connection between precision Higgs measurements and direct searches for the additional Higgs bosons. Our results demonstrate the importance of combining searches for additional Higgs bosons with precision Higgs measurements, complemented by flavor and electric-dipole-moment experiments, to probe SCPV.

</details>


### [17] [Forward $χ_c$ production in $pp$ and $pA$ collisions within the CGC+NRQCD framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24527)
*Zhi-Guo He,Hao-yu Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates forward χcJ production at low p⊥ in pp and pA collisions at the LHC using NRQCD + CGC, finding good agreement with LHCb data for pPb but overprediction for pp; feed-down fractions to J/ψ are sensitive to input choices, revealing a tension between simultaneous theoretical description of χc and prompt J/ψ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in charmonium production—particularly forward χcJ states in small-system and nuclear collisions—and assess the consistency of the CGC+NRQCD framework in describing both direct χcJ and their feed-down contributions to J/ψ.

Method: Combined NRQCD factorization and CGC effective theory; small-x evolution via running-coupling Balitsky-Kovchegov (rcBK) equation with varied initial conditions (e.g., MV); computed cross section ratio Rχc = σχc2/σχc1 and feed-down fractions Fχc1,2→J/ψ = σχc1,2→J/ψ / σJ/ψ, comparing to LHCb data.

Result: Rχc predictions are robust against rcBK initial condition choices; CGC+NRQCD matches LHCb pPb data well but overshoots pp data; feed-down fractions agree with data only when using experimental prompt J/ψ cross sections in denominator, not when using fully theoretical J/ψ predictions.

Conclusion: The CGC+NRQCD framework successfully describes forward χcJ production in pA collisions but faces a persistent challenge in simultaneously predicting χcJ and prompt J/ψ in pp collisions, indicating missing or inconsistent elements—e.g., in J/ψ production mechanisms, feed-down modeling, or universality of non-perturbative NRQCD LDMEs across collision systems.

Abstract: We study the forward $\chi_{cJ}$ production in the low $p_{\perp}$ region in proton-proton $(pp)$ and proton-nucleus $(pA)$ collisions at the LHC within the framework of non-relativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization and the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective field theory. Incorporating the small-$x$ gluonic evolution via the running-coupling Balitsky-Kovchegov equation with various initial conditions, we systematically analyze the cross section ratio $R_{\chi_{c}}=\sigma_{\chi_{c2}}/\sigma_{\chi_{c1}}$, and find that the theoretical predictions weakly depend on these inputs. Our predictions align well with the LHCb measurements for $p\mathrm{Pb}$ collisions, however, overshoot those for $pp$ collisions. We further investigate the feed down fractions $F_{\chi_{c1,2}\to J/\psi}=\sigma_{\chi_{c1,2}\to J/\psi}/\sigma_{J/\psi}$. We notice that with the experimental measurement of prompt $J/\psi$ cross section as inputs in the denominator the CGC+NRQCD predictions broadly match the data when choosing the standard McLerran-Venugopalan initial conditions. In contrast, implementing purely theoretical CGC+NRQCD predictions for both $J/\psi$ and $\chi_{c}$ production yield systematic discrepancies across all tested parameters. This highlights a persistent challenge in achieving a simultaneous, unified description of $\chi_{c}$ and prompt $J/\psi$ production within the current framework.

</details>


### [18] [Ultra-relativistic freeze-out of fermion dark matter through a heavy scalar mediator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24547)
*Jin-Lei Yang,Ke-Shuo Cai,Yin-Jie Zhang,Hai-Bin Zhang,Tai-Fu Feng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies Majorana fermion dark matter production via a heavy scalar mediator during reheating, identifying three regimes—freeze-in, WIMP-like freeze-out, and ultra-relativistic freeze-out (UFO)—and emphasizes the need to jointly model continued DM production and entropy dilution in UFO. Scalar cubic interactions, especially those coupling the heavy mediator to a light scalar, significantly alter regime boundaries and relic-density predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently explain the observed dark matter relic abundance across diverse thermal histories during reheating, particularly in the ultra-relativistic freeze-out (UFO) regime where standard equilibrium assumptions break down and entropy dilution competes with ongoing production.

Method: Analytical and numerical solution of coupled Boltzmann equations governing DM number density and radiation entropy, incorporating time-dependent Hubble expansion, temperature-dependent interaction rates, and entropy injection from inflaton decay; parameter scans over mediator mass, Yukawa and scalar cubic couplings, and reheating temperature.

Result: Three distinct DM production regimes—freeze-in, WIMP-like freeze-out, and UFO—are identified and mapped in parameter space; in UFO, relic density is highly sensitive to both continued post-decoupling production and entropy dilution; scalar cubic interactions shift regime boundaries, with the mediator–light-scalar coupling having stronger impact than heavy-scalar self-coupling.

Conclusion: Ultra-relativistic freeze-out is a viable and phenomenologically rich DM production mechanism during reheating, requiring joint treatment of non-equilibrium production and dynamical entropy dilution; scalar cubic interactions are essential for accurate relic density predictions and can substantially reshape viable parameter space.

Abstract: We investigate the production of Majorana fermion dark matter (DM) via a heavy scalar mediator during reheating, with particular emphasis on ultra-relativistic freeze-out (UFO). Depending on the mediator mass, interaction strengths, and reheating temperature, we identify three distinct production regimes: freeze-in, WIMP-like freeze-out, and UFO, all of which can account for the observed DM relic abundance. In the UFO regime, significant DM production can persist after chemical decoupling, while entropy injection during reheating simultaneously dilutes the DM yield. An accurate determination of the final relic abundance therefore requires a consistent treatment of both continued production and entropy dilution. We further examine the impact of scalar cubic interactions and find that they modify the boundaries between the different production regimes and reshape the relic-density contours. In particular, the results are more sensitive to the cubic coupling involving the mediator and the light scalar than to the heavy-scalar self-coupling.

</details>


### [19] [Tensor-polarized gluon distribution \texorpdfstring{$h_{1TT}^{g}$}{h1TTg} in the deuteron: QCD evolution and Drell-Yan phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24552)
*Xiupeng Xie,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the QCD evolution of tensor-polarized gluon distributions—specifically $h_{1TT}^g$ and $f_{1LL}^g$—in the deuteron using the spin-1 spectator model, leading-order autonomous evolution, and replica-based uncertainty quantification; it finds suppressed Drell-Yan cross sections and a steeper $Q^2$-dependence of asymmetry $A_{E_{xy}}$ compared to prior longitudinal-gluon benchmarks, with sensitivity to large-$x$ behavior at low $\sqrt{s}$ and high $Q^2$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the impact of tensor-polarized gluon distributions—particularly $h_{1TT}^g$—on proton-deuteron Drell-Yan observables, and to assess theoretical uncertainties arising from nonperturbative input and evolution, given limited experimental constraints on such transverse-spin-dependent gluonic structure.

Method: Uses the spin-1 spectator model for nonperturbative input of $h_{1TT}^g$, evolves it via the leading-order autonomous gluon evolution equation; propagates model-fit uncertainty using 100 replicas; computes tensor-polarized cross-section difference and asymmetry $A_{E_{xy}}$; additionally evolves $f_{1LL}^g$ in the coupled singlet-gluon system to evaluate its contribution to the unpolarized reference cross section.

Result: Evolved $h_{1TT}^g$ yields smaller Drell-Yan cross sections than earlier longitudinal-gluon benchmarks across most kinematics, and a significantly stronger decrease of $|A_{E_{xy}}|$ with $Q^2$; $A_{E_{xy}}$ varies weakly with $\sqrt{s}$ at high energy but is sensitive to large-$x$ behavior at low $\sqrt{s}$ and large $Q^2$; $f_{1LL}^g$ contributes moderately to the reference cross-section sum where the spectator model is reliable.

Conclusion: Tensor-polarized gluon distributions—especially $h_{1TT}^g$—have distinctive and quantifiable effects on Drell-Yan spin asymmetries, strongly dependent on $Q^2$ and large-$x$ modeling; precise extraction requires high-energy, high-$Q^2$ measurements and improved theoretical control over the large-$x$ region.

Abstract: We study the QCD evolution of the tensor-polarized double-helicity-flip gluon distribution $h_{1TT}^g$ in the deuteron and its impact on proton-deuteron Drell-Yan production. The nonperturbative input is taken from the spin-1 spectator model and evolved at leading order with the autonomous gluon evolution equation. The uncertainty of the model fit is propagated to the tensor-polarized cross-section difference and the asymmetry $A_{E_{xy}}$ using an ensemble of 100 replicas. Compared with the earlier benchmark estimate based on a longitudinally polarized gluon distribution, the evolved spectator-model input gives smaller cross sections in most of the kinematic region and a much stronger decrease of $|A_{E_{xy}}|$ with $Q^2$. At sufficiently large center-of-mass energy, the asymmetry varies only slowly with $\sqrt{s}$, whereas the low-$\sqrt{s}$ and large-$Q^2$ regions probe the poorly constrained large-$x$ tail of the model. We also evolve the tensor-polarized gluon distribution $f_{1LL}^g$ in the coupled singlet-gluon system and assess its contribution to the reference cross-section sum. Its effect remains moderate in the kinematic region where the spectator-model prediction is under reasonable control.

</details>


### [20] [Analytic results for the two-loop renormalization constants of Feynman gauge QED](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26750)
*Gregory S. Adkins,Elias Mitchell,A. Volkan Turan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Computes two-loop renormalization constants $Z_m$ and $Z_1=Z_2$ for QED in Feynman gauge and on-shell scheme using dimensional regularization, providing analytic results and high-order $\epsilon$-expansion coefficients up to $\epsilon^5$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain high-precision analytic renormalization constants for QED at two loops, enabling future multiloop QED calculations and serving as cross-checks for analogous QCD computations.

Method: Dimensional regularization in the on-shell renormalization scheme; analytic computation of $Z_m$ and $Z_1 = Z_2$ at two loops in Feynman-gauge QED.

Result: Full analytic expressions for $Z_m$ and $Z_1 = Z_2$, plus their Laurent-series expansion coefficients in $\epsilon$ from $1/\epsilon^2$ through $\epsilon^5$.

Conclusion: The derived analytic and high-order $\epsilon$-expanded renormalization constants constitute a benchmark for multiloop QED and QCD calculations.

Abstract: The two-loop renormalization constants $Z_m$ and $Z_1\!=\!Z_2$ of Feynman gauge quantum electrodynamics (QED) in the on-shell scheme are computed using dimensional regularization. Analytic results are obtained for these constants. In addition, analytic expressions are obtained for their expansion coefficients in the dimensional regularization parameter $\epsilon$ from order $1/\epsilon^2$ through order $\epsilon^5$. The high-order expansion coefficients will be needed for future multiloop calculations in QED and will provide a useful check for multiloop calculations in QCD.

</details>


### [21] [Probing dark-matter scenarios of Belle II excess in $B^+\to K^+M_X$ with $K^+\to π^+ M_X$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24701)
*Alexander Berezhnoy,Wolfgang Lucha,Dmitri Melikhov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how the Belle II excess in B→KM_X decays, interpreted as top-philic dark matter, constrains the mediator R and informs predictions for K→πM_X decays, which are highly sensitive to the dark-matter fermion mass m_χ — potentially enabling its measurement or setting a lower bound via NA62 data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To leverage the Belle II B→KM_X excess — interpreted as top-philic dark matter production — to predict and constrain the related kaon decay K→πM_X, which offers complementary and stronger sensitivity to the dark-matter fermion mass m_χ.

Method: Analyzes loop-induced flavor-changing neutral current decays B→KM_X and K→πM_X within a model where a top-quark-coupled scalar/pseudoscalar mediator R decays to dark-matter fermion pairs χ̄χ; uses Belle II constraints on R parameters to predict K→πM_X rates and assess sensitivity to m_χ.

Result: B→KM_X data tightly constrain the mediator R’s couplings and mass but poorly constrain m_χ; in contrast, K→πM_X decay rate exhibits strong, monotonic dependence on m_χ, making it a powerful probe — with improved theory/experiment, NA62 measurements could determine m_χ or set a robust lower limit.

Conclusion: K→πM_X decays provide a uniquely sensitive channel to pin down or bound the dark-matter fermion mass m_χ in top-philic models motivated by the Belle II excess, complementing and extending B→KM_X constraints.

Abstract: The hypothesis of top-philic dark matter as the origin of the Belle II excess events in $B\to K M_X$ decays has direct impact on $K\to \pi M_X$ decays: Both of these decays proceed via loop diagrams~with a top quark emitting some mediator field $R$ and the subsequent decay of $R$ into a pair of dark-matter fermions $\bar\chi\chi$. The Belle II results on $B\to K M_X$ allowed us to extract the parameters of the mediator $R$ with reasonable accuracy, whereas the sensitivity to the fermion mass $m_\chi$ proved to be rather low. In contrast to $B\to K M_X$ decays, the $K\to \pi M_X$ decays exhibit a strong sensitivity to $m_\chi$; therefore, if - upon reducing theoretical and experimental uncertainties - the tension between standard-model predictions for the $K\to\pi M_X$ decays and the most recent NA62 measurements becomes pronounced the latter decays may either enable the extraction of the value of $m_\chi$ or provide a lower limit on~$m_\chi$.

</details>


### [22] [Dynamical resonances $Λ(1405)$, $Λ(1670)$, and $f_0(980)$ in the process $Λ_c^+ \to p K^+ K^-$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24939)
*Sheng-Chao Zhang,Gui-Ye Guo,Guan-Ying Wang,Li-Sheng Geng,En Wang,Ju-Jun Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Λc+ → p K+ K− decay using chiral unitary approaches, incorporating dynamically generated resonances (Λ(1405), Λ(1670), f0(980)/a0(980), and φ(1020)), and successfully reproduces BESIII data—revealing threshold enhancement (Λ(1405)) and cusp (Λ(1670)) in pK−, plus clear f0(980) and φ(1020) signals in K+K−.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: New BESIII measurements of the singly Cabibbo-suppressed decay Λc+ → p K+ K− motivate a theoretical investigation into the role of dynamically generated hadronic resonances in this channel.

Method: Chiral unitary approach: includes S-wave pseudoscalar meson–octet baryon interactions generating Λ(1405) and Λ(1670); S-wave pseudoscalar meson–pseudoscalar meson interactions generating f0(980)/a0(980); and explicit φ(1020) contribution. Invariant mass distributions are computed and compared to BESIII data.

Result: Theoretical calculations reproduce both pK− and K+K− invariant mass spectra from BESIII. A near-threshold enhancement in pK− is attributed to Λ(1405); a cusp at ~1670 MeV to Λ(1670); distinct peaks from f0(980) and φ(1020) appear in K+K− spectrum.

Conclusion: The Λc+ → p K+ K− decay serves as a sensitive probe of dynamically generated Λ resonances and light scalar/vector mesons; future high-statistics experiments (BESIII, Belle II, STCF) will further constrain their pole positions, couplings, and internal structure.

Abstract: Motivated by new BESIII measurements of the singly Cabibbo-suppressed process $\Lambda_c^+ \to p K^+ K^- $, we investigate the $\Lambda_c^+ \to p K^+ K^-$ reaction by taking into account contributions from the low-lying excited baryons $\Lambda(1405)$ and $\Lambda(1670)$, which are dynamically generated via the $S$-wave pseudoscalar meson-octet baryon interaction. In addition, contributions from the light scalar mesons $f_0(980)/a_0(980)$ dynamically generated via the $S$-wave pseudoscalar meson-pseudoscalar meson interaction, as well as the vector meson $\phi(1020)$, are also considered. The theoretical calculations could reproduce the invariant mass distributions of $K^- p$ and $K^+K^-$ measured by the BESIII Collaboration well. Specifically, a near-threshold enhancement and a cusp structure around 1670~MeV in the $pK^-$ invariant mass spectrum are obtained, which are associated with the $\Lambda(1405)$ and $\Lambda(1670)$ resonances, respectively. Furthermore, contributions from the scalar meson $f_0(980)$ and vector meson $\phi(1020)$ are clearly observed in the $K^+ K^-$ invariant mass distribution. Future high-precision measurements by the BESIII, Belle II, and the proposed Super Tau-Charm Facility experiments will substantially deepen our understanding of the intrinsic properties of the low-lying excited $\Lambda$ states and the $f_0(980)/a_0(980)$ meson.

</details>


### [23] [$U$-spin sum rules in charmed baryon decays beyond the $U$-spin limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24975)
*Si-Jia Wen,Di Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives U-spin sum rules for charmed baryon decays that remain valid up to first-order U-spin breaking, enabling quantitative tests of flavor symmetry beyond the exact symmetry limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: U-spin symmetry is useful in charmed hadron weak decays, but its predictive power is limited by sizable U-spin breaking; the work aims to extend its applicability by incorporating controlled symmetry-breaking effects.

Method: Derivation of rate and decay parameter U-spin sum rules valid up to first-order U-spin breaking in the charmed baryon weak decay sector.

Result: Dozens of U-spin sum rules for decay rates and parameters that hold under first-order U-spin breaking, providing testable predictions for flavor symmetry in charmed baryon decays.

Conclusion: The derived sum rules enhance the utility of U-spin symmetry as a phenomenological tool by accommodating leading-order breaking, thus improving constraints on hadronic matrix elements and potential new physics contributions.

Abstract: $U$-spin symmetry is an important flavor symmetry in studying charmed hadron weak decays. However, the sizeable $U$-spin breaking limits the predictive power of $U$-spin symmetry. In this work, we derive dozens of rate and decay parameter $U$-spin sum rules for charmed baryon decays beyond the exact $U$-spin symmetry limit. These sum rules provide tests of flavor symmetry up to the first-order $U$-spin breaking in the charmed baryon sector.

</details>


### [24] [Solar Neutrino Signals and Capture-Annihilation Equilibrium of SU(5) Neutralino Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25077)
*M. Adeel Ajaib,Abdul Rehman*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies neutralino dark matter in supersymmetric SU(5) with nonuniversal gaugino masses, linking relic abundance (micrOMEGAs) and solar neutrino/muon signals (DarkSUSY) to IceCube solar search limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by IceCube solar searches, the study aims to understand neutralino dark matter signatures in the Sun within a supersymmetric SU(5) framework featuring nonuniversal gaugino masses.

Method: Combines micrOMEGAs for relic abundance calculations and DarkSUSY for solar neutrino and muon signal predictions; analyzes how local abundance, neutralino composition, and capture-annihilation equilibrium affect signals beyond simple dark matter fraction scaling.

Result: Identifies key dependencies of solar neutrino/muon fluxes on neutralino properties; provides integrated fluxes and benchmark points tied to IceCube reference limits.

Conclusion: Establishes a consistent framework for interpreting IceCube solar searches and evaluating signals from subdominant thermal neutralino dark matter components.

Abstract: Motivated by IceCube solar searches, we investigate neutralino dark matter in supersymmetric SU(5) with nonuniversal gaugino masses. Combining micrOMEGAs relic abundances with DarkSUSY calculations, we develop a consistent picture of solar neutrino and muon signals from thermal neutralino subcomponents. We identify how local abundance, neutralino composition, and capture-annihilation equilibrium shape these signals beyond simple scaling with the dark matter fraction. Integrated fluxes and representative benchmarks connect these predictions to published IceCube reference limits. Our results provide a framework for interpreting solar searches and assessing the signals of subdominant neutralino dark matter.

</details>


### [25] [Summary of Aspen discussions on perturbative uncertainties beyond 7-point variations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25091)
*Enzo Canonero,Glen Cowan,Stefano Forte,Joey Huston,Sebastian Jaskiewicz,Manfred Kraus,Christian Preuss,Daniel Reichelt,Peter Skands,Robert Szafron,Yannick Ulrich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes enhanced uncertainty estimation for LHC scattering amplitudes by augmenting standard scale variations with targeted missing higher-order term estimates and introducing a frequentist treatment of non-Gaussian theory uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve robustness and comprehensiveness of uncertainty estimates in perturbative QCD predictions for LHC observables, addressing limitations of conventional renormalisation- and factorisation-scale variations.

Method: Combines established higher-order amplitude and resummation techniques with novel prescriptions: (i) supplementary estimates of known missing higher-order contributions beyond standard 7-point scale variations; (ii) adaptation of a recent proposal for incorporating non-Gaussian theory uncertainties within a frequentist statistical framework.

Result: A refined methodology for estimating theoretical uncertainties in collider phenomenology, enabling more reliable interpretation of experimental data and improved precision in Standard Model and BSM analyses.

Conclusion: Standard scale-variation uncertainty estimates are insufficient alone; incorporating physics-motivated missing higher-order terms and rigorously treating non-Gaussian uncertainties yields more realistic and actionable theory uncertainties for LHC event simulations and measurements.

Abstract: This writeup elaborates on constructive discussions which took place at the Aspen Center for Physics, Aug - Sep 2024, during the program "Tightening the gap between scattering amplitudes and events at the LHC at higher orders". Building on already existing theoretical understanding from the study of scattering amplitudes and resummations at higher orders, we suggest to complement standard 7-point renormalisation- and factorisation-scale variations by additional estimates of missing higher-order terms which are known to be present in the perturbative series. We also summarise a recent proposal for at least one way that non-Gaussian theory uncertainties might be treated within a frequentist framework. This may help to provide more robust and comprehensive uncertainty estimates for experimental and phenomenological applications of perturbative calculations.

</details>


### [26] [alpha_s from hadron multiplicities: an updated analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25135)
*A.V. Kotikov,B.G. Shaikhatdenov,N.D. Godlevsky,I.A. Kotikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the first Mellin moments of quark and gluon fragmentation functions using NNLL-resummed anomalous dimensions, fits them to e+e− data on charged-hadron multiplicities in jets, and extracts α_s^(5)(M_Z) = 0.1177±0.0026.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision in extracting the strong coupling constant α_s from jet fragmentation observables by incorporating higher-order resummation effects in the evolution of fragmentation functions.

Method: Resummation of anomalous dimensions to next-to-next-to-leading-logarithmic (NNLL) accuracy; extraction of first Mellin moments D_q,g(μ²) as average hadron multiplicities; global fit to world e⁺e⁻ annihilation data on charged-hadron jet multiplicities.

Result: Good agreement with experimental data; extracted value α_s^(5)(M_Z) = 0.1177^{+0.0027}_{−0.0025}.

Conclusion: NNLL-resummed evolution significantly enhances the theoretical description of jet fragmentation multiplicities, enabling a competitive and robust determination of α_s(M_Z) from fragmentation function moments.

Abstract: Exploring the anomalous dimensions, resummed through next-to-next-to-leading-logarithmic order, we study the first Mellin moments D_{q,g}(\mu^2) of the quark and gluon fragmentation functions, which correspond to the average hadron multiplicities in jets initiated by quarks and gluons, respectively. By fitting the world data on D_{q,g}(\mu^2) for charged hadrons measured in e^+e^- annihilation, we find a good agreement with the data and obtain the following value for the strong force coupling \alpha_s^{(5)}(M_Z)=0.1177+0.0027-0.0025.

</details>


### [27] [Probing the Energy Evolution and Hadronic Cascade Time Dependence of Forward-Backward Multiplicity Correlations in pp Collisions at $\sqrt{s} = 0.9$--$13.6$~TeV with AMPT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25154)
*Dibakar Dhar,Tumpa Biswas,Prabir Kumar Haldar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies forward-backward multiplicity correlations in pp collisions across multiple energies using the AMPT model (DF and SM configurations), comparing with ALICE data; it finds correlation strength decreases with pseudorapidity gap, increases nonlinearly with window width and collision energy, differs between DF/SM configurations, and is only mildly reduced by longer hadronic cascade times—suggesting FB correlations are predominantly set at the partonic stage.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin and evolution of forward-backward (FB) multiplicity correlations in proton–proton collisions, specifically whether they arise primarily from initial partonic dynamics or are significantly modified by later hadronic rescattering.

Method: Simulated pp collisions using the AMPT model in default (DF) and string melting (SM) modes at √s = 0.9–13.6 TeV; computed FB correlation coefficient b_corr for varying pseudorapidity gaps, azimuthal windows, pT intervals, and hadronic cascade times (τ_HC = 0.6–30 fm/c); validated against ALICE pseudorapidity densities and charged-pion pT spectra.

Result: b_corr decreases gradually with pseudorapidity gap (with residual pedestal), rises nonlinearly with window width, and increases strongly with √s; DF yields larger b_corr than SM; SM better describes ALICE data at 0.9 and 2.76 TeV, DF at 7 TeV; largest deviations occur at low pT; increasing τ_HC monotonically reduces b_corr—modestly but significantly—across all energies and windows.

Conclusion: Forward-backward correlations are predominantly established during the early partonic stage, with only mild suppression due to hadronic rescattering, indicating limited sensitivity to late-stage hadronic dynamics.

Abstract: Forward-backward (FB) multiplicity correlations are studied in proton--proton collisions at centre-of-mass energies of $\sqrt{s} = 0.9$, $2.76$, $5.02$, $7$, $13$, and $13.6$~TeV using the AMPT model in its default (DF) and string melting (SM) configurations. The coefficient $b_{\rm corr}$ is obtained for windows separated in pseudorapidity and azimuth, and its variation with the pseudorapidity gap, window width, collision energy, transverse momentum interval, and hadronic cascade time $\tau_{\rm HC}$ is investigated. The simulated results are compared with the available ALICE measurements, and the model is validated against experimental pseudorapidity densities and charged-pion transverse momentum spectra. The correlation strength is found to decrease gradually with increasing pseudorapidity gap while retaining a substantial pedestal, to rise non-linearly with the window width, and to increase substantially with collision energy. The DF configuration yields systematically larger values of $b_{\rm corr}$ than the SM configuration, while the SM configuration provides the better description of the ALICE data at $0.9$ and $2.76$~TeV and the DF configuration at $7$~TeV. In the lowest transverse momentum intervals the deviations from the data are largest for both configurations. The hadronic cascade time is varied within the SM configuration over $\tau_{\rm HC} = 0.6$, $5$, $15$, and $30$~fm/$c$. A monotonic reduction of $b_{\rm corr}$ with increasing $\tau_{\rm HC}$ is observed at all energies and for all window widths, with the ordering of the curves preserved throughout. The effect is modest but statistically significant. These results indicate that the FB correlation strength is primarily imprinted at the partonic stage and only mildly diluted by the subsequent hadronic evolution.

</details>


### [28] [Quantum Veto Parton Shower Algorithm in the Conformal Window](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25189)
*Nongqiu Cai,Xu Deng,Jinmian Li,Bingwei Long,Shuo Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum veto parton shower algorithm for a confining dark sector with SU(3)_D × U(1)_D gauge symmetry, featuring infrared fixed-point dynamics, flavor mixing, and coherent evolution across interaction and mass bases; it shows enhanced zero-emission probability, a U-shaped mass-eigenstate spectrum, and power-law-like energy-energy correlations—demonstrating quantum interference beyond classical Markovian showers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model multi-flavor quantum interference effects in strongly interacting, confining dark sectors—beyond the reach of classical Markovian parton showers—by leveraging quantum coherence across flavor-mixed bases and non-perturbative IR dynamics.

Method: Develops a quantum veto parton shower algorithm where shower evolution occurs coherently in the interaction basis (with unitary flavor mixing), while state preparation and measurement are performed in the mass basis; incorporates two-loop running of the non-Abelian coupling toward an IR fixed point and a finite dark-quark mass threshold for decoupling.

Result: Quantum simulation yields (i) significantly enhanced zero-emission rate vs. classical shower, (ii) characteristic U-shaped distribution in mass-eigenstate population, and (iii) energy-energy correlators showing approximate linearity in double-logarithmic coordinates—indicative of power-law scaling over intermediate angles.

Conclusion: The framework establishes that quantum algorithms can faithfully capture flavor-coherent interference in strongly coupled, multi-flavor hidden sectors—offering a path toward quantum simulation of non-perturbative QCD-like dynamics inaccessible to classical stochastic methods.

Abstract: We develop a quantum veto parton shower algorithm for a confining dark sector with gauge symmetry $\mathrm{SU}(3)_D \times \mathrm{U}(1)_D$ operating in the conformal window ($N_C = 3$, $N_F = 12$). The non-Abelian gauge coupling exhibits two-loop running toward an infrared fixed point before decoupling at a finite dark-quark mass threshold, while the dark quarks carry non-degenerate $\mathrm{U}(1)_D$ charges with unitary flavor mixing between the interaction and mass bases. Shower evolution proceeds coherently in the interaction basis, with states prepared and measured in the mass basis. Compared with the classical Markovian shower, the flavor-coherent quantum simulation produces a significantly enhanced zero-emission rate, and a characteristic U-shaped mass-eigenstate spectrum.
