Furthermore, energy-energy correlators exhibit approximate linearity in the double-logarithmic coordinates over an intermediate angular range, suggesting power-law scaling in that region. This framework demonstrates the capability of quantum algorithms to capture multi-flavor quantum interference in strongly interacting hidden sectors.

</details>


### [29] [Lorentz-Invariance Violation with neutrinos from gamma-ray bursts using IceCube High-Energy Starting Events 12-year dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25214)
*Mathieu Lamoureux,José Manuel Carmona,José Luis Cortés,Ardit Gkioni,Antoine Kouchner,Maykoll A. Reyes*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper searches for Lorentz-invariance violation (LIV) using 12-year IceCube neutrino data and 746 gamma-ray bursts, finding mild evidence (1.9σ) for linear LIV with scale Λ_LIV ≈ 5.8×10¹⁷ GeV—dominated by subluminal effects; no evidence for quadratic LIV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-energy astrophysical neutrinos are sensitive probes of Lorentz-invariance violation due to energy-dependent speed deviations that accumulate over cosmological distances; gamma-ray bursts provide well-timed, redshift-calibrated counterparts for time-of-flight tests.

Method: A likelihood analysis is performed on IceCube’s 12-year High-Energy Starting Event dataset and a catalog of 746 GRBs with measured redshifts, jointly modeling neutrino directional/energy uncertainties, intrinsic emission time offsets, and LIV-induced neutrino decay (per effective field theory). Both linear and quadratic LIV models are tested.

Result: For linear LIV: p = 3.1% (1.9σ), best-fit Λ_LIV = 5.8×10¹⁷ GeV; signal is subluminal-dominated due to strong suppression of superluminal neutrinos via LIV-induced decay at high energies. No statistically significant evidence for quadratic LIV. Results align with prior studies using different methods.

Conclusion: Mild but intriguing evidence for linear LIV supports continued investigation with larger or lower-energy neutrino datasets—especially where superluminal components may be detectable—while reinforcing the role of multi-messenger timing as a probe of quantum gravity effects.

Abstract: High-energy astrophysical neutrinos provide a unique probe of Lorentz-invariance violation (LIV), as small energy-dependent deviations from the speed of light can produce measurable time delays over cosmological distances. We search for LIV-induced correlations between neutrinos and gamma-ray bursts using the public 12-year IceCube High-Energy Starting Event dataset and a catalog of 746 bursts with measured redshifts. We develop a likelihood analysis accounting for neutrino directional and energy uncertainties, a possible intrinsic time offset between neutrino and gamma-ray emission, and the decay of superluminal neutrinos expected within effective field theory. For linear LIV, the data show a mild preference over the background-only hypothesis, with a $p$-value of $3.1\%$, corresponding to a one-sided Gaussian significance of approximately $1.9\sigma$. The best fit corresponds to a LIV scale $\Lambda_{\rm LIV}=5.8\times10^{17}\,\mathrm{GeV}$ and is effectively driven by the subluminal component, as superluminal neutrinos are strongly suppressed by LIV-induced decays at the analyzed energies. The preferred LIV scale and the most significant neutrino--gamma-ray burst associations are consistent with previous studies based on different statistical approaches. No evidence for quadratic LIV is found. These results motivate further searches with complementary neutrino datasets, particularly at lower energies, where the superluminal component may become observable.

</details>


### [30] [More than a Lifetime: Towards Precision Predictions for $B$-Meson Decays in the Standard Model and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25311)
*Ali Mohamed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This thesis advances theoretical understanding of B-meson lifetimes using HQE and LCSR, computes new $1/m_b^4$ two-quark and $1/m_b^3$ BSM Wilson coefficients, includes missing QCD-penguin contributions, and explores B-mesogenesis linking baryogenesis, dark matter, and exotic B decays—constraining models with BaBar/Belle/Belle II data and exposing HQE breakdown regions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To use B-meson lifetimes as precision probes of SM dynamics and BSM physics—including baryogenesis and dark matter—via systematic HQE and LCSR calculations beyond current state-of-the-art.

Method: Heavy Quark Expansion (HQE) at leading QCD order for dimension-seven two-quark operators ($1/m_b^4$); model-independent BSM extension of Darwin term to $1/m_b^3$; computation of dimension-six four-quark weak annihilation; inclusion of SM QCD-penguin induced dimension-five/six terms; light-cone sum rules for exclusive $B^+\to p\psi$; comparison with inclusive decay rates and lifetime ratios against experimental data.

Result: Analytical Wilson coefficients for $1/m_b^4$ corrections to $b\to c\bar{u}d$ and $b\to c\bar{c}s$; complete $1/m_b^3$ BSM Wilson coefficients for general non-leptonic Hamiltonian; first determination of corresponding dimension-six weak annihilation contributions; identification of previously missing SM QCD-penguin contributions; constraints on B-mesogenesis parameter space from BaBar, Belle, and Belle II; discovery of kinematic regions where power-suppressed HQE terms dominate over leading ones.

Conclusion: The work significantly extends HQE-based lifetime predictions, enables sharper SM tests and BSM sensitivity in flavour observables, establishes concrete links between heavy-quark decays and cosmological asymmetries/dark matter, and reveals intrinsic limitations of HQE validity—thereby guiding future theoretical and experimental efforts in precision flavour physics.

Abstract: This thesis investigates B-meson lifetimes as probes of Standard Model (SM) dynamics and physics beyond the SM (BSM), employing the Heavy Quark Expansion (HQE) and light-cone sum rules (LCSR). Within the HQE, we calculate at leading order in QCD the complete dimension-seven two-quark contributions to the CKM-favoured non-leptonic transitions $b\to c\bar{u}d$ and $b\to c\bar{c}s$, providing analytical Wilson coefficients for the $1/m_b^4$ corrections. We then extend the dimension-six Darwin contribution to a model-independent BSM framework, deriving the Wilson coefficients through order $1/m_b^3$ for a general non-leptonic effective Hamiltonian. We also determine the corresponding dimension-six four-quark weak annihilation contributions. As a special case, our results provide previously missing dimension-five and dimension-six contributions induced by SM QCD-penguin operators. Finally, we investigate B-mesogenesis, which connects the cosmic matter-antimatter asymmetry and dark matter to exotic B-meson decays. We study inclusive decay rates and lifetime ratios within the HQE and exclusive $B^+\to p\psi$ decays using LCSR. Comparisons with experimental constraints from BaBar, Belle and Belle II restrict the allowed parameter space. We also identify kinematic regions where power-suppressed contributions exceed the leading terms, highlighting limitations of the HQE. Together, these studies advance the theoretical understanding of heavy-hadron lifetimes, strengthen the bridge between precision flavour physics and physics beyond the SM, and open new avenues for probing the interplay between heavy-quark dynamics and the dark sector.

</details>


### [31] [Real-time quantum simulation of hadron production in deep inelastic scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25350)
*Xianghui Cao,Meijian Li,Yang Li,Wenyang Qian*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum simulation framework for meson production in deep inelastic scattering using light-front QCD Hamiltonian formalism, validated against classical and analytical eikonal results, and demonstrates feasibility on quantum emulators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a first-principles, amplitude-level computational pathway for exclusive hadron production—particularly J/ψ—in deep inelastic scattering (DIS), directly supporting the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) physics program.

Method: Uses light-front QCD Hamiltonian formalism; evolves a quark-antiquark dipole (as single-momentum state or virtual photon component) in real time through nuclear color fields via Trotterized unitary evolution; computes observables (transverse momentum broadening, total/elastic cross sections, J/ψ production rate) by projecting onto the J/ψ light-front wavefunction; evaluates nuclear modification factor R_A vs. Q²; benchmarks with classical simulations and analytical eikonal predictions; implements small-scale quantum emulation.

Result: Classical simulations agree well with analytical eikonal predictions across wide parameter ranges; quantum emulation of single-momentum dipole evolution reproduces classical benchmark results; R_A is quantified as function of Q² for proton and gold targets; transverse broadening and J/ψ production rates are extracted consistently.

Conclusion: The framework provides a viable, ab initio quantum simulation approach for exclusive meson production in DIS, bridging non-perturbative QCD dynamics and near-term quantum hardware, and lays groundwork for future EIC-related quantum computations.

Abstract: We develop a quantum simulation framework for meson production in deep inelastic scattering based on light-front QCD Hamiltonian formalism. A quark-antiquark dipole, prepared either as a single-momentum state or as the $q\bar q$ component of a virtual photon, is evolved in real time through the color field of a nuclear target via Trotterized unitary evolution. We consider both proton and gold nuclear targets, each characterized by their respective saturation scale. From the evolved state we extract the transverse momentum broadening, the total and elastic cross sections, and the $J/\psi$ production rate by projecting onto the $J/\psi$ light-front wavefunction. We then extract the nuclear modification factor $R_A$ at different photon virtuality $Q^2$ and analyze nuclear modification effect. Results of classical simulations show good agreement with analytical eikonal predictions across a broad range of parameters. For the single-momentum dipole, quantum simulation is demonstrated on a quantum circuit emulator with a smaller lattice size, and results are consistent with the classical benchmark. This work establishes a first-principles, amplitude-level pathway toward computing exclusive hadron production in deep inelastic scattering, directly relevant to the physics program of the Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [32] [Exothermic Dark Matter with a Dark Higgs: The LZ 248 keV Event and the Galactic Center Excess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25423)
*Jinheung Kim,Jongkuk Kim,Pyungwon Ko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an exothermic dark matter model within a dark U(1) framework—featuring a dark Higgs and dark photon—to explain a single 248 keV nuclear-recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ); it identifies two viable dark photon mass branches (~1 GeV and ~26 GeV) for kinetic mixing ε = 10⁻⁴, excludes the lighter branch via CMB constraints, and finds the heavier branch consistent with the Galactic Center γ-ray excess.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keV nuclear-recoil event reported by LZ using exothermic dark matter, which deposits more energy than its incident kinetic energy due to internal inelastic transitions, within a theoretically consistent dark sector model.

Method: Constructs a dark U(1) model where a dark Higgs generates both dark photon mass and a mass splitting between dark matter ground and excited states; computes the thermally averaged excited-state fraction; confronts predictions with INTEGRAL/SPI bounds on excited-state decays and Planck CMB constraints on energy injection; compares surviving parameter space with the Galactic Center γ-ray excess morphology and spectrum.

Result: Two dark photon mass solutions emerge near 1 GeV and 26 GeV for ε = 10⁻⁴; the 1 GeV branch is excluded at its likelihood minimum by CMB limits; the 26 GeV branch survives all constraints and matches the southern-sky Galactic Center γ-ray excess when the dark Higgs mass is near the dark photon mass.

Conclusion: The LZ event can be consistently explained by exothermic dark matter in a minimal dark U(1) model; only the heavier (~26 GeV) dark photon branch remains viable after multi-messenger astrophysical constraints, and it intriguingly aligns with independent γ-ray observations—suggesting a testable, unified explanation for both direct detection and indirect signals.

Abstract: LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) reported a $248~\mathrm{keV}$ nuclear-recoil event. Exothermic dark matter can explain it in a dark $U(1)$ model whose dark Higgs generates the dark photon mass and the mass splitting. A computed excited-state fraction gives two branches with dark photon masses near $1$ and $26~\mathrm{GeV}$ for kinetic mixing $\epsilon=10^{-4}$. INTEGRAL/SPI limits on excited-state decays bound the LZ rate, and cosmic microwave background limits exclude the lighter branch at its likelihood minimum. For a dark Higgs boson near the dark photon mass, the heavier branch agrees with the southern-sky Galactic Center $\gamma$-ray excess.

</details>


### [33] [Parton Fragmentation Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25607)
*Hongxi Xing*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This chapter offers a pedagogical and comprehensive review of parton fragmentation functions (FFs) in QCD—non-perturbative objects describing hadronization—covering their theoretical definition, experimental constraints from e⁺e⁻, SIDIS, and pp data, global analysis methods, and advanced extensions including dihadron, spin- and TMD-dependent FFs, and nuclear medium effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a pedagogical and unified introduction to parton fragmentation functions—crucial yet non-perturbative components of QCD—and to bridge theoretical foundations with modern experimental and analytical techniques for constraining them.

Method: Review-based exposition integrating theoretical formalism (gauge-invariant operator definitions), phenomenological analysis (global QCD fits to e⁺e⁻, SIDIS, and pp data), and discussion of advanced frameworks (dihadron FFs, spin- and transverse-momentum-dependent FFs, nuclear modification).

Result: A coherent, self-contained overview of FFs: their universality, scale evolution, process dependence, extraction methodologies, and extensions into multidimensional and nuclear contexts—enabling deeper insight into hadronization dynamics.

Conclusion: Fragmentation functions are indispensable universal tools in QCD; their robust determination via global analyses, combined with advances in spin-, transverse-momentum-, and nuclear-dependent formulations, significantly enhances our understanding of non-perturbative hadronization physics.

Abstract: This chapter provides a pedagogical and comprehensive introduction to parton fragmentation functions (FFs), the essential non-perturbative quantities in Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) that describe the universal process of hadronization, the transition of quarks and gluons into observable hadrons. Formally defined as gauge-invariant matrix elements, the determination of FFs is achieved through global QCD analyses that synthesize world data from three primary processes: electron-positron annihilation, semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering, and proton-proton collisions. This chapter covers the theoretical foundations of FFs, including their operator definitions and properties, the experimental probes used to constrain them, and the methodologies of modern global analyses. The discussion is extended to more advanced topics, including dihadron FFs, the three-dimensional picture of hadronization provided by spin- and transverse-momentum-dependent FFs, and the modification of fragmentation in a nuclear medium, which is key to probe their fundamental properties.

</details>


### [34] [Coherent and Dissociative Photon-Induced Production in Herwig 7](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25855)
*L. Grazette,K. Lohwasser,M.R. Masouminia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a particle-level study of photon-induced production in Herwig 7, comparing coherent and dissociative proton configurations using dedicated Rivet analysis; it finds that rapidity-gap and charged-particle activity observables are most sensitive to dissociative effects, providing a baseline for LHC semi-exclusive measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a reliable simulation baseline for semi-exclusive photon-induced processes at the LHC, particularly distinguishing coherent from dissociative proton configurations, which are critical for precision electroweak and QCD studies.

Method: Uses Herwig 7 with Budnev elastic photon flux + inelastic photon PDF; employs internal matrix elements for γγ → ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ and γγ → W⁺W⁻ → ℓ⁺ℓ⁻νν̄; applies dedicated Rivet analysis to compare hard-system kinematics, charged-particle multiplicity, rapidity gaps, and forward remnant distributions across coherent, single-dissociative, and double-dissociative configurations.

Result: Shower-setting variations have only modest impact on hard-system kinematics in well-populated regions; coherent and dissociative events are best distinguished by charged-particle activity and rapidity-gap observables—not by hard-system variables alone.

Conclusion: Rapidity-gap and underlying-event-sensitive observables are key discriminators of proton dissociation in photon-induced processes; this work provides a validated generator-level benchmark for future experimental analyses and generator tuning for LHC semi-exclusive measurements.

Abstract: We present a particle-level study of photon-induced production in Herwig 7, focusing on coherent, single-dissociative and double-dissociative proton configurations. The simulation combines the Budnev elastic photon flux with an inelastic photon PDF and uses internal Herwig 7 matrix elements for $\gamma\gamma\to \ell^+\ell^-$ and $\gamma\gamma\to W^+W^-\to \ell^+\ell^-\nu\bar{\nu}$ as the central setup. Using a dedicated Rivet analysis, we compare hard-system kinematics, charged-particle activity, rapidity-gap observables and forward-remnant-sensitive distributions. We find that shower-setting changes generally have modest effects on hard-system distributions in well-populated regions, while coherent and dissociative samples differ most clearly in additional charged activity and rapidity-gap observables. These results provide a useful baseline for modelling semi-exclusive photon-induced measurements at the LHC and identify the observables most sensitive to dissociative activity in future generator comparisons.

</details>


### [35] [Thermodynamics of two-flavor isospin-asymmetric superfluid quark matter at next-to-leading order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25882)
*Tim Rehberg-Juditzky,Andreas Geißel,Jens Braun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes next-to-leading-order thermodynamic properties of superfluid quark matter at high isospin asymmetry, zero baryon density, and zero temperature, accounting for non-perturbative pion condensate-induced gap effects; it finds gap corrections increase pressure and speed of sound, with a characteristic slope change in the latter as isospin asymmetry decreases, consistent with lattice QCD and Dyson-Schwinger results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically incorporate non-perturbative gap effects from charged-pion condensation in high-isospin-asymmetry quark matter—where conventional weak-coupling perturbation theory fails due to the gap's exponential dependence on the strong coupling—and to extend precision calculations to next-to-leading order (NLO) analogous to color-superconducting quark matter.

Method: Perturbative QCD at next-to-leading order (NLO) in the strong coupling constant α_s, incorporating a finite, small pion-condensate-induced gap Δ ≪ μ_I (isospin chemical potential), within two-flavor massless quark limit; thermodynamic quantities (pressure, speed of sound) derived from the grand potential including gap-dependent self-energy corrections.

Result: Gap-induced NLO corrections increase both pressure and speed of sound relative to the gapless (non-superfluid) case; the speed of sound exhibits a systematic change in slope as isospin asymmetry decreases from asymptotically large values; quantitative agreement is found with lattice QCD and Dyson-Schwinger equation studies in overlapping regimes.

Conclusion: Superfluid quark matter at high isospin asymmetry exhibits robust, quantitatively significant gap-driven thermodynamic corrections already at NLO; its equation of state is qualitatively distinct from the gapless phase, with implications for isospin-asymmetric dense matter modeling and validation of non-perturbative methods.

Abstract: We study quark matter at high isospin asymmetry in the limit of vanishing baryon density, a state of matter governed by the condensation of charged pions. In the weak-coupling limit, this state of matter can be reliably described by conventional perturbative methods where a trivial ground state is assumed. However, since the condensate of charged pions generates a gap in the excitation spectrum of the quarks with a non-perturbative dependence on the strong coupling, corrections to the results from conventional perturbative methods arise. This situation is very reminiscent of the one encountered in isospin-symmetric quark matter at high baryon densities which is expected to be a color superconductor. In the present work, we calculate the thermodynamic properties of superfluid quark matter at high isospin asymmetry, zero temperature, and zero baryon density at next-to-leading order (NLO) in the coupling in the presence of a finite gap. We work in the limit of two massless quark flavors and further assume that the gap is small compared to the isospin chemical potential. Similar to the situation in color-superconducting quark matter at high baryon densities, we find that the gap-induced corrections lead to an increase of the pressure and the speed of sound above their values obtained for non-superfluid quark matter. Notably, starting from asymptotically high isospin asymmetries, these corrections lead to a systematic change of the slope of the speed of sound when the isospin asymmetry is decreased. We compare our results with recent lattice QCD and Dyson-Schwinger equation studies and find good agreement in the regimes where these methods are applicable.

</details>


### [36] [Exploring a low-mass H dibaryon in light of the LZ event: A QCD sum rule study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25925)
*Wei-Han Tan,Hua-Xing Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the LUX-ZEPLIN candidate event could stem from a 1.85 GeV $uuddss$ hexaquark using QCD sum rules; only the $SU(3)$-singlet all-scalar-diquark current $J_{SSS}$ yields a compatible mass, but the prediction is OPE-truncation-dependent and not fully controlled.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the viability of a $J^P = 0^+$ $uuddss$ hexaquark as an explanation for the LUX-ZEPLIN high-energy nuclear recoil candidate event, interpreted by Lee, Takahashi, and Tsai as an invisible baryon-number-two scalar.

Method: QCD sum rule analysis of five local diquark-diquark-diquark interpolating currents (with scalar/axial-vector diquarks) and one baryon-baryon current; operator product expansion computed up to dimension sixteen; systematic examination of OPE truncation effects on spectral sum rules.

Result: Only the $SU(3)_F$-singlet current $J_{SSS}$ — built from three scalar color-$\bar{\mathbf{3}}_C$ diquarks in configuration $[ud][ds][su]$ — yields a stable low-mass solution at $M_{SSS} = 1.85^{+0.19}_{-0.20}$ GeV under dimension-12 OPE truncation; other currents fail to produce such a solution; higher-dimensional OPE contributions show strong truncation sensitivity.

Conclusion: The $[ud][ds][su]$ all-scalar-diquark hexaquark is a theoretically plausible, flavor-unique candidate matching the observed mass, but its predicted mass lacks robustness due to significant dependence on OPE truncation order — thus it remains a conditional, not definitive, prediction.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration recently reported a candidate event consistent with a high-energy nuclear recoil, motivating a possible interpretation by Lee, Takahashi, and Tsai involving the production of an invisible baryon-number-two scalar with a mass near $1.85$ GeV. Motivated by this hypothetical scenario, we investigate the $J^P=0^+$ $uuddss$ hexaquark system using QCD sum rules. We consider five local diquark-diquark-diquark interpolating currents with different scalar and axial-vector diquark combinations, together with a baryon-baryon current constructed from two $\Lambda$-type baryon currents. The operator product expansions are calculated up to dimension sixteen, and their numerical behaviors are examined under different OPE truncations. Among the currents considered, only the $SU(3)_{\rm flavor}$-singlet current $J_{SSS}$, consisting of three scalar color-$\bar{\mathbf{3}}_C$ diquarks, yields a low-mass solution at $D=12$, with $M_{SSS}=1.85^{+0.19}_{-0.20}$ GeV. Interestingly, scalar color-$\bar{\mathbf{3}}_C$ diquarks belong to the flavor $\bar{\mathbf{3}}_F$ representation, and their local color-singlet construction has a unique nonvanishing flavor structure, $[ud][ds][su]$, corresponding to $uuddss$. Analogous all-scalar-diquark currents vanish for other flavor configurations, such as $uuuddd$ and $uuudds$. Together with our QCD sum-rule results, this property highlights the special role of the all-scalar-diquark configuration, $[ud][ds][su]$, as a possible deeply bound structure. However, the higher-dimensional OPE contributions exhibit significant truncation dependence, and the low-mass solution remains conditional on the adopted OPE truncation rather than constituting a fully controlled mass prediction.

</details>


### [37] [Transverse momentum dependent parton distributions at small $x$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26004)
*Paul Caucal,Farid Salazar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This chapter reviews transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) parton distributions in the small-$x$ saturation regime, using the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective theory to bridge standard TMD factorization and high-density QCD dynamics; it covers operator definitions, gauge-link structures, Wilson-line correlators, evolution equations (BK/JIMWLK for $1/x$, CSS for Sudakov), sub-eikonal corrections for spin-dependent TMDs, and phenomenological implications—especially for the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard TMD factorization breaks down at small Bjorken $x$ due to large $1/x$ logarithms and non-linear gluon saturation effects; a unified theoretical framework connecting moderate- and small-$x$ TMDs is needed for precision QCD studies at current (LHC, RHIC) and future (EIC) facilities.

Method: Systematic review of CGC-based TMD formalism: derivation of quark/gluon TMD operators with proper gauge links; classification of Wilson-line correlators for unpolarized/polarized cases; factorization analysis of inclusive/diffractive processes; resummation via BK/JIMWLK and Collins-Soper-Sterman evolution; inclusion of sub-eikonal corrections for spin-dependent observables.

Result: A coherent CGC-based description of small-$x$ TMDs is established, identifying process-dependent universal Wilson-line structures; sub-eikonal operators enable access to helicity and spin asymmetries; double-logarithmic small-$x$ evolution yields asymptotic scaling laws; sub-eikonal corrections provide a controlled path toward moderate-$x$ matching.

Conclusion: The CGC provides a rigorous, factorization-compatible framework for small-$x$ TMDs, unifying saturation physics with TMD phenomenology; bridging to moderate-$x$ requires systematic inclusion of sub-eikonal effects, and full unification remains an open theoretical challenge critical for EIC physics.

Abstract: Transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) parton distributions provide a three-dimensional description of the momentum structure of hadrons. They are probed in proton-proton and proton-nucleus collisions at the LHC and RHIC, and are a central part of the physics program of the future Electron-Ion Collider (EIC). While TMD factorization is well established at moderate Bjorken $x$, the small-$x$ regime requires a different treatment, since large logarithms of $1/x$ must be resummed and non-linear effects due to high gluon densities become important. The Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective theory provides a natural framework to describe TMDs in this saturation regime and to connect them with standard TMD factorization. This chapter reviews quark and gluon TMDs at small $x$, covering operator definitions, gauge-link structures, and Wilson-line correlators in the CGC for unpolarized and polarized distributions. We discuss factorization for inclusive and diffractive observables, highlighting the role of process dependence and the emergence of a limited set of Wilson-line operators. We then summarize the evolution of these distributions, namely the resummation of logarithms of $1/x$ by the Balitsky-Kovchegov and JIMWLK equations, and of Sudakov logarithms of the hard scale through Collins-Soper-Sterman evolution. We also review TMDs beyond the eikonal approximation, including the sub-eikonal Wilson-line operators that give access to helicity and other spin-dependent distributions, their double-logarithmic small-$x$ evolution and the resulting asymptotics, and the sub-eikonal corrections to the eikonal distributions, which provide a first step toward the moderate-$x$ regime. Finally, we discuss phenomenological implications for present and future experiments, with emphasis on the EIC, and outline the theoretical challenges toward a unified description of TMDs.

</details>


### [38] [Flattenicity dependence of charge and transverse-momentum balance functions in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV with PYTHIA8](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26058)
*Srabani Dutta Roy,Rohit Agarwala,Kalyan Dey*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates charge balance (B) and charge-dependent transverse-momentum balance (P₂^CD) functions in 13.6 TeV pp collisions using PYTHIA 8.315, employing flattenicity to classify event topology; both observables show stronger, narrower near-side correlations with higher multiplicity and lower flattenicity (jet-like events), with P₂^CD exhibiting greater sensitivity to event shape than B.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle the effects of overall event activity (multiplicity) and event topology (via flattenicity) on charge and momentum correlations in high-energy pp collisions, and to assess the complementary sensitivity of B and P₂^CD to underlying particle-production mechanisms.

Method: Monte Carlo simulation using PYTHIA 8.315 with Monash tune at √s = 13.6 TeV; computation of B(Δη,Δφ) and P₂^CD(Δη,Δφ); event classification by flattenicity (a flow-insensitive event-shape observable); analysis across charged-particle multiplicity intervals.

Result: Both B and P₂^CD exhibit enhanced and narrowed near-side peaks (at Δη≈0, Δφ≈0) with increasing multiplicity and decreasing flattenicity; RMS widths decrease systematically; P₂^CD shows stronger flattenicity dependence than B, indicating superior sensitivity to event topology.

Conclusion: B and P₂^CD are complementary: B primarily reflects charge-dependent pair correlations, while P₂^CD is more sensitive to event-shape-driven momentum correlations; flattenicity-based event selection provides a powerful tool to isolate topological effects in small-system QCD dynamics.

Abstract: We study the charge balance function $B(\Delta\eta,\Delta\phi)$ and the charge-dependent transverse-momentum balance function $P_{2}^{\rm CD}(\Delta\eta,\Delta\phi)$ in proton--proton ($pp$) collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$~TeV using \texttt{PYTHIA~8.315} with the Monash tune. Flattenicity is employed as an event-shape observable to probe how these correlations depend on the topology of particle production. The results are examined across different charged-particle multiplicity intervals to disentangle the effects of overall event activity and event topology on $B$ and $P_{2}^{\rm CD}$. Both observables exhibit pronounced near-side correlations around $(\Delta\eta,\Delta\phi)\approx(0,0)$. The near-side peaks become stronger with increasing charged-particle multiplicity, and the RMS widths of both observables decrease systematically, indicating that the correlations become more localized in relative pseudorapidity and azimuth. A clear dependence on flattenicity is also observed: jet-like events (low flattenicity) generally exhibit stronger and narrower near-side correlations than isotropic events (high flattenicity). This event-shape dependence is more pronounced for $P_{2}^{\rm CD}$ than for $B$, indicating that $P_{2}^{\rm CD}$ is more sensitive to event topology, whereas $B$ primarily probes charge-dependent particle-pair correlations. These results demonstrate the complementary nature of the two observables and the potential of flattenicity-based event selection for studying particle-production mechanisms in high-multiplicity $pp$ collisions.

</details>

*Siyuan Li,Vladimir Pascalutsa,Maxim Pospelov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper highlights strong correlation between hadronic contributions to the electron and muon anomalous magnetic moments (a_e and a_μ), suggesting improved a_e and α measurements can constrain high-energy hadronic uncertainties and help resolve lattice vs. data-driven evaluation discrepancies.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the persistent discrepancy between lattice QCD and data-driven evaluations of hadronic vacuum polarization contributions to the anomalous magnetic moments, particularly a_μ, and to leverage correlations between a_e and a_μ for uncertainty reduction.

Method: Analysis of correlations between hadronic vacuum polarization contributions to a_e and a_μ within the Standard Model framework, combined with sensitivity studies to experimental improvements in a_e and the fine-structure constant α.

Result: Quantification of strong correlation between hadronic contributions to a_e and a_μ; demonstration that improved precision in a_e and α can significantly reduce dominant hadronic uncertainties in a_μ, especially those from higher-energy regions poorly constrained by e+e− data.

Conclusion: Precision measurements of a_e and α provide a complementary and powerful pathway to cross-check and constrain hadronic vacuum polarization, mitigating tensions between lattice and dispersive approaches and sharpening tests of the Standard Model.

Abstract: We note that the hadronic contributions to $a_e$ and $a_\mu$ are strongly correlated, which potentially allows one to reduce the uncertainties associated with higher-energy contributions (and address the current discrepancy between lattice and data-driven evaluations) by improved measurements of $a_e$ and the fine-structure constant.

</details>


### [40] [Gravitational Dark Matter Production in Einstein-Cartan Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26237)
*Kamil Mudrunka,Kazunori Nakayama*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how torsion in General Relativity—via Einstein-Cartan and Holst-modified gravity—affects fermionic dark matter production through new four-fermion interactions, including s-channel graviton exchange, and computes resulting changes to cross sections, Boltzmann collision terms, and relic abundance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve predictions of fermionic dark matter production by incorporating torsion-induced four-fermion interactions in Einstein-Cartan theory and extending to the Holst-modified gravity framework, which introduces additional interaction channels and coupling modifications.

Method: Analytical computation of scattering amplitudes and total cross sections for fermion–fermion interactions mediated by torsion and s-channel gravitons; derivation and incorporation of modified collision terms into the momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation; inclusion of Holst-term contributions yielding altered coupling constants and two new four-fermion operators.

Conclusion: Torsion-based extensions of gravity significantly impact fermionic dark matter genesis; neglecting s-channel graviton exchange or Holst-term contributions leads to incomplete predictions; full treatment is necessary for robust constraints on torsion scales and dark matter properties.

Abstract: Introducing torsion into General Relativity leads to new four-point interactions for fermions. These are universal and must also affect any fermion dark matter. We improve existing calculations of dark matter production through the Einstein-Cartan theory four-fermion interaction by also considering the s-channel graviton exchange as another contributing process. The effect on both the total cross section as well as the momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation collision term is calculated and discussed. We also introduce the Holst term, which generally modifies the four-fermion interaction coupling constant as well as introduces two new four-fermion interactions, and discuss and compute its effect on the total produced dark matter abundance.

</details>


### [41] [KNO scaling violation: the role of the fragmentation region](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26330)
*R. Terra,A. V. Giannini,F. S. Navarra*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the origin of KNO scaling violation in pp collisions at LHC energies using a two-component CGC model, finding that inclusion of collinear gluons and valence quarks—beyond small-x gluons—breaks KNO scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether collinear gluons and valence quarks are responsible for the observed violation of KNO scaling when extending from the central rapidity region (where scaling holds) to the full fragmentation region (where it breaks), within the CGC framework.

Method: Constructs a two-component Color Glass Condensate (CGC) model combining k_T-factorization (for small-x gluon-dominated central region) and hybrid formalism (including collinear gluons and valence quarks for fragmentation region), calibrated to reproduce pseudorapidity and multiplicity distributions in pp collisions at LHC energies.

Result: The model reproduces measured pseudorapidity and multiplicity distributions; KNO scaling holds when only small-x gluons contribute, but is violated upon inclusion of collinear gluons and valence quarks.

Conclusion: Collinear gluons and valence quarks are indeed responsible for KNO scaling violation in high-energy pp collisions, supporting the interpretation that scaling emerges from pure small-x dynamics and breaks when higher-x partonic degrees of freedom become relevant.

Abstract: This work is motivated by two independent considerations. The first is that KNO scaling is observed in the central rapidity region ($|\eta|<0.5$, for example) and violated when the fragmentation region is included ($|\eta|<3.4$, for example). The second is that, in the CGC framework, the $k_T$-factorization formalism is more appropriate to describe particle production in the central region and the hybrid formalism is more appropriate for the fragmentation region. The former contains small-x gluon interactions, where the latter introduce also collinear gluons and valence quarks in the dynamics. These two considerations put together raise the natural question: are collinear gluons and valence quarks responsible for KNO scaling violation? To answer this question we build a two-component CGC model which is able to reproduce the measured pseudorapidity distributions over all the available range as well as multiplicity distributions in pp collisions at LHC energies. With this model we find that KNO scaling occurs when the interaction is dominated by small-x gluons but is broken by the interactions including small-x gluons, collinear gluons and valence quarks.

</details>


### [42] [Photon induced corrections to Drell-Yan production in POWHEG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26334)
*Filippo Belloni,Mauro Chiesa,Clara Del Pio,Fulvio Piccinini*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper implements photon-induced contributions—including photon-initiated Drell-Yan and irreducible γγ → l⁺l⁻ background—into the Z_ew-BMNNPV Monte Carlo generator at NLO QCD + NLO EW accuracy, matched to QED/QCD parton showers (POWHEG), and presents phenomenological results for dilepton invariant mass and forward-backward asymmetry at LHC/HL-LHC energies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-precision theoretical predictions are essential for precision electroweak measurements at the LHC/HL-LHC; specifically, photon-induced corrections and the γγ → l⁺l⁻ background significantly impact the determination of the effective leptonic weak mixing angle via template-fit analyses of neutral-current Drell-Yan, as their uncertainties feed directly into experimental systematics.

Method: The authors implement photon-initiated processes (γq → l⁺l⁻q and γγ → l⁺l⁻) in the Z_ew-BMNNPV generator, achieving combined NLO QCD + NLO EW accuracy and consistent matching to both QED and QCD parton showers within the POWHEG framework.

Conclusion: Photon-initiated processes must be consistently included at NLO QCD+EW accuracy with QED/QCD shower matching in Monte Carlo generators used for precision Drell-Yan analyses; this implementation enables robust control of associated theoretical uncertainties in electroweak precision observables at the LHC and HL-LHC.

Abstract: High-precision theoretical predictions for both signal and background processes are crucial for the precision physics program at the LHC and HL-LHC. A prime example is the determination of the effective leptonic weak mixing angle from neutral current Drell-Yan via template-fit methods, where Monte Carlo templates directly serve as input to the measurement, making theoretical uncertainties part of the systematic error affecting the experimental results. In this paper, we focus on photon-induced corrections to neutral current Drell-Yan, as well as on the irreducible $\gamma\gamma \to l^+l^-$ background. We present the implementation of the photon-initiated contributions in the Z_ew-BMNNPV Monte Carlo generator for Drell-Yan production at NLO QCD + NLO EW accuracy matched to QED and QCD parton showers within the POWHEG framework. We discuss phenomenological results for the dilepton invariant-mass distribution and the forward-backward asymmetry in the Z-resonance region and in the TeV range.

</details>


### [43] [Pion and $η$ meson gravitational form factors from GPDs at non-zero skewness and distribution amplitudes within the Graviton Soft-Wall model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26377)
*Filippo Fornetti,Matteo Rinaldi,Sergio Scopetta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes gravitational form factors (GFFs) of the pion and η meson using the graviton soft-wall (GSW) model, compares pion GFFs with lattice QCD data, derives mechanical properties (pressure, shear force, etc.), and contrasts pion vs. η structural differences.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extract hadronic mechanical properties from gravitational form factors—key probes of internal energy-momentum distribution—and to compare light-quark meson structures (pion vs. η) within a holographic framework.

Conclusion: The GSW model provides a consistent holographic description of meson gravitational structure; mechanical properties are sensitive to quark content and mass, enabling discrimination between pion and η internal dynamics.

Abstract: We present the calculations of the pion and $\eta$ meson gravitational form factors (GFFs) from the evaluation of generalized parton distribution functions at non-zero skewness within the graviton soft-wall (GSW) model of hadron structure. Results for the pion have been successfully compared with lattice data for both the $A$ and $D$ GFFs. From these quantities, the mechanical properties of the pion, i.e., the pressure, shear-force, normal and tangential force distributions have been also investigated. Finally, we study also the $\eta$ meson GFFs to predict and observe differences between the pion and $\eta$ structures in the corresponding mechanical properties within the GSW model.

</details>


### [44] [Probing Leptophilic Scalar Dark Matter with SMEFT and Direct Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26512)
*Simone Biondini,Chiara Megan Bolloni,Orlando Panella,Lorenzo Tiberi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies leptophilic scalar dark matter (real and complex) within SMEFT, performing full one-loop matching to the Warsaw basis and combining precision electroweak, direct/indirect detection, and relic density constraints—including coannihilation, Sommerfeld enhancement, bound-state formation, and loop corrections. It derives exact DM–photon interactions for nuclear recoils and shows SMEFT global fits constrain DM up to ~850 GeV; direct detection dominates in compressed spectra; complex scalars are tightly constrained by charge-radius operators; real scalars remain viable; and modified cosmology significantly reshapes relic-density–allowed parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Heavy dark sectors may be inaccessible to direct collider production but leave detectable imprints in precision observables and DM searches; the authors aim to systematically assess viability and complementarity of probes for leptophilic scalar DM using SMEFT as a consistent EFT framework.

Method: Complete one-loop matching onto dimension-six Warsaw basis; computation of relic abundance including coannihilation with vector-like leptonic mediators, Sommerfeld enhancement, bound-state formation, and one-loop annihilation corrections; derivation of exact effective DM–photon couplings for nuclear recoil (beyond heavy-mediator limit); combination of electroweak precision tests (global SMEFT fits), direct detection (XENONnT/LZ), indirect detection (Fermi-LAT), and cosmological constraints.

Result: SMEFT global fits constrain scalar DM up to ~850 GeV; direct detection is most sensitive for compressed spectra (small DM–mediator mass splitting); complex scalar DM is strongly excluded by the dimension-six charge-radius operator h†h BμνBμν; real scalar DM survives across broad parameter space; modified expansion history (e.g., early dark energy or kination) alters relic-density–allowed regions and probe complementarity.

Conclusion: Leptophilic scalar DM—especially real—is still viable and testable across multiple frontiers; SMEFT provides a powerful, unified language to connect high-energy collider precision, low-energy DM experiments, and cosmology; neglecting loop effects, mediator dynamics, or cosmological assumptions risks misleading exclusion limits.

Abstract: Heavy dark sectors may evade direct production at current colliders while leaving measurable imprints in precision observables and dark matter searches. We study leptophilic scalar dark matter within the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT), considering both real and complex scalar dark matter coupled to vector-like leptonic mediators. We perform the complete one-loop matching of the model onto the dimension-six Warsaw basis and combine the resulting precision constraints with predictions for direct and indirect detection. We compute the relic abundance including coannihilations with the mediator, Sommerfeld enhancement, bound-state formation, and one-loop corrections to scalar dark matter annihilation. We also derive the effective dark matter-photon interactions relevant for nuclear recoils, retaining the full dependence on the dark matter and mediator masses and extending previous results beyond the heavy-mediator limit. We find that global SMEFT fits provide competitive constraints for dark matter masses up to approximately $850$ GeV, while direct detection becomes particularly powerful for compressed spectra. The complex scalar scenario is strongly constrained by the charge-radius interaction, whereas real scalar dark matter remains viable across a substantial region of parameter space. Finally, we show that a modified cosmological expansion history can significantly shift the relic-density-compatible parameter space, thereby changing the complementarity among the different probes.

</details>


### [45] [Dark Matter Interpretations of the Galactic Center Gamma-Ray Excess with 18 Years of Fermi-LAT Data and the FL16Y Source Catalog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26648)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes dark matter interpretations of the Galactic Center Excess using 17.7 years of Fermi-LAT data, testing multiple annihilation channels and diffuse models; it finds that dSph limits strongly constrain most two-body and some four-body DM models (e.g., bb, cc, 4b, 2b2τ), while τ⁺τ⁻, 4τ, and certain mediator-coupled cascade scenarios remain partially viable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the Galactic Center Excess (GCE) — an unexplained gamma-ray excess at ~1–3 GeV — can be explained by dark matter annihilation, in light of improved data, systematic treatment of diffuse emission, and robust multi-messenger constraints (dSphs, AMS-02 antiprotons).

Method: Bayesian template fitting of Fermi-LAT 17.7-yr data using FL16Y catalog and 80 Galactic diffuse models; inclusion of correlated systematics from Galactic-plane control regions; derivation of joint 95% CL limits from 30 dSphs with J-factor uncertainties; comparison with AMS-02 antiproton bounds under BIG cosmic-ray propagation; analysis across two-body (bb, cc, ττ, gg, light quarks), effective four-body (4b, 4τ, 2b2τ), and scalar-mediated cascade models (including Model X and secluded singlet fermion).

Result: dSph limits exclude the 68% CL GCE parameter space for bb, cc, light quarks, gluons, and effective 4b/2b2τ; ττ and 4τ retain compatible subregions (though best-fit points exceed limits); 4b cascade survives for r = m_med/m_DM ≲ 0.4; antiproton bounds (weaker under BIG model) constrain part of the 4b cascade region; secluded singlet fermion with 4b cascade remains a viable benchmark.

Conclusion: Most canonical two-body DM annihilation channels are ruled out as explanations of the GCE given current dSph and antiproton constraints; however, specific four-body final states — particularly 4τ and mediator-enabled 4b cascades with light mediators — remain marginally consistent and warrant further theoretical and observational scrutiny.

Abstract: We investigate dark matter (DM) interpretations of the Galactic Center Excess (GCE) using 17.7 years of Fermi-LAT data and the FL16Y source catalog. We test 80 Galactic diffuse-emission models and include correlated systematic uncertainties from Galactic-plane control regions. We derive combined 95\% CL annihilation limits from 30 dwarf spheroidal galaxies (dSphs), including $J$-factor uncertainties, and compare the GCE-preferred regions with these limits and AMS-02 antiproton bounds. For self-conjugate DM, we study two-body annihilation, effective $4b$, $4\tau$, and $2b2\tau$ channels, and cascades through on-shell scalar mediators. For Model X, the best-fitting diffuse-emission model in our template analysis including a DM component, the dSph limits exclude the 68\% CL GCE regions for $b\bar b$, $c\bar c$, light quarks, gluons, and effective $4b$ and $2b2\tau$. Parts of the $\tau^+\tau^-$ and effective $4\tau$ regions remain compatible, although their best-fit points exceed these limits. Heavy two-body final states are less constrained but fit the GCE less well. Mediator masses affect both the preferred regions and the constraints. Part of the 68\% CL $4b$ cascade region remains below the dSph limits for $r=m_{\rm mediator}/m_{\rm DM}\lesssim0.4$. Among the four-body benchmarks, $4\tau$ retains a comparatively large preferred region compatible with dSph and antiproton bounds. With the adopted BIG propagation model, antiproton bounds are generally weaker but constrain part of the $4b$ cascade region. Finally, we examine a secluded singlet fermionic DM benchmark with a predominantly four-bottom cascade.

</details>


### [46] [Impact of cosmic evolution on the gravitational wave spectrum from cosmic strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26652)
*Xi-He Hu,Danny Marfatia,Ye-Ling Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines how various early-Universe physics scenarios—such as kination, early matter domination, and extra relativistic degrees of freedom—affect the gravitational wave spectrum from cosmic strings, finding that kination enhances the signal while others suppress it; late-time dynamical dark energy has negligible effect.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how departures from the standard radiation-dominated cosmology in the early Universe alter the predicted stochastic gravitational wave background from cosmic string networks.

Method: Systematic analytical and semi-analytical modeling of cosmic string network evolution and loop production under modified expansion histories—including kination, early matter domination, intermediate vacuum domination, and extra relativistic degrees of freedom—followed by computation of resulting GW spectra.

Result: Kination enhances the GW amplitude across observable frequencies (e.g., LIGO, LISA, PTA bands); early matter domination, extra radiation, and vacuum domination suppress the signal; late-time dynamical dark energy introduces no measurable change to the GW spectrum.

Conclusion: The GW spectrum from cosmic strings is a sensitive probe of pre-BBN cosmology; detection or non-detection of the string signal can constrain or rule out specific early-Universe physics scenarios, especially kination.

Abstract: In the standard cosmological paradigm, the gravitational wave (GW) spectrum from a cosmic string network is well defined. However, early-Universe dynamics can significantly alter this relic signal. We systematically investigate how different new physics epochs in the radiation era--including extra relativistic degrees of freedom, early matter domination, intermediate vacuum domination, and kination--modify string network evolution and loop production. We find that a kination era enhances the stochastic GW signal, whereas the other scenarios suppress the signal to varying degrees. We also demonstrate that late-time dynamical dark energy has a negligible impact on the GW spectrum from cosmic strings.

</details>


### [47] [Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism with non-invertible symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26761)
*Gui-Jun Ding,Stephen F. King,Bu-Yao Qu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism using non-invertible symmetries (via ℤ₂ gauging of ℤₙ or U(1)), combines it with a flavon field to fix both texture zeros and hierarchical coupling magnitudes via fusion rules, and constructs viable quark/lepton models reproducing observed mass/mixing patterns—including nine texture zeros in quarks and a type-I seesaw with normal ordering in leptons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified symmetry framework—based on non-invertible symmetries—that simultaneously explains fermion mass hierarchies and texture-zero structures, going beyond conventional invertible flavor symmetries which cannot fix coupling magnitudes.

Method: Constructs non-invertible symmetries by ℤ₂ gauging of ℤₙ or U(1); incorporates a flavon field S whose VEV introduces a small parameter ε = ⟨S⟩/Λ; uses fusion rules of the resulting topological defect lines to constrain and quantify Yukawa couplings; builds explicit quark and lepton models with analytical and numerical validation.

Result: A benchmark quark model with nine texture zeros successfully fits quark masses and CKM parameters using non-Hermitian Yukawa matrices; the associated lepton sector realizes minimal type-I seesaw with normal neutrino mass ordering and one massless neutrino; fusion rules fix both zero/nonzero structure and ε-powers of all entries up to O(1) coefficients.

Conclusion: Non-invertible symmetries, augmented by a flavon, provide a robust, predictive framework for fermion mass textures and hierarchies—unifying explanation of zeros and magnitude patterns in both quark and lepton sectors.

Abstract: We formulate the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism in the framework of non-invertible symmetries obtained by $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gauging of $\mathbb{Z}_N$ or $U(1)$. While the non-invertible selection rules can constrain the texture-zero structure of fermion mass matrices, they do not by themselves determine the magnitudes of the allowed couplings. Introducing a flavon field allows the fusion rules to fix the leading powers of the small parameter $\varepsilon=\langle S\rangle/\Lambda$, thereby determining both the texture zeros and the orders of magnitude of the nonzero entries up to $\mathcal{O}(1)$ coefficients. We have constructed explicit quark and lepton models and study their predictions for the fermion mass and mixing hierarchies through analytical estimates and numerical analysis. In particular, a benchmark model with nine texture zeros in the quark sector can well accommodate the quark masses and mixing parameters with non-Hermitian Yukawa matrices. Its lepton sector admits a minimal type-I seesaw realization with normal neutrino mass ordering and one massless neutrino. These results provide a common symmetry framework for fermion mass hierarchies and texture zeros.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Abstract: We present a quantum simulation of the SU(2) lattice gauge theory in 1+1 dimensions with one flavor of fermions, performed using the trapped-ion Quantinuum System Model H2. This work is based on the loop-string-hadron (LSH) formulation of the gauge theory, and is distinguished by using qubits to represent both bosonic-gauge and fermionic-matter degrees of freedom. Furthermore, we access the $j=1$ electric-flux sector beyond the $j_{\max}=1/2$ truncation commonly used in SU(2) quantum simulations. To that end, we digitize an electric-field degree of freedom using a multiqubit register for representing the bosonic quantum number. Our simulation therefore synthesizes several key ingredients relevant to quantum simulations in higher spatial dimensions, in particular explicit local gauge-field degrees of freedom represented here by a multiqubit bosonic register, while relying solely on circuit-construction optimizations that are expected to scale polynomially with lattice size. This work demonstrates that modern quantum hardware is increasingly able to accommodate the features of complicated but physically important lattice gauge theories while successfully reproducing the expected Trotterized dynamics.

Abstract: We extend the perturbative investigation of the renormalization and mixing patterns of the supercurrent operator in lattice ${\cal N}=1$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills (SYM) theory, using stout-smeared gauge links. We employ the SLiNC action for gluinos and a family of Symanzik improved gauge actions for gluons. Building upon previous studies employing clover-improved fermions and the Wilson gauge action, we compute the one-loop mixing matrix of the supercurrent operator among gauge-invariant and gauge-variant operators. Particular emphasis is placed on the structure of operator mixing in the presence of stout smearing parameters. Our results contribute toward refining fine-tuning strategies necessary for the restoration of supersymmetry in the continuum limit, and provide a stepping stone for future perturbative and nonperturbative investigations in supersymmetric theories on the lattice.

### [50] [Recent Advances in Transverse-Momentum--Dependent Distributions in Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24785)
*Huey-Wen Lin,Wei Wang,Yong Zhao*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent progress in extracting Transverse Momentum Dependent parton distributions (TMDs) from lattice QCD, highlighting methods like Mellin-moment ratios, LaMET, and Coulomb-gauge approaches; it reports first-principles results for unpolarized and spin-dependent TMDs (e.g., Boer-Mulders function), identifies key challenges (continuum limit, infinite-momentum extrapolation), and outlines paths toward 3D hadron structure determination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable first-principles, non-perturbative access to TMDs — essential for understanding 3D hadron structure and spin-momentum correlations — bridging the gap between experimental measurements (JLab, COMPASS, LHC, EIC) and QCD theory.

Method: Lattice QCD techniques including ratios of Mellin moments, large-momentum effective theory (LaMET), and Coulomb-gauge formulations to extract TMD-related quantities (Collins-Soper kernel, soft functions, TMD wave functions, TMDPDFs).

Result: First lattice-QCD extractions of both unpolarized and spin-dependent TMDs (e.g., Boer-Mulders function); demonstration of feasibility to access transverse-momentum–dependent information directly from QCD.

Conclusion: Lattice QCD has matured sufficiently to deliver quantitative TMD insights, but robust continuum and infinite-momentum limits remain critical open challenges; future work aims at precision 3D hadron tomography via systematic error control and algorithmic advances.

Abstract: Unraveling the three-dimensional structure of hadrons is a central objective in modern nuclear and particle physics. Transverse Momentum Dependent parton distributions (TMDs) provide crucial insights into the interplay between partonic transverse motion and spin, and are indispensable for describing a wide range of high-energy processes at facilities such as JLab, COMPASS, the LHC, and the upcoming Electron-Ion Collider. In recent years, significant progress has been made in accessing TMDs from lattice QCD using novel approaches. Methods based on ratios of Mellin moments, large-momentum effective theory (LaMET), and Coulomb-gauge techniques have enabled first-principles investigations of the Collins-Soper kernel, intrinsic soft functions, TMD wave functions, and TMDPDFs. Recent lattice results for both unpolarized and spin-dependent distributions, including the Boer-Mulders function, demonstrate the feasibility of extracting transverse-momentum--dependent information directly from QCD. This paper reviews recent advances in lattice-QCD studies of TMDs, outlines remaining challenges such as continuum and infinite-momentum extrapolations, and discusses future directions toward precision determinations of hadron structure in three dimensions.

</details>


### [51] [Expansion schemes for finite density QCD equation of state: which one is the best?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25494)
*Szabolcs Borsányi,Zoltán Fodor,Jana N. Guenther,Piyush Kumar,Paolo Parotto,Attila Pásztor,Chik Him Wong*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper compares convergence properties of two expansion schemes—based on constant baryon density vs. constant entropy density contours—for extrapolating the QCD equation of state to finite baryon chemical potential, using high-order susceptibilities (up to order 8) computed via extreme-statistics lattice simulations on a 16³×8 lattice.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct lattice QCD simulations at finite baryon density suffer from the sign problem; thus, expansion methods around μ_B = 0 using conserved-charge susceptibilities are widely used—but their convergence behavior is poorly quantified. This work aims to systematically assess and compare convergence efficacy of different contour-based extrapolation schemes.

Method: Extreme-statistics lattice QCD simulations on a 16³×8 anisotropic lattice with Wilson-Clover fermions and tree-level Symanzik-improved gauge action; computation of baryon-number and entropy susceptibilities up to eighth order; comparison of Taylor expansions along contours of constant normalized baryon density (n_B/T³ = const) versus constant entropy density (s/T³ = const); analysis in both 1D (μ_B) and 3D (μ_B, μ_S, μ_Q) chemical potential space.

Result: The entropy-density-based contour method converges significantly faster: for μ_B ≤ 500 MeV, only susceptibilities up to fourth order are needed for sub-1% accuracy, whereas the baryon-density-based method requires higher orders; this superiority persists in the full three-flavor (μ_B, μ_S, μ_Q) parameter space.

Conclusion: Entropy-density-based expansion contours provide superior convergence for mapping the finite-density QCD equation of state, making them the preferred choice for reliable extrapolations into the QCD phase diagram—especially where high-order susceptibilities are costly or noisy.

Abstract: The finite density equation of state of QCD is a fundamental quantity for understanding the phase structure of the theory, and also for the realistic modeling of the QCD medium in simulations. While direct lattice simulations at finite density are extremely challenging, several expansion schemes have been devised to extrapolate into the QCD phase diagram. All of these schemes are based on susceptibilities of conserved charges, i.e. derivatives of the pressure with respect to the chemical potential(s). In this work we set out to determine which of these schemes possesses the best convergence properties. To this end, we employ extreme-statistics lattice simulations on a $16^3\times8$ lattice, which allow us to obtain susceptibilities up to order eight with remarkably small errors. We compare in particular two methods, based on contours of constant (normalized) baryon density and of constant entropy density. We find that the latter converges more quickly, effectively requiring susceptibilities only up to order four to cover the chemical potential range up to at least $\mu_B \approx 500$ MeV. We also extend the comparison to the three dimensional space of chemical potentials, and find that the entropy-based method consistently shows superior convergence.

</details>

### [52] [Onset of Chaos and Orbital Instabilities around Rotating Weyl Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23976)
*Shubham Kala,M. Yousaf,Pradeep Singh,Faisal Javed,Hemwati Nandan,G. Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies chaotic photon motion near rotating black holes in Weyl conformal gravity using multiple chaos indicators; it finds that BH spin and conformal gravity parameters (ξ, γ) jointly drive a transition from integrable to chaotic dynamics, destabilizing photon spheres and offering a probe for testing gravity beyond General Relativity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how deviations from General Relativity—specifically higher-order curvature corrections in Weyl conformal gravity—affect the nonlinear dynamics of photons near rotating black holes, and whether such effects can serve as observational probes of strong-field gravity.

Method: Numerical analysis of null geodesics using Poincaré sections, Lyapunov exponents, Fast Lyapunov Indicators (FLI), Kolmogorov–Sinai (KS) entropy, and Discrete Indicator of Chaos (DIG) maps, under varying black hole spin and Weyl gravity parameters ξ and γ.

Result: Increasing ξ and γ deform and break invariant tori, generate resonance islands and stochastic layers, expand chaotic phase-space regions (especially near extremal spin), elevate Lyapunov exponents and KS entropy, accelerate FLI growth, and shrink regular domains in DIG maps—indicating strong destabilization of null circular orbits.

Conclusion: Relativistic frame-dragging and Weyl gravity’s higher-curvature terms synergistically induce chaos in photon trajectories; nonlinear photon dynamics thus provides a sensitive diagnostic for testing conformal gravity and other GR extensions in the strong-field regime.

Abstract: We investigate nonlinear photon motion in the vicinity of a rotating black hole (BH) within Weyl conformal gravity (WCG) by employing Poincare sections, Lyapunov exponents, Fast Lyapunov Indicators (FLI), Kolmogorov-Sinai (KS) entropy, and Discrete Indicator of Chaos (DIG) maps. The joint influence of BH spin and higher order curvature corrections, governed by the parameters $\xi$ and $\gamma$, induces a transition from nearly integrable trajectories to markedly chaotic dynamics in the strong field domain. As $\xi$ and $\gamma$ increase, the invariant tori are increasingly deformed and eventually break up. Resonance islands and stochastic layers then form, producing large chaotic regions in phase space, especially near the extremal spin regime. Increasing Lyapunov exponents, faster FLI growth, and higher KS entropy provide quantitative measures of the enhanced dynamical instability, the increased sensitivity to initial conditions, and the corresponding rate of information production. DIG maps show shrinking regular regions and expanding chaotic domains around the photon sphere, indicating that conformal gravity corrections destabilize null circular orbits. Taken together, these findings clarify how relativistic frame dragging and higher order curvature terms jointly control photon chaos and point to nonlinear photon dynamics as a potential probe of departures from General Relativity in the strong-gravity regime.

</details>


### [53] [How do late-time photons "record" primordial curvature perturbations?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23999)
*Tomotaka Kuroda,Fumiya Sano,Yusuke Yamada*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces Cosmological Classical Branches (CCBs) to resolve the quantum-to-classical transition problem in inflationary cosmology, using quantum information concepts—specifically physical records—to explain how a single classical realization emerges from a global quantum superposition of primordial fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the fundamental gap between the quantum nature of primordial fluctuations and the classical description used in standard cosmology, where decoherence and squeezing alone fail to justify the 'one-realization' assumption underlying CMB and LSS observations.

Method: Employs quantum-information-theoretic framework of 'physical records': distinct late-time matter components encode compatible, redundant records of the same primordial field configuration; interference between configurations is suppressed when multiple disjoint records exist, enabling effective classicality per branch while preserving global superposition.

Result: Demonstrates that Cosmological Classical Branches allow finite-resolution c-number labels for primordial configurations—recovering the operational validity of classical perturbation theory—without collapsing the universal wavefunction.

Conclusion: CCBs provide a physically well-motivated, record-based mechanism for emergent classicality in cosmology, grounding the standard classical treatment of curvature perturbations in quantum information principles rather than ad hoc assumptions.

Abstract: Inflationary cosmology faces a fundamental gap between primordial quantum fluctuations and the classical cosmic history inferred from observations. Although the primordial quantum state is a superposition of field configurations, standard cosmology assumes one definite realization of curvature perturbations and uses classical perturbation theory to describe the resulting gravitational potentials, CMB anisotropies, and large-scale structure. Squeezing and decoherence of primordial fluctuations alone do not explain why this one-realization description is physically meaningful. We address this issue through Cosmological Classical Branches (CCBs), using the quantum-information-theoretic notion of physical records. In CCBs, distinct late-time matter components carry compatible records of the same primordial configuration. A record in one component makes that configuration readable, while a disjoint compatible record suppresses interference accessible to operations on the first. Each branch thus admits a common c-number primordial label at finite resolution, while the global quantum superposition persists. This Letter summarizes the framework developed in Ref.~\cite{Kuroda2026CCB}.

</details>


### [54] [Group averaging and the geometry of physical quantum states: from normalizable solutions to solvable loop quantum cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24027)
*Alejandro Corichi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes group averaging in canonical quantum gravity using geometric quantum mechanics, showing how physical states emerge from kinematical seeds; it distinguishes intrinsic Dirac structures (e.g., physical rays, geometry) from artifacts of seed/test-space/clock choices, and applies the framework to loop quantum cosmology where constraint spectrum edges induce clock-dependent biases and break Kähler isometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the geometric structure of group averaging—how physical states are constructed from kinematical seeds—and to disentangle fundamental Dirac structures (e.g., physical ray space, observables) from gauge- or regularization-dependent artifacts such as seed choice, test-space topology, or clock implementation.

Method: Uses geometric quantum mechanics to characterize the seed-to-state map: treats kinematical rays as a holomorphic vector bundle over physical ray space when averaging is orthogonal projection; for distributional states, constructs an affine bundle over physical rays on a Sobolev test space; analyzes Hessian conditions for observable descent; applies to solvable loop quantum cosmology with explicit constraint and Klein–Gordon inner product reconstruction.

Result: Group averaging yields a holomorphic vector bundle (orthogonal case) or affine bundle (distributional case); fibers strictly exceed gauge orbits; observables descend iff constraint Hessian vanishes along their flow; in loop quantum cosmology, quadratic scalar momentum induces a spectral edge near the constraint shell, causing mode-dependent non-Kähler canonical sections, bias of small-universe seeds off-shell, and separation of Page–Wootters and Klein–Gordon slices—none of which appear with an ideal clock.

Conclusion: Physical rays, physical geometry, and Dirac observables are canonical and independent of regularization choices, whereas seed embeddings, test-space inner products, clock-induced biases, and approximate solution sequences (e.g., Weyl sequences) depend on auxiliary structures—highlighting a clean separation between intrinsic quantum-gravitational content and methodological artifacts.

Abstract: In canonical quantum gravity the physical states solve the constraints but are typically not normalizable in the kinematical Hilbert space. Group averaging constructs them from normalizable kinematical seeds. We describe the geometry of this seed-to-state map in the language of geometric quantum mechanics. When averaging is an orthogonal projection, the admissible kinematical rays form a holomorphic vector bundle over the physical ray space. The embedded exact solutions form its zero section, and its fibers are strictly larger than gauge orbits. Observables descend to the base exactly when the Hessian of the squared constraint vanishes along their flow. For distributional physical states, group averaging on an explicit Sobolev test space yields an affine bundle of representatives. Its sections are embeddings of the physical ray space whose images contain no exact solutions. The test-space inner product singles out one of them, and narrowing the seed profile gives Weyl sequences of approximate solutions. We apply this to solvable loop quantum cosmology. Group averaging reproduces the Klein--Gordon physical inner product on an explicit test space. Because the scalar-field momentum enters the constraint quadratically, the constraint spectrum has an edge near the shell. The canonical section is then mode-dependent and not Kähler isometric, and the canonical seeds of small universes are biased off the shell. The edge also separates the Page--Wootters state from the slice of the Klein--Gordon solution. An ideal clock shows none of these effects. The results separate the canonical structures, namely physical rays, physical geometry and Dirac observables, from those that depend on a choice of seeds, test space or clock.

</details>


### [55] [Linearised gravitational radiation in de Sitter spacetime at all multipole orders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24303)
*Harsh,Omkar Shetye,Amitabh Virmani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives gravitational wave solutions in de Sitter spacetime using a flat-space multipole formalism, absorbing causal tail effects into an auxiliary stress tensor, yielding radiative multipole moments as local integrals of the physical stress-energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a clean, local multipole expansion for gravitational waves in de Sitter spacetime—where causal structure and memory (tail) effects complicate standard approaches—by leveraging flat-space techniques without history integrals.

Method: Exact mapping of linearised de Sitter gravity in harmonic gauge to an auxiliary flat-space problem with a conserved, effective stress tensor; application of Damour–Iyer multipole formalism; explicit construction of radiative moments and canonical exterior metric; verification via quadrupole limit and residual gauge analysis.

Result: Radiative gravitational wave field expressed at arbitrary multipole order via two families of moments defined as local (instantaneous) integrals of the physical stress tensor; de Sitter tail fully encoded in the auxiliary source; quadrupolar solution recovered up to an explicit residual gauge transformation.

Conclusion: The linearised gravitational radiation problem in de Sitter spacetime admits a fully local multipole description akin to flat space, provided the tail is absorbed into an effective conserved source—eliminating non-local history integrals from both moments and the canonical metric.

Abstract: We derive the linearised gravitational waves generated by a localised matter source in the Poincare patch of de Sitter spacetime at arbitrary multipole order. The linearised de Sitter problem is mapped exactly to an auxiliary flat-space problem in harmonic gauge, with a conserved source built from the physical stress tensor. This allows us to use the multipole expansion of Damour and Iyer verbatim, and yields the exterior metric at all orders in terms of two families of radiative multipole moments given as explicit integrals of the physical stress tensor. The de Sitter tail is absorbed entirely into the auxiliary stress tensor, so that neither the moments nor the canonical exterior metric carry an integral over the past history of the source. As a check, we recover the known quadrupolar metric: in the quadrupolar truncation it is our canonical field up to a residual gauge transformation, which we construct explicitly.

</details>


### [56] [Grey-body factors of black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24356)
*Roman Konoplya,Alexander Zhidenko*

Main category: gr-qc
