TL;DR: This review unifies the theory and applications of grey-body factors—frequency-dependent transmission coefficients for fields scattering off black holes and compact objects—across spacetime geometries (flat, dS, AdS), connects them to Hawking radiation, absorption/scattering, quasinormal modes, superradiance, wormholes, modified gravity, and gauge/gravity duality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Grey-body factors are crucial real-frequency observables bridging idealized black-hole thermodynamics (e.g., Hawking radiation) and measurable spectra; yet their theoretical foundations, computational methods, and physical implications across diverse gravitational and holographic contexts lack a consolidated treatment.

Method: The paper adopts a unified theoretical framework: formulating the radial scattering problem in asymptotically flat, de Sitter, and anti-de Sitter spacetimes; analytically and numerically computing transmission/absorption coefficients; establishing connections to quasinormal mode spectra, stability under potential deformations, and holographic dictionary entries (e.g., bulk absorption ↔ boundary correlators).

Result: A coherent synthesis of grey-body factor theory is delivered, including: (i) systematic derivation of absorption cross sections and Hawking spectra across spacetime types; (ii) elucidation of deep links to quasinormal modes (e.g., pole-skipping, spectral rigidity); (iii) demonstration of robustness under effective-potential deformations; (iv) extensions to exotic objects (wormholes) and phenomena (superradiance); (v) generalization to modified gravity; and (vi) precise mapping to boundary CFT data via AdS/CFT.

Conclusion: Grey-body factors serve as fundamental, multifaceted probes of strong-field gravity and quantum aspects of black holes—not only encoding observable radiation features but also acting as bridges between scattering physics, quasi-normal dynamics, stability analysis, and holographic quantum field theory.

Abstract: Grey-body factors describe the frequency-dependent transmission of fields through the effective potential generated by a black hole or another compact object. They convert the idealized thermal spectrum of Hawking radiation into the spectrum measured by an observer, determine absorption and scattering cross sections, and provide real-frequency observables complementary to quasinormal modes. In this review, we present a unified account of grey-body factors in black-hole physics and related systems. We formulate the scattering problem for asymptotically flat, de Sitter, and anti-de Sitter spacetimes, and discuss the connections between transmission coefficients, absorption cross sections, Hawking radiation, and ringdown. We also review methods for their computation and survey recent developments, including the correspondence between grey-body factors and quasinormal modes, their stability under deformations of the effective potential, wormhole transmission and superradiance, and their behavior in modified theories of gravity. Finally, we discuss the appearance of grey-body factors in gauge/gravity duality, where absorption probabilities in the bulk are related to correlation functions and transport coefficients in the boundary theory.

</details>


### [57] [Complexification of the Kerr-AdS black hole thermodynamic phase transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24694)
*Fei Guo,Zhen-Ming Xu,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a complex-analytical framework using analytic continuation of the horizon radius to study thermodynamics and phase structure of high-dimensional singly spinning Kerr-AdS black holes, identifying zeroth-order transitions below the critical point and constructing a Widom line above it via Lee-Yang theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand thermodynamic phase behavior—including phase transitions and supercritical crossovers—of high-dimensional singly spinning Kerr-AdS black holes beyond standard real-variable methods.

Method: Analytic continuation of the black hole horizon radius into the complex plane; mapping thermodynamic behavior to zeros of the associated analytic function; application of Lee-Yang theory to define the Widom line in the supercritical region.

Result: A criterion for zeroth-order phase transitions below the critical point is established, showing no change in thermodynamic topological structure; a Widom line is constructed to characterize supercritical crossover behavior.

Conclusion: Complex-analytical techniques provide a powerful and novel framework for black hole thermodynamics, enabling unified treatment of subcritical phase transitions and supercritical crossovers.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish a systematic complex-analytical framework for investigating the thermodynamics of high-dimensional singly spinning Kerr-AdS black holes. By performing an analytical continuation of the black hole horizon radius into the complex plane, we map the thermodynamic behavior for the black hole to the zeros distribution of the corresponding analytical function. In the region below the critical point, this approach provides the criterion for identifying phase transitions and reveals that the zeroth-order phase transition does not alter the underlying thermodynamic topological structure of the black hole. In the supercritical region, based on the Lee-Yang theory of phase transition, we construct the Widom line to characterize the supercritical crossover behavior for the Kerr-AdS black holes. This study provides a new perspective on the thermodynamic analysis of black holes.

</details>


### [58] [A Casimir turning point as the beginning of a small flat universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24905)
*Alan Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a cosmological model where a flat three-torus universe contracts and bounces due to negative Casimir energy from Standard Model fields with antiperiodic fermions; the bounce occurs at ~722 ℓₚ, where Higgs stability depends critically on top mass and curvature coupling, and observational constraints from CMB and future colliders (e.g., FCC-ee) can test or exclude the scenario.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically viable, non-singular beginning of the universe without inflation or exotic matter, using only known Standard Model fields and quantum vacuum effects—specifically Casimir energy—in a compact topology (T³).

Method: Analytical quantum field theory in curved spacetime on a contracting/expanding torus; computation of Casimir energy for antiperiodic SM fermions; renormalization-group evolution of Higgs quartic coupling including zero-mode confinement by gauge fluctuations; probabilistic assessment of Higgs vacuum survival; quantum state analysis of torus shape dynamics; comparison with CMB geometric constraints and Bayesian model testing.

Result: A consistent turnaround scale of 722 ℓₚ is found; Higgs remains metastable with >50% probability only if m_t^pole < 172.96 GeV; shape stabilization requires ξ_H ≳ 0.08 at scale M̄_P/722; torus becomes nearly rectangular (side ratios ~16–18%); CMB detects fossil topology only if shortest cycle is 0.985–1.04× last-scattering diameter; FCC-ee measurement of m_t > 173.7 GeV would strongly disfavor the model (Bayes factor ≤ 0.03).

Conclusion: The Standard Model alone, in a compact T³ geometry with antiperiodic boundary conditions, can drive a quantum-gravitational turnaround acting as a beginning—not a bounce—of spacetime; however, its viability is tightly constrained by particle physics parameters (especially top mass and Higgs self-coupling) and CMB topology.

Abstract: We study a flat three-torus universe whose contraction is turned around by the negative Casimir energy of Standard Model fields. The trace anomaly forbids conformal matter alone to turn an anisotropic universe around, so matter with $w<1/3$, most cheaply a positive potential, must supply the negative trace; with antiperiodic fermions the Standard Model supplies the negative energy, and the universe turns at an edge of 722 reduced Planck lengths. There the Higgs quartic coupling is negative, but zero-point fluctuations of the constant gauge modes confine the Higgs zero mode down to a finite-volume threshold $\lambda_c\approx-0.012$, below the Standard Model value $-0.0098$. The Higgs survives with probability above $1/2$ for a top-quark pole mass below $172.96$ GeV ($\bar m_t<163.16$ GeV; $\alpha_s(M_Z)=0.1180$), and with probability $\approx0.7$ for current data, given a curvature coupling $\xi_H\gtrsim0.08$ at the scale $\bar M_{\mathrm{P}}/722$. The turning point can hold only about five quanta of the lowest modes, whereas a reheating coupling in a time-symmetric past would produce radiation exceeding the Casimir energy by at least $10^{94}$, so we read the turn as a beginning rather than a bounce. Negative Casimir energy cannot hold the shape of the torus: the shape state can be real at most once, and for a real Gaussian state survival of the shape on both branches with $90\%$ probability requires that moment to lie within $0.35$ Hubble times of the turn. The natural state, real at the turn, freezes the torus into a nearly rectangular box whose sides typically differ by $16$-$18\%$. The microwave background can read this fossil only if the shortest cycle lies between $0.985$ and about $1.04$ last-scattering diameters. A top-quark pole mass above $173.7$ GeV, within reach of FCC-ee, would exclude this beginning (Bayes factor $\lesssim0.03$).

</details>


### [59] [Astrophysical Signatures of Charged Anisotropic Compact Star in f(R,T) Gravity via Minimal Geometric Deformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23993)
*Hira Shadab,M. Sharif,Tayyab Naseer*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs two new anisotropic, charged stellar solutions in $f(R,T)$ gravity using minimal geometric deformation (MGD), couples two fluid sources gravitationally, matches interior solutions to Reissner-Nordström exterior, and validates both models against observational data of LMC X-4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generate physically viable, charged anisotropic compact star solutions in modified $f(R,T)$ gravity—beyond standard GR—by extending known isotropic or uncharged seeds via minimal geometric deformation.

Method: Applies minimal geometric deformation (MGD) to a static, spherically symmetric anisotropic fluid seed in $f(R,T)$ gravity; introduces a second gravitationally coupled fluid source; solves the resulting Einstein-Maxwell field equations after radial decoupling into two independent subsystems; imposes continuity of metric potentials and extrinsic curvature at the stellar surface (matching to Reissner-Nordström spacetime); fixes integration constants using observed mass and radius of LMC X-4.

Result: Two novel interior anisotropic, charged stellar solutions in $f(R,T)$ gravity; both satisfy energy conditions, hydrostatic equilibrium (TOV equation), causality (subluminal sound speeds), and stability (via adiabatic index > 4/3); validated quantitatively against LMC X-4.

Conclusion: MGD is an effective technique for generating physically acceptable, charged anisotropic compact star models in $f(R,T)$ gravity; the resulting solutions are consistent with observational constraints and theoretical viability criteria.

Abstract: This work employs minimal geometric deformation to develop two new anisotropic solutions under the effects of an electromagnetic field in the context of $f(R,T)$ theory. Our investigation begins with a static spherical system with an anisotropic fluid as a seed source. After that, a new source is inserted to the existing fluid distribution and both the fluid distributions are assumed to be gravitationally coupled with each other. After computing the Einstein-Maxwell field equations, a radial transformation is applied which results in two different sets of equations. These systems are solved individually under different constraints, generating new solutions. To obtain the constants in our interior solutions, we match the internal geometry with the external Reissner-Nordström spacetime at the surface. We further validate our models by using the known mass and radius of a star LMC X-4. Our investigation confirms that both relativistic stellar models satisfy all the physical requirements for the specified decoupling parametric values.

</details>


### [60] [Dressing a naked singularity with zero-point length](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24000)
*Kimet Jusufi,Francisco S. N. Lobo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper regularizes the central density cusp of the Joshi–Malafarina–Narayan spacetime using a zero-point-length-motivated kernel, yielding two physically distinct regular, horizonless models; it analyzes geodesic completeness, optical delays, light rings, scalar operator positivity, and trapped-mode leakage, distinguishing geometric universality from pressure-closure dependence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically viable, regular, horizonless alternatives to black hole singularities by smoothing the central density cusp in the Joshi–Malafarina–Narayan spacetime—motivated by quantum gravity hints (e.g., zero-point length)—while preserving key geometric and causal features and avoiding event horizons.

Method: Convolution with a normalized kernel inspired by zero-point length; analytical computation of convolved density and enclosed mass for unbounded and truncated cusps; junction condition matching to Schwarzschild exterior; compactness analysis for horizon avoidance; computation of central lapse, optical delay, light-ring location, and spectral properties of the massless scalar wave operator; comparison of pressure closures to isolate geometric vs. closure-dependent behavior.

Result: Two regular, horizonless interior solutions: (i) truncated cusp yields finite mass and smooth boundary; (ii) convolved finite cloud preserves total mass and yields smooth matter tail with guaranteed horizon avoidance under compactness bound. Geodesics are causally complete despite extreme optical delays; uniform optical-delay formula holds across critical compactness; stable interior light ring exists for ultracompact configurations; scalar operator is nonnegative; semiclassical trapped modes show strongly suppressed leakage; delay exponents depend on pressure closure, while core geometry (e.g., light ring, lapse, completeness) is robust.

Conclusion: The construction provides well-behaved, static, effective sources that evade singularities and horizons without invoking microscopic matter physics or dynamical collapse resolution; it identifies universal geometric signatures (e.g., light ring, completeness, operator positivity) separable from model-specific closure assumptions, offering testable phenomenology—especially in extreme redshift/delay regimes—though photon propagation in the Planck-scale core requires beyond-classical physics.

Abstract: We regularize the central density cusp of the Joshi--Malafarina--Narayan spacetime using a normalized kernel motivated by a zero-point length. For an unbounded cusp, the convolution and enclosed mass are obtained analytically; truncating this density gives a regular, horizonless tangential-pressure interior matched to Schwarzschild. Convolving the finite cloud instead preserves its total mass and yields a smooth matter tail. A compactness bound establishes horizon avoidance for this second prescription. We determine the central lapse, establish causal geodesic completeness despite extreme optical delays, derive a uniform optical-delay formula across the critical compactness, and identify the stable interior light ring of ultracompact configurations. The massless scalar probe operator is nonnegative, while semiclassical trapped modes exhibit strongly suppressed leakage. An explicit pressure-closure comparison separates robust geometric properties from closure-dependent delay exponents. Planck-scale examples illustrate extreme redshifts and delays, but their photon interpretation requires physics beyond classical propagation in the core. The construction specifies an effective static source, rather than a microscopic matter theory or a dynamical resolution of collapse.

</details>


### [61] [Canonical currents without improvements: the QCD example](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24040)
*Tomi Koivisto*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces General Parallel Relativity (GPR) to define unambiguous, conserved energy-momentum and angular-momentum currents in both flat and curved spacetimes by using an independent parallel frame; it derives gauge-invariant results for QCD—including the Belinfante-Rosenfeld relation and spin surface term—from a variational principle, and demonstrates exact solutions with conserved energy/rotation charges even without spacetime symmetries, revealing helicity-dependent rotation charge separation invisible to the symmetric energy-momentum tensor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in conventional relativistic field theory where energy-momentum and angular-momentum currents depend on improvement terms in flat spacetime and are only conserved along metric symmetries in curved spacetime.

Method: Formulates General Parallel Relativity (GPR) using canonical Noether currents defined relative to an independent parallel frame; applies variational principle to QCD to derive gauge-invariant stress-energy and spin tensors, Belinfante-Rosenfeld identity, and spin surface term.

Result: Demonstrates exact coupled solutions with strictly conserved energy and rotation charges despite absence of Killing symmetries; shows quarks of opposite helicity—indistinguishable via metric or symmetric energy-momentum tensor—carry opposite rotation charges.

Conclusion: GPR provides a geometrically robust, symmetry-free framework for defining and conserving fundamental currents; reveals physically distinct rotational degrees of freedom tied to helicity, inaccessible to standard symmetric tensor formalisms.

Abstract: In conventional formulations of relativistic field theory, energy-momentum and angular-momentum currents are defined only up to improvement terms in flat spacetime, and in curved spacetime they are conserved only along symmetries of the metric. When defined as canonical Noether currents relative to an independent parallel frame, as in General Parallel Relativity, they are both unambiguous and conserved. For QCD, the gauge-invariant tensor, the Belinfante-Rosenfeld relation and the spin surface term follow from the variational principle. An exact coupled solution is shown to have conserved energy and rotation charges without the corresponding symmetries. Quarks of opposite helicity are indistinguishable by the metric and the symmetric energy-momentum tensor, yet carry opposite rotation charges.

</details>


### [62] [Effect of induced frame dragging on energy extraction from axially symmetric magnetized Reissner-Nordström BH and observational benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24103)
*Ikhtiyor Eshtursunov,Chen Zhou,Sanjar Shaymatov,Pankaj Sheoran,Chengxun Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how induced frame dragging in magnetized Reissner-Nordström black holes (MRNBHs) enables magnetic reconnection–driven energy extraction, finding that extraction efficiency scales with BH charge Q and magnetic field B, reaching Kerr-like levels for large B; predicted powers vastly exceed observed values for M87* and Sgr A*.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether electromagnetic induction—rather than rotation—can generate frame-dragging effects sufficient to support efficient energy extraction via magnetic reconnection near charged, non-rotating black holes.

Method: Analytical study of axially symmetric MRNBH spacetime: derivation and comparison of geometric vs. induced ergospheres; identification of negative-energy particle regions; computation of plasma outflow energies and reconnection efficiency as functions of Q and B; direct estimation of asymptotic reconnection power and comparison with observational luminosities of M87* and Sgr A*.

Result: Induced ergosphere is particle-charge-dependent and distinct from the geometric ergosphere; energy extraction via magnetic reconnection is possible within specific (Q,B)-dependent regions; reconnection efficiency increases monotonically with Q and B, approaching extremal Kerr efficiency for large B; predicted extraction power P_ext ~ 10^53–10^57 erg/s exceeds observed jet powers of M87* and Sgr A* by many orders of magnitude for Q = M and |B|M ≳ 0.2.

Conclusion: Electromagnetically induced frame dragging in charged black holes can support energy extraction efficiencies comparable to those of rotating (Kerr) black holes, but the required magnetic fields imply unrealistically high reconnection powers relative to current astrophysical observations—suggesting either sub-dominant role of charge or need for refined models incorporating radiative losses, turbulence, or field geometry.

Abstract: In this paper, we probe the effect of induced frame dragging on the energy extraction process via magnetic reconnection in axially symmetric magnetized Reissner-Nordström black hole (MRNBH) spacetime and show the influence of BH charge $Q$ and the magnetic field $B$ parameters, respectively. We start by analyzing the metric and present the distinction between the geometrical and induced ergospheres. We argue that the induced ergosphere, unlike the geometric ergosphere, is not a property of the spacetime metric but rather a particle-dependent negative energy region that changes as the charge on the particle varies. We find the region where magnetic reconnection can extract energy from the axially symmetric MRNBH and determine the conditions required for this process. We further examine the energies of the accelerated and decelerated plasma outflows and the corresponding magnetic reconnection efficiency. We show that within the parameter ranges considered, the extraction efficiency increases with the MRNBH charge $Q$ and the magnetic field parameter $B$. For sufficiently large $B$, it reaches the efficiency comparable to that of an extremal Kerr BH case. Our results therefore show that electromagnetically induced frame dragging can support energy extraction efficiencies similar to those associated with rotation parameter. To connect with observations, we evaluate the reconnection power directly from the decelerated-plasma energy at infinity and compare it with the observed power scales of M87* and Sgr~A*. For $Q=M$, reconnection extraction appears in our scan only for $|B|M\gtrsim0.2$ ($B\sim10^{9}\,\mathrm{G}$ for M87*, $\sim10^{12}\,\mathrm{G}$ for Sgr~A*), where $P_{\mathrm{ext}}\sim10^{53}$--$10^{57}\,\mathrm{erg\,s^{-1}}$ exceeds the observed power scales by many orders of magnitude.

</details>


### [63] [How accurate is Spectral Einstein Code (SpEC)? And what limits its accuracy?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24283)
*Himanshu Chaudhary,Mark A. Scheel,Robert Owen,Saul A. Teukolsky,Michael Boyle,Lawrence E. Kidder,Harald P. Pfeiffer*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes error sources in binary black hole (BBH) simulations using the Spectral Einstein Code (SpEC), focusing on the inspiral phase; it identifies constraint violations, waveform errors, floating-point inaccuracies, and multiscale variable imbalances as key accuracy limits, and proposes targeted parameter/resolution adjustments—while noting that overcoming fundamental limits requires major code and formulation changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The improved sensitivity of next-generation gravitational-wave detectors demands higher-accuracy BBH simulations; however, their computational cost necessitates a thorough understanding of error sources to allocate resources efficiently.

Method: The authors perform systematic numerical experiments with SpEC, varying simulation parameters and spatial resolution across subdomains, and quantify errors via constraint violation norms and gravitational waveform differences—particularly during the inspiral phase.

Result: Targeted resolution increases and parameter tuning reduce constraint violations and waveform errors; however, at high resolutions, floating-point roundoff errors and severe scale disparities among evolved variables emerge as dominant, previously underappreciated accuracy bottlenecks.

Conclusion: Current SpEC accuracy is limited not only by discretization but increasingly by floating-point arithmetic and variable scaling; achieving further gains requires reformulating Einstein’s equations and redesigning core numerical infrastructure—insights broadly applicable to other numerical relativity codes.

Abstract: The improved sensitivity of upcoming gravitational-wave detectors will require correspondingly more accurate binary black hole (BBH) simulations. Given the high cost of such simulations, it is essential to understand their sources of error and use computational resources efficiently. We study sources of error in BBH simulations performed with the Spectral Einstein Code (SpEC) and seek to understand what limits their accuracy. In particular, we focus on the inspiral phase because it is the longest-running part of the simulation and accounts for the majority of the waveform error. We find that targeted changes to simulation parameters and resolution in different parts of the computational domain reduce constraint violations and gravitational waveform differences. We also discuss more fundamental limitations on our accuracy that become important at higher resolutions: floating-point errors and large differences in the scales of the fundamental variables. Addressing these limitations will require substantial changes to both the SpEC code and the formulation it uses to evolve Einstein's equations. Many of the improvements and changes discussed in this paper are likely to be relevant to other numerical relativity codes.

</details>


### [64] [Scalar fields in the background of a linear Vaidya spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24909)
*Arielle Schutz,Ratindranath Akhoury*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how black hole evaporation, modeled by a linearly decaying mass in the ingoing Vaidya metric, modifies vacuum entanglement near the horizon—specifically altering the thermofield-double structure via a horizon-adiabatic phase shift—and quantifies the resulting reduction in nonlocal correlations using the CHSH inequality; it further extends the analysis to collapse-formed Vaidya black holes, finding a modified thermal factor instead of eternal-black-hole convergence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dynamical black hole evaporation affects quantum vacuum entanglement near the horizon—beyond the static eternal black hole paradigm—and to quantify observable consequences of horizon dynamics on nonlocal quantum correlations.

Method: Semiclassical quasi-stationary approximation applied to massless scalar fields in ingoing linear Vaidya spacetime; conformal equivalence to eternal black hole in near-horizon limit enables modular-theoretic and Bogoliubov-based analysis; CHSH inequality used as quantitative probe of entanglement change; Wald’s collapse-adapted framework extended to incorporate mass loss.

Result: Evaporation induces a horizon-adiabatic phase in the thermofield-double vacuum, reducing CHSH correlation by a factor dependent on evaporation rate; for collapse-formed linear Vaidya black holes, near-horizon state does not approach the eternal black hole vacuum but exhibits a modified thermal weight reflecting mass loss.

Conclusion: Black hole evaporation fundamentally alters the structure of vacuum entanglement near the horizon—not merely thermally, but coherently via geometric phase—yielding testable reductions in Bell-type correlations; this implies that semiclassical unitarity and information recovery must account for such adiabatic vacuum reconfiguration.

Abstract: We study, in a semiclassical, quasi-stationary approximation, the entanglement properties of massless scalar fields near the event horizon of a dynamical black hole modeled by an ingoing Vaidya metric with a linearly decaying mass. In the near-horizon limit, the geometry becomes conformally equivalent to an eternal black hole, allowing the use of modular theory to analyze vacuum entanglement. We first study the modifications to the entanglement across the bifurcation surface of such a black hole from that of an eternal one. The conformal factor accounting for the evaporation modifies the Bogoliubov transformation, inducing a phase in the thermofield-double-like structure of the global vacuum. This phase change of the vacuum state is a consequence of the adiabatic shift of the horizon. A quantitative measure of the entanglement change due to the phase is obtained through the Clauser-Horne-Shimony-Holt (CHSH) inequality. We compare the CHSH inequalities in the vacuum states for the static and the linear Vaidya black holes and find that the CHSH correlation quantity for the latter is decreased by a factor depending on the evaporation rate. Next, we discuss the changes that must be considered for the linear Vaidya black hole formed from gravitational collapse. For this purpose we adapt an analysis by Wald for the case of the Schwarzschild black hole. We find, as expected, the near-horizon structure and state do not approach that of the eternal black hole. Rather, the thermal factor is modified by a factor incorporating the change in the black hole mass due to evaporation. The entangled global vacuum state again has a thermofield double structure with the modification mentioned above.

</details>


### [65] [Interferometric trilemma for ultraviolet-softened gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25994)
*Hiroki Matsui*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines how ultraviolet-softened extensions of general relativity weaken gravity at short distances, showing that such weakening necessarily violates one of three interferometric consistency conditions—visibility amplification, decoherence-energy matching, or causal response—each linked to a specific ghost prescription in quadratic gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between UV-softened gravity (which weakens gravity at short distances) and the Källén--Lehmann spectral density constraint (which forbids gravity from falling below Newtonian strength), by identifying the fundamental mediator property sacrificed in such models.

Method: Analyzes Lorentz-invariant Gaussian mediators linearly coupled to the conserved stress tensor; derives three independent interferometric conditions (visibility non-amplification, decoherence fixed by radiated energy from difference source, causal response); tests them against tree-level ghost prescriptions in quadratic gravity (negative-norm, negative-energy, and fakeon ghosts).

Result: Each ghost prescription violates exactly one of the three interferometric conditions: negative-norm ghosts amplify visibility, negative-energy ghosts cause decoherence without corresponding positive energy transfer, and fakeons produce advanced (acausal) fringe shifts. Thus, any universal short-distance weakening of gravity implies violation of at least one condition.

Conclusion: Short-distance weakening of gravity certifies that its mediator violates a fundamental interferometric consistency condition—implying that proposed quantum gravity observables (e.g., in gravitational interferometry) serve not as direct evidence but as consistency tests of the theory’s UV completion.

Abstract: Ultraviolet-softened extensions of general relativity can weaken gravity at short distances, whereas a positive Källén--Lehmann spectral density forbids gravity from falling below Newtonian strength. Which property of the mediator must such a weakening sacrifice? For Lorentz-invariant Gaussian mediators coupled linearly to the conserved stress tensor, we formulate three independent interferometric conditions that jointly imply this bound: visibility is never amplified, decoherence is fixed by the energy radiated by the difference source, and no interferometer responds to a later one. At tree level, the three ghost prescriptions of quadratic gravity each violate exactly one: negative-norm ghosts amplify coherence, negative-energy ghosts decohere without positive energy transfer from the difference source, and a time-symmetric fakeon response produces advanced fringe shifts. Any universal short-distance weakening of gravity therefore certifies that its mediator violates one of these conditions, and the observables proposed to certify quantum gravity become consistency tests of its ultraviolet completion.

</details>


### [66] [Black hole free energy landscapes from constrained instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26094)
*Shan-Ping Wu,Jia-Zhou Liu,Yu-Xiao Liu,Shao-Wen Wei*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses gravitational path integral factorization to give a semiclassical interpretation of generalized black hole free energy landscapes at fixed temperature and angular velocity, deriving a constrained saddle action matching the thermodynamic free energy in both Einstein and higher-curvature (e.g., $f(	ext{Riemann})$) gravity theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a semiclassical gravitational path integral foundation for black hole thermodynamics—specifically, the generalized free energy landscape—at fixed external temperature and angular velocities, beyond standard Euclidean methods and including higher-curvature theories.

Method: Applies gravitational path integral factorization with boundary integral transforms over constrained sectors labeled by partial-Neumann data on an auxiliary hypersurface; identifies paired regular subregion solutions as constrained instantons via independent variation; introduces Lagrange multipliers to enforce thermal and rotational matching across interfaces; extends to local parity-even $f(\text{Riemann})$ theories without curvature derivatives by preserving full auxiliary interface data.

Result: Derives constrained saddle action $I_{\rm constr} = \beta \mathcal{F}_{\rm gen} = \beta(E_{\mathcal{B}} - \Omega_{\mathcal{B}}^I J_{\mathcal{B},I}) - S$, where $S$ is Wald entropy (horizon cap contribution), valid for nonextremal stationary black holes with asymptotic boundaries; verified explicitly for Schwarzschild, Kerr, 5D AdS Einstein–Gauss–Bonnet, and rotating BTZ in new massive gravity.

Conclusion: The constrained gravitational path integral yields a geometric, semiclassical realization of black hole free energy landscapes in both Einstein and higher-curvature gravities, unifying thermodynamic potentials with constrained instanton geometry without relying on small-deficit or perturbative expansions.

Abstract: We apply gravitational path integral factorization to provide a semiclassical interpretation of generalized black hole free energy landscapes at fixed external temperature and angular velocities. Boundary integral transforms express the formal partition function as an integral over constrained sectors labeled by complete local partial-Neumann data on an auxiliary hypersurface. Independent variation establishes paired regular subregion solutions as constrained instantons, with Lagrange multipliers supporting lapse and tangential shift jumps that accommodate thermal and rotational mismatches. For selected nonextremal stationary families with an asymptotic boundary, the constrained saddle action takes the form $I_{\rm constr}=\beta\mathcal F_{\rm gen}=\beta(E_{\mathcal B}-\Omega_{\mathcal B}^{I}J_{\mathcal B,I})-S$, with $\beta=T^{-1}$. Here $E_{\mathcal B}$ and $J_{\mathcal B,I}$ are renormalized Hamiltonian charges and $S$ is the black hole entropy, whose contribution to the action comes from the regular horizon cap. Interior stationary points of the landscape correspond to smooth equilibrium saddles when the retained variations independently span the entropy and angular momentum directions. We extend the construction to local, parity-even $f(\mathrm{Riemann})$ theories without curvature derivatives by retaining the complete auxiliary interface data. The same thermodynamic form follows with Wald entropy, without a small-deficit expansion. Schwarzschild and Kerr black holes illustrate the Einstein construction, while five-dimensional static AdS black holes in Einstein--Gauss--Bonnet gravity and the rotating BTZ branch of new massive gravity provide explicit checks of the higher-curvature extension.

</details>


### [67] [Construction of Causal Set Propagators in $2+1$-Dimensional Constant-Curvature Spacetimes from Two-Link Paths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26221)
*Arsim Kastrati*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a retarded scalar propagator on causal sets in 2+1D Minkowski, AdS, and dS spacetimes using the squared link matrix L²; it recovers continuum Klein-Gordon propagators for a massless scalar with effective curvature coupling ξ_link = 1/6 — not the conformal value 1/8 — suggesting a UV feature tied to causal set link structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a physically meaningful, discrete, link-based retarded propagator on causal sets that reproduces correct continuum behavior across curved spacetimes, and to understand the origin of the emergent curvature coupling.

Method: Defines a propagator from the expectation value of the squared link matrix L² on sprinklings into 2+1D Minkowski, AdS, and dS; analyzes its high-density limit via localization near the intersection of null boundaries of the Alexandrov interval; compares resulting kernels to continuum Klein–Gordon propagators with general curvature coupling ξ.

Result: The L²-based propagator reproduces the exact retarded Klein–Gordon propagator for a massless scalar field with effective coupling ξ_link = 1/6 in all three spacetimes; the flat-space normalization is preserved under constant curvature; ξ_link = 1/6 differs from the 2+1D conformal coupling ξ_conf = 1/8, and matches values previously found in 1+1D and 3+1D, indicating dimensional independence.

Conclusion: The persistent emergence of ξ_link = 1/6 across dimensions signals an intrinsic, ultraviolet property of the causal set link structure — possibly related to the (1/6 − ξ)ℛ term in heat-kernel expansions — rather than a low-energy conformal or geometric artifact.

Abstract: We study a link-based retarded scalar propagator on causal sets sprinkled into $2+1$-dimensional Minkowski, anti-de Sitter, and de Sitter spacetimes. The construction is based on the squared link matrix $\mathbf{L}^2$, whose expectation value counts two-link paths between fixed endpoints. In the high-density limit, the dominant contribution localizes near the intersection of the null boundaries of the Alexandrov interval, and the flat-space normalization remains unchanged in constant curvature. The resulting kernels agree with the corresponding continuum Klein-Gordon propagators for a nominally massless scalar with effective curvature coupling $\xi_{\rm link}=1/6$. Since the conformal coupling in $2+1$ spacetime dimensions is $1/8$, this value is not a conformal-coupling effect. Together with link-based results in $1+1$ and $3+1$ dimensions, where the same coefficient $1/6$ appears, this suggests that the coupling may reflect an ultraviolet property of the link structure, reminiscent of the combination $(1/6-\xi)\mathcal{R}$ in local heat-kernel expansions.

</details>


### [68] [Geodesics of the five-dimensional Kerr-(A)dS black hole in closed form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26242)
*Özgür Sarıoğlu,Enes Yılmaz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides a general closed-form solution to the Hamilton-Jacobi equation for geodesic motion in the five-dimensional Kerr-(A)dS black hole, unifying treatment across all root structures of radial and polar quartic polynomials, using Biermann–Weierstrass theory; coordinates are expressed manifestly real in terms of an arbitrary orbit point, and temporal/azimuthal evolution reduces to elliptic integrals of the third kind.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work solved geodesic equations case-by-case based on root structure of radial and polar polynomials; a unified, closed-form solution valid for all cases (timelike/null, arbitrary root configurations) was lacking.

Method: Leverages the Biermann–Weierstrass theorem for quartic polynomials in dimensionless variables to express radial and polar coordinates as manifestly real functions parametrized by an arbitrary orbit point; time and azimuthal angles obtained via quadratures reduced exclusively to elliptic integrals of the third kind; affine parameter similarly derived.

Result: General closed-form solution for all geodesics (timelike and null) in 5D Kerr-(A)dS spacetime; explicit real expressions for r(λ), θ(λ), t(λ), φ(λ), ψ(λ), and affine parameter λ; correction of previously published inverse metric and radial potential.

Conclusion: The geodesic problem in 5D Kerr-(A)dS is fully solvable in closed form using elliptic integrals of the third kind only, independent of polynomial root structure—resolving a long-standing gap in exact integrability of higher-dimensional rotating black hole spacetimes.

Abstract: The Hamilton-Jacobi equation for geodesic motion in the five-dimensional Kerr-(A)dS black hole separates in Boyer-Lindquist coordinates, but the resulting first-order system has so far been integrated case by case, according to the real roots of the radial and the polar polynomial. We give the general solution in closed form. In dimensionless variables both polynomials are quartic, and a theorem of Biermann and Weierstrass then yields the radial and the polar coordinate as one pair of manifestly real formulas, parametrized by an arbitrary point of the orbit rather than by a turning point, and valid for timelike and null geodesics alike whatever the root structure. The time coordinate and the two azimuthal angles follow by quadratures that reduce to elliptic integrals of the third kind alone, and the affine parameter by one more of the same type. Along the way we correct the inverse metric and the radial potential as published.

</details>


### [69] [Cosmological perturbations from localized sources: a gauge-invariant multipolar theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26623)
*Geoffrey Compère,Sk Jahanur Hoque*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a first-principles, gauge-invariant multipolar formalism for linearized gravitational fields of localized sources in general FLRW cosmologies—including ΛCDM—without slow-motion or geometric-optics approximations; it introduces canonical mass, current, and cosmic fluid moments, accounts for curvature tails, and unifies with cosmological perturbation theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Blanchet-Damour multipolar formalism to generic spatially flat FLRW universes (beyond Minkowski and de Sitter) and provide a rigorous, gauge-invariant, fully retarded description of gravitational radiation and fluid coupling that incorporates cosmological curvature effects—including memory-like 'curvature tails'—without relying on approximations like slow motion or geometric optics.

Method: Constructs the full retarded linearized metric and induced cosmic fluid perturbation from prescribed source stress-energy and a cosmologically stationary past field; generalizes Blanchet-Damour formalism to define gauge-invariant mass, current, and a new canonical scalar 'cosmic fluid' moment; performs analysis directly in position space (avoiding nonlocal interior projections); relates canonical multipole metric to standard cosmological perturbation variables (e.g., Φ, Ψ, v_i); defines effective multipolar sources via Cartesian moment truncation for sub-Hubble extended sources.

Result: Derives analytical expressions for gravitational propagation in ΛCDM (including radiation era); recovers known Minkowski and de Sitter multipolar fields as limits; shows the cosmic fluid moment vanishes in Minkowski and pure de Sitter but curvature tails persist in de Sitter; establishes that the formalism includes all multipole orders and intrinsically encodes causal dependence on source history within the light cone.

Conclusion: A complete, gauge-invariant, first-principles multipolar framework is achieved for linearized gravity in arbitrary smooth FLRW backgrounds, enabling precise modeling of gravitational wave emission and backreaction in realistic cosmologies—bridging post-Newtonian methods, cosmological perturbation theory, and observational GW astrophysics.

Abstract: We develop a first-principles description of the linearized gravitational field of localized sources in spatially flat Friedmann-Lema\^ıtre-Robertson-Walker universes with arbitrary smooth expansion histories. The construction determines the complete retarded metric and the induced perturbation of the surrounding cosmic fluid from prescribed source stresses and a cosmologically stationary past field. It includes all multipole orders and accounts for curvature tails, through which the field depends on the source's past history inside the light cone. Generalizing the Blanchet-Damour multipolar formalism, we identify gauge-invariant mass and current moments together with a canonical cosmic fluid moment that determines an independent scalar response. This response vanishes in Minkowski and pure de Sitter spacetime, although curvature tails persist in de Sitter. The gauge-invariant multipoles are obtained directly in position space. This gives an exterior description without the nonlocal spatial projection through the source interior required by the usual scalar-vector-tensor decomposition. We also relate the canonical multipole metric to the standard variables of cosmological perturbation theory. The retarded construction requires neither a slow-motion nor a geometric-optics approximation. We define effective multipolar sources by truncating the moments of slowly varying sub-Hubble extended sources at a prescribed Cartesian multipole order, recovering the earlier quadrupolar and octupolar models in de Sitter spacetime. We recover the known Minkowski and de Sitter multipolar fields and obtain an analytical representation for gravitational propagation in the $\Lambda$CDM model including radiation.

</details>


### [70] [A new class of rotating black hole solutions endowed with a scalar hair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26678)
*Hugo Candan,Philippe Grandclement*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs new rotating, hairy black hole solutions in a Horndeski scalar-tensor theory without curvature coupling; solutions are obtained numerically (Kadath/spectral methods), feature a primary scalar hair from linear time dependence, possess a Killing horizon but violate circularity and the zeroth law of black hole thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct stationary, axisymmetric black hole solutions with scalar hair in Horndeski gravity—specifically in the subclass without non-minimal curvature coupling—where analytical Kerr-like ansätze fail and numerical techniques are required to explore deviations from GR predictions.

Method: Numerical construction using the Kadath spectral library; employs coordinate-based, ansatz-free metric representation; solves full coupled Einstein-scalar field equations for rotating configurations with linear-in-time scalar field dependence.

Result: New family of stationary, axisymmetric, hairy black hole solutions with a primary scalar hair parameter; solutions possess a Killing horizon but are non-circular and violate the zeroth law of black hole thermodynamics; metric deviates from Kerr generically.

Conclusion: Primary scalar hair arising from linear time dependence yields physically distinct, thermodynamically anomalous black holes in Horndeski gravity without curvature coupling—demonstrating that hairy black holes can evade no-hair theorems while retaining key geometric features like Killing horizons.

Abstract: We present new hairy stationary and axisymmetric black hole solutions. These solutions belong to a subclass of Horndeski scalar-tensor theories, in which there is no non-minimal coupling to curvature. The full rotating solutions are obtained numerically using the Kadath library based on spectral methods. The metric does not rely on an explicit ansatz but rather on the use of a set of numerical coordinates. Black holes obtained differ from Kerr in general, and depend on a new independent parameter, a primary hair, coming from a linear time dependence in the scalar field. These solutions admit a Killing horizon like the Kerr black hole, but are non-circular, and break the zeroth law of black hole thermodynamics.

</details>


### [71] [Observational constraints on scalar-nonmetricity cosmology: the role of the connection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24402)
*Ghulam Murtaza,Avik De,Emmanuel N. Saridakis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares three symmetric-teleparallel connection branches in scalar-nonmetricity gravity using cosmological data (SNe Ia, BAO, CMB); Connection I is statistically favored over ΛCDM under AIC, while all branches support cosmic acceleration but differ in observational sensitivity and reconstruction stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the three inequivalent symmetric-teleparallel connection branches in scalar-nonmetricity gravity—allowed even for spatially flat FLRW geometry—yield distinguishable observational signatures in late-time cosmology.

Method: Systematic Bayesian parameter inference and model comparison across all three connection branches, assuming a steep scalar-field potential and power-law nonminimal coupling; joint analysis of Pantheon+ & DES Y5 SNe Ia, DESI DR2 BAO, and compressed CMB data.

Result: All branches reproduce matter-to-dark-energy transition; Connection I shows strongest SNe dependence and best fit (Δχ²_min = −13.99, ΔAIC = −5.99 vs ΛCDM); Connection II yields most stable reconstructed expansion histories; Connection III is intermediate.

Conclusion: The geometrical freedom in scalar-nonmetricity gravity has observable consequences: Connection I is preferred by current data under AIC, demonstrating that connection choice meaningfully impacts phenomenology and statistical viability.

Abstract: Scalar-nonmetricity gravity provides a natural scalar-tensor extension of symmetric teleparallel gravity, with the gravitational interaction encoded in nonmetricity. A distinctive feature is that, even for a spatially flat Friedmann-Robertson-Walker geometry, three inequivalent symmetric-teleparallel connection branches are allowed. Whether this geometrical freedom has observable consequences is therefore an important question. In this work we perform a systematic observational comparison of all three connection branches, considering a steep scalar-field potential and a power-law nonminimal coupling. We constrain the models using combinations of Pantheon Plus and DES Year 5 Type Ia supernovae, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations, and compressed cosmic microwave background information. We find that all three branches can reproduce the transition from matter domination to late-time acceleration, but their observational behavior is connection dependent. Connection I exhibits the strongest sensitivity to the supernova compilation, Connection II yields remarkably stable reconstructed histories, while Connection III shows an intermediate behavior. Most notably, for DES Y5+CMB+DESI DR2, Connection I gives $\Delta\chi^2_{\min}=-13.99$ and $\Delta{\rm AIC}=-5.99$ relative to $\Lambda$CDM, and is therefore favored by the Akaike information criterion.

</details>


### [72] [Static tidal Love numbers of Schwarzschild and non-Schwarzschild black holes in quadratic gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24625)
*Soham Bhattacharyya*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the static electric quadrupolar tidal response (Love numbers) of black holes in 4D quadratic gravity (R − αC² + βR²), finding that while Schwarzschild black holes have vanishing Love numbers, non-Schwarzschild solutions exhibit nonzero negative values; the analysis uses Kodama–Ishibashi gauge-invariant perturbations, numerical shooting for background solutions, and intermediate-radius matching for tidal solutions, with coupling among three radial equations preventing simple separation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether black holes in higher-curvature gravity (specifically quadratic gravity) exhibit nonzero static electric quadrupolar Love numbers—testing the 'no-hair' and rigidity implications beyond general relativity—and to resolve apparent tension between full-theory results and low-energy effective theory expectations.

Method: Numerical shooting to construct non-Schwarzschild black hole backgrounds; gauge-invariant perturbation theory in Kodama–Ishibashi formalism; reduction of static electric quadrupole sector to three coupled second-order radial ODEs after excluding scalar-curvature modes via horizon regularity and absence of scalar tide; selection of physical tidal solution via matching at an intermediate radius; definition and numerical extraction of curvature amplitude ratios to compute λ_E and dimensionless tidal deformability k₂^(r_h).

Result: Schwarzschild black holes in this theory have zero massless gravitational Love number; a numerically obtained non-Schwarzschild black hole solution yields a negative static electric quadrupolar response: λ_E/r_h⁵ ≈ −0.1224928 and k₂^(r_h) ≈ −0.1837391 at α/r_h² = 1/2, with high-precision convergence (outer-endpoint variation ~1.60×10⁻⁷); the coupling structure explains the negative sign and its suppression near the Schwarzschild limit.

Conclusion: Black holes in quadratic gravity can support nonzero, negative static electric quadrupolar Love numbers when deviating from Schwarzschild geometry—demonstrating finite tidal deformability absent in GR—while remaining consistent with the decoupling of curvature-squared operators in the low-energy EFT limit, as the effect arises from non-perturbative background modification rather than local higher-derivative corrections to linearized dynamics.

Abstract: We calculate the static electric quadrupolar response of black holes in four-dimensional quadratic gravity, with action $R-\alpha C^2+\beta R^2$. The Schwarzschild solution has zero massless gravitational Love number under the stated boundary conditions. A numerical non-Schwarzschild solution instead has a nonzero negative response. At $\alpha/r_h^2=1/2$, we find $\lambda_E/r_h^5\simeq-0.1224928$ and $k_2^{(r_h)}\simeq-0.1837391$, with a final outer-endpoint change in $k_2^{(r_h)}$ of about $1.60\times10^{-7}$. We explain how the background is obtained by shooting and how the tidal solution is selected by matching at an intermediate radius. The perturbations are written in Kodama--Ishibashi gauge-invariant variables. After the scalar-curvature perturbation is excluded by horizon regularity and the absence of an applied scalar tide, the remaining static sector has three coupled second-order radial equations. We define the curvature amplitudes in these equations and describe why simple attempts to separate them fail. The coupling scan shows a negative response approaching zero near the Schwarzschild intersection. We also explain why this full-theory result does not conflict with the removal of curvature-squared operators in a perturbative low-energy expansion.

</details>


### [73] [Radius of the innermost stable circular orbit of black-holes with localized Astrophysical Dark Matter halo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24673)
*Anup S. Karekar,Siba Prasad Das*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how an astrophysical dark matter (ADM) halo affects the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) of massive particles around a static, spherically symmetric black hole; using a regularized halo density profile and stiff-matter equation of state (w=1), it finds that the ADM halo shifts r_ISCO inward relative to Schwarzschild, with larger core radii causing greater shifts — e.g., r_ISCO ≈ 5.97, 5.61, 5.53 for r₀ = 2.0, 10.0, 20.0 — consistent with the theoretical upper bound r_ISCO ≤ 6.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe black-hole 'hair'—deviations from the no-hair theorem—via observable effects of localized astrophysical dark matter halos on geodesic structure, particularly the ISCO radius, which is accessible through high-energy astrophysical observations such as X-ray reflection spectroscopy or quasi-periodic oscillations.

Method: Numerical solution of the Einstein field equations for a static, spherically symmetric black hole coupled to a regularized ADM-halo density profile (with core radius r₀ and exponential cutoff ensuring finite mass and vanishing at the horizon); stiff-matter equation of state (p = ρ); analysis of effective potential and its first/second derivatives to locate r_ISCO for timelike geodesics.

Result: The ADM halo consistently reduces r_ISCO below the Schwarzschild value of 6 (in units of GM/c²); quantitatively, r_ISCO ≈ 5.97, 5.61, 5.53 for halo core radii r₀ = 2.0, 10.0, 20.0 respectively; the shift increases monotonically with r₀, confirming halo-induced deviation from bald black-hole geometry.

Conclusion: The presence of a localized ADM halo constitutes detectable black-hole hair, imprinting characteristic inward shifts in r_ISCO; this provides a theoretically grounded, observationally testable signature for distinguishing hairy black holes from vacuum solutions in astrophysical contexts.

Abstract: We investigate the geodesic structure of a static, spherically symmetric black-hole in the background of a localized Astrophysical Dark Matter (ADM)-halo. The halo is modeled by a core-radius ($r_0$), regularized density profile that vanishes at the event horizon and exponential factor ensures the finite total halo mass. We solved the Einstein field equations numerically with equation of state with $p=w\rho$ for stiff matter $w=1$ scenario. By analyzing the effective potential and its first and second derivatives, we evaluated the characteristic radius of the time-like innermost stable circular orbit ($r_{\text{ISCO}}$) of massive particle in the vicinity of hairy black-hole. Our numerical analysis is consistent with the recently proposed theoretical upper bound $r_{\text{ISCO}} \le 6$ normalized to unit mass black-hole. We showed that the ADM-halo shifts $r_{\text{ISCO}}$ inward with respect to the Schwarzschild (bald) scenario and the shifts are larger for larger halo core-radius. In particular our numerical finding enumerate $r_{\text{ISCO}}$ are approximately $5.97$, $5.61$, $5.53$ for $r_0=2.0,10.0,20.0$ respectively. We believe that our analysis with ADM-halo model shed light on probing black-hole hair through high-energy astrophysical observables.

</details>


### [74] [Weak Cosmic Censorship in Einstein-Born-Infeld Black Hole: A Parameter-Space Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24731)
*M. L. Peñafiel,F. T. Falciano,J. C. Fabris*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel method to test the cosmic censorship conjecture for Einstein-Born-Infeld black holes—circumventing the intractable self-energy calculation due to Born-Infeld nonlinearity—enabling full-parameter-space analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the cosmic censorship conjecture for Einstein-Born-Infeld black holes, which violate Bekenstein's entropy bound and are prone to overcharging, but whose self-energy calculation is hindered by electromagnetic nonlinearities.

Method: Proposes an alternative analytical approach that avoids direct computation of the divergent self-energy of a test particle near the black hole, enabling systematic exploration of the full parameter space of the Einstein-Born-Infeld solution.

Result: Enables comprehensive testing of the cosmic censorship conjecture across the entire parameter space of the Einstein-Born-Infeld black hole without requiring infeasible nonlinear self-field calculations.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides a viable framework to assess cosmic censorship in strongly nonlinear electrodynamics regimes, reinforcing or challenging the conjecture in a previously inaccessible domain.

Abstract: Einstein-Born-Infeld is a charged black hole solution of general relativity with Born-Infeld nonlinear electrodynamics, which has interesting physical features. It violates Bekenstein's entropy upper bound, and it can overcharge with respect to the Reissner-Nordström black hole. It presents an optimum situation to test the cosmic censorship conjecture, which is another important conjecture in black hole physics. In principle, this analysis requires the calculation of the self-energy of a test particle in the vicinity of the black hole, which, in general, is not feasible because of the electromagnetic nonlinearities. In this work, we propose an alternative approach that allows us to explore the whole parameter space of the Einstein-Born-Infeld black hole and test the validity of the cosmic censorship conjecture.

</details>


### [75] [Global isometric embedding of the Kerr spacetime and beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24735)
*Mikhail Markov,Anton Sheykin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper constructs a simple, asymptotically flat isometric embedding of the Kerr spacetime into (5+7)-dimensional flat space, covering all r > 0, and extends the method to general Kerr-Schild-like spacetimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a globally well-behaved, explicit isometric embedding of the full Kerr spacetime—addressing limitations of prior embeddings (e.g., restricted domain, complexity, or non-asymptotic flatness)—and to establish a systematic embedding framework for Kerr-Schild-type geometries.

Method: Constructs an explicit isometric embedding using algebraic and geometric techniques tailored to the Kerr metric's structure; leverages the Kerr-Schild form g_{μν} = η_{μν} + H k_μ k_ν to generalize the embedding construction to any spacetime admitting such a decomposition.

Result: A novel (5+7)D isometric embedding of Kerr that is asymptotically flat, covers the entire r > 0 domain (including ergosphere and event horizon), and admits a compact analytic expression; further proves the existence of analogous embeddings for arbitrary Kerr-Schild spacetimes.

Conclusion: The embedding resolves key geometric and global issues in prior constructions and provides a unifying method for embedding a broad class of physically relevant spacetimes, facilitating studies of extrinsic geometry, quantum gravity models, and holographic interpretations.

Abstract: We construct a new isometric embedding of the Kerr spacetime into $(5+7)$-dimensional flat spacetime. It is asymptotically flat, covers the entire $r>0$ region and has a very simple form. We also show how this embedding can be generalized to any spacetime admitting a Kerr-Schild-like decomposition of the metric.

</details>


### [76] [Bayesian Inference of Primary Hair from Black Hole Shadows in Generalized Proca Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24996)
*Jiao-Zhen She,Shu-Min Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole shadows in a rotating generalized Proca theory with primary vector hair, deriving a one-parameter Kerr deformation; it uses ray tracing and Bayesian inference on EHT data of M87* and Sgr A* to jointly constrain vector hair, spin, and inclination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect optical signatures of primary vector hair—specifically in rotating black hole solutions of generalized Proca theory—to quantitative, horizon-scale observational constraints from EHT shadow measurements.

Method: Analyzes an exact rotating solution of generalized Proca theory; selects a coupling slice yielding a simple noncircular Kerr deformation dependent on spin and latitude; determines regular outer horizon domain; computes shadow and lensing images via numerical ray tracing; applies Bayesian inference using shadow area-equivalent radius and axis ratio on EHT data of M87* and Sgr A*.

Result: Ray-traced shadows show modest enlargement and altered lensing patterns; Bayesian analysis yields joint posterior constraints: M87* prefers low spin with strong spin–hair anticorrelation; Sgr A* provides upper limits on spin and hair, with inclination poorly constrained.

Conclusion: Primary vector hair produces distinctive, measurable shadow deformations; EHT observations already place meaningful joint constraints on vector hair, spin, and inclination—demonstrating the viability of using black hole shadows to test beyond-GR vector-field theories.

Abstract: We investigate black hole shadows and observational constraints on primary vector hair in an exact rotating solution of generalized Proca theory. Selecting a coupling slice yields a simple, one-parameter Kerr deformation that is noncircular and depends on spin and latitude. The deformation vanishes in the nonrotating limit, while the vector field remains nontrivial. We determine the domain admitting a regular outer horizon and construct shadow and lensing images by numerical ray tracing. Ray-traced images show a modest enlargement of the shadow and changes in the surrounding lensing pattern. Bayesian inference using the shadow area-equivalent radius and axis ratio yields joint constraints on hair, spin, and viewing inclination from Event Horizon Telescope observations of M87* and Sgr A*. The M87* posterior favors relatively low spin and exhibits a pronounced spin--hair anticorrelation, while Sgr A* yields upper limits on spin and hair with its inclination remaining essentially unconstrained. These results connect the optical signatures of primary vector hair to quantitative horizon-scale constraints.

</details>


### [77] [Critical limits of accelerating Kerr--NUT spacetimes and the non-expanding Plebański--Demiański family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25031)
*Kenneth Hong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes degenerate-horizon limits of vacuum accelerating Kerr–NUT spacetimes with nonzero NUT charge, identifying a cubic criticality condition (a² = m² + ℓ², αm = 1) under which the near-horizon geometry is locally homothetic to the 'swirling universe'; it embeds extremal, Nariai, and cubic near-horizon metrics into the Plebański–Demiański family via curvature-sector classification and discusses scaling behavior and open questions on global identifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the geometric structure and universality of near-horizon geometries in accelerating, rotating, NUT-charged black holes—particularly at critical parameter regimes where horizons coalesce in nonstandard (cubic) fashion—extending known classifications (e.g., extremal, Nariai) to include NUT and acceleration.

Method: Analytic study of vacuum Kerr–NUT–(A)dS spacetimes with acceleration (C-metric generalization); use of projective coordinate transformations, local homothety analysis, near-horizon limiting procedures, and embedding into the non-expanding Plebański–Demiański family via curvature-based longitudinal sector identification; cubic-adapted near-critical scaling for horizon separation.

Result: At cubic criticality (a² = m² + ℓ², αm = 1), the near-horizon geometry is locally homothetic to the swirling universe; the cubic horizon has one finite axis and a noncompact angular end; extremal, Nariai, and cubic near-horizon metrics map bijectively to negative-, positive-, and zero-curvature sectors of the non-expanding Plebański–Demiański family; Nariai case includes an exceptional endpoint requiring a distinct Killing chart; leading-order metric is insensitive to fixed first-order horizon separations under cubic-adapted scaling.

Conclusion: Cubic criticality defines a new universal near-horizon class beyond extremal and Nariai, governed by local homothety and curvature-sector embedding; however, global regularity and consistent Killing identifications for the full critical parent spacetime remain unresolved.

Abstract: We study degenerate-horizon limits of vacuum accelerating Kerr--NUT spacetimes with finite, nonzero NUT parameter. At cubic criticality, $a^2=m^2+\ell^2$ and $\alpha m=1$, a projective coordinate transformation relates the complete critical parent metrics by a local homothety. The cubic horizon has one finite axis and a noncompact angular end, and its near-horizon limit is locally homothetic to the swirling universe. Explicit four-dimensional coordinate transformations place the ordinary extremal, Nariai and cubic near-horizon metrics in the negative-, positive- and zero-curvature longitudinal sectors of the non-expanding Plebański--Demiański family. The Nariai correspondence includes an exceptional endpoint that requires a separate Killing chart. Under a cubic-adapted near-critical scaling, fixed first-order separations between the approaching horizons do not affect the leading metric. The global Killing identifications and regular completion of the critical parent remain open.

</details>


### [78] [Gravitational lensing effects on neutrino oscillations around a Schwarzschild black hole immersed in perfect fluid dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25053)
*Sojida Mannobova,Farruh Atamurotov,Ahmadjon Abdujabbarov,Phongpichit Channuie,Chengxun Yuan,Chen Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how neutrino flavor oscillations are modified by weak gravitational lensing around a black hole embedded in perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM), deriving covariant oscillation phases and showing that PFDM parameter λ significantly affects interference patterns and flavor transition probabilities—especially in the μ–τ channel—and retains sensitivity to absolute neutrino mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize how perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM) modifies neutrino flavor oscillations in the weak gravitational-lensing regime near a static, spherically symmetric black hole—motivated by the need to probe dark matter properties via high-energy astrophysical neutrinos.

Method: Covariant quantum-mechanical treatment of ultrarelativistic neutrino propagation; derivation of radial and non-radial oscillation phases in PFDM spacetime; construction of two-path coherent flavor-transition probability for two- and three-flavor mixing; parametric analysis of λ-dependence, mass ordering, and flavor-channel sensitivity.

Result: Non-radial oscillation phases acquire λ-dependent corrections; flavor-transition probabilities (especially P_μτ) exhibit strong, non-monotonic sensitivity to λ; path-interference terms encode absolute neutrino mass scale; extrema shifts, amplitude modulation, and contrast changes depend on lens mass, neutrino mass scale, flavor channel, and mass ordering.

Conclusion: PFDM induces distinctive, measurable modifications to lensed neutrino oscillations—providing a novel probe of dark matter microphysics—and lays groundwork for realistic observational modeling including decoherence, detector effects, and trajectory corrections.

Abstract: We investigate neutrino flavor oscillations in the weak gravitational-lensing regime of a static, spherically symmetric black hole surrounded by perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM). Within a covariant quantum-mechanical framework, we derive the oscillation phases for radial and non-radial propagation of ultrarelativistic neutrinos. While the leading radial phase is independent of the metric function, non-radial trajectories acquire impact-parameter-dependent corrections governed by the PFDM parameter $\lambda$. We construct the flavor-transition probability for two coherent lensed paths and analyze the resulting interference in two and three flavor scenarios. Varying $\lambda$ shifts the oscillation extrema and modifies their amplitudes and contrast, with a non-monotonic dependence on the neutrino mass scale, lens mass, flavor channel, and mass ordering. The $P_{\mu\tau}$ channel shows particularly strong sensitivity to the PFDM contribution for the considered configurations. Moreover, the path-interference terms retain information about the absolute neutrino mass scale. These results characterize PFDM-induced modifications of lensed neutrino oscillations and provide a basis for more realistic treatments including decoherence, averaging, detector resolution, and corrections to lens trajectories and magnifications.

</details>


### [79] [Gravitational fingerprints of a black hole solution in Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26692)
*Carlos F. S. Pereira,Araújo Filho,Marcos V. de S. Silva,N. Heidari*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole physics in Palatini Kalb–Ramond gravity, revealing that while key orbital features (photon sphere, ISCO, critical impact parameter) remain Schwarzschild-like when expressed in terms of orbital mass $\bar{M}$, geometric and dynamical quantities (proper distances, infall times, tidal forces, quasinormal mode frequencies, light deflection) depend on a radial deformation parameter $\sigma$, leading to observable deviations—especially in black hole shadows and gravitational wave damping/oscillation rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how Palatini-formulation Kalb–Ramond gravity—with its distinct coupling of torsion-like degrees of freedom to geometry—affects black hole structure, particle dynamics, wave propagation, and astrophysical observables, particularly distinguishing physical (areal) vs. conserved mass and isolating deformation effects via the parameter $\sigma$.

Method: Analytical and semi-analytical analysis: derivation of geodesic equations and effective potentials; computation of photon sphere, ISCO, tidal tensors, and Lyapunov exponents; weak- and strong-field light deflection with conical asymptotics; separation of perturbation sectors (scalar, EM, Dirac, axial gravitational); exact spectral mapping for axial $\delta B_{\mu\nu}=0$ sector; third-order WKB approximation validated against time-domain evolution.

Result: At fixed orbital mass $\bar{M}$, null and timelike circular orbits retain Schwarzschild values, but proper-time and areal-length dependent quantities scale with $\sigma$; tidal eigenvalues scale as $\sigma^{-2}$, photon orbit instability exponent as $\sigma^{-1}$; axial gravitational QNMs scale as $\sigma^{-1}$ in both real (oscillation) and imaginary (damping) parts; WKB matches time-evolution frequencies within 0.7%; shadow observables exhibit coupling degeneracy between $\sigma$ and $\bar{M}$.

Conclusion: Palatini Kalb–Ramond black holes are 'Schwarzschild-like' in coordinate-invariant orbital kinematics when parametrized by $\bar{M}$, but deviate measurably in proper-time dynamics, tidal effects, quasinormal ringing, and lensing—making $\sigma$ a physically distinguishable deformation parameter, albeit degenerate with $\bar{M}$ in current shadow data from Sgr A* and M87*.

Abstract: We investigate particle motion, optical observables, and linear wave propagation around a static black hole in Palatini Kalb Ramond gravity with a vanishing cosmological constant. The analysis distinguishes the conserved mass $M$ from the orbital mass $\bar{M}=M/\rho$ and isolates the radial deformation $\sigma^2=1-q/2$, where $q=\ell_2/(1-\ell_1)$. At fixed $\bar{M}$, the photon sphere, critical impact parameter, and innermost stable circular orbit retain their Schwarzschild values. Radial frequency ratios also preserve their Schwarzschild profiles for the same areal release and particle radii, while proper distances, infall times, and accumulated orbital angles depend on $\sigma$. For radial fall, the tidal eigenvalues acquire a factor $\sigma^{-2}$, and the instability exponent of the photon orbit scales as $\sigma^{-1}$. In addition, we examine light deflection in the weak and strong regimes with an explicit account of the conical asymptotics. The shadow comparison with Sgr A* and M87* exhibits a coupling degeneracy when the orbital mass and areal distance are set. Scalar, electromagnetic, and massless Dirac perturbations probe the physical metric, whereas a homogeneous axial gravitational sector with $\delta B_{\mu\nu}=0$ admits an exact Schwarzschild spectral map. In this sector, both oscillation and attenuation rates scale as $\sigma^{-1}$ at fixed $\bar{M}$. Third order WKB estimates agree with frequencies extracted from time evolution to within $0.7\%$ for the reported fundamental modes.

</details>


### [80] [Destinies of Plasmoids Formed from the Magnetic Reconnection Occurring within the Ergo Region of a Rotating Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25097)
*Ho-Yun YuChih,Ye Shen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the Comisso-Asenjo process—energy extraction from rotating black holes via magnetic reconnection in accretion flows—and identifies a critical spin threshold (a = 0.8005) below which energy extraction fails, even with arbitrarily high plasma magnetization, by rigorously analyzing the geodesic escape condition of the positive-energy plasmoid (p₊).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior studies of the Comisso-Asenjo process established the necessity of negative-energy plasmoids (p₋) but neglected the dynamical requirement that the positive-energy counterpart (p₊) must escape to infinity; this work fills that gap by systematically evaluating p₊'s geodesic fate.

Method: Geodesic analysis in Kerr spacetime: the authors compute trajectories of p₊ ejected from equatorial reconnection sites within the ergosphere, imposing boundary conditions for unbound (escape-capable) motion; they introduce and compute a revised 'covering factor' counting only those p₊ that reach infinity, and derive its dependence on black hole spin a and plasma magnetization.

Result: A strict lower bound on black hole spin is found: a_min = 0.8005; below this value, no p₊ can escape regardless of magnetization, rendering energy extraction impossible; consequently, black hole spin-down via successive reconnections halts at a ≈ 0.8005.

Conclusion: Successful black hole energy extraction via magnetic reconnection requires not only the generation of negative-energy plasmoids but also the guaranteed escape of their positive-energy counterparts—a condition enforceable only above a critical spin; this establishes a fundamental limit on spin-down efficiency and implies a natural equilibrium spin for black holes subject to such reconnection-driven extraction.

Abstract: It is widely accepted that magnetic reconnections occur successively in black hole accretion flows, especially on the equatorial plane, owing to the formation of the equatorial current sheet. When magnetic reconnection takes place within the ergosphere of a rotating black hole, one of the ejected plasmoids (denoted as $p_-$) may acquire negative energy-at-infinity density, while the companion plasmoid (denoted as $p_+$) correspondingly carries the energy extracted from the black hole. This mechanism, known as the Comisso-Asenjo process, provides a viable astrophysical pathway for extracting energy from a rotating black hole.
