Generally speaking, two conditions have to be satisfied for successful energy extraction. First, $p_-$ is generated via magnetic reconnection with negative energy-at-infinity density. Second, the associated $p_+$, which carries the extracted energy, must escape to infinity. Though a number of studies have promoted the theory of the Comisso-Asenjo process, the escape of $p_+$ lacks consideration. In this work, we adopt geodesic approach to systematically examine the destinies of $p_+$ after ejection, thereby establishing the complete conditions for energy extraction from a rotating black hole via magnetic reconnection. We redefine the covering factor, for which only escaping $p_+$ are counted. This quantity reflects how probable the successful energy extraction occurs in an accretion system. Through analysis of this revised covering factor, we identify a lower bound on the black hole spin, $a=0.8005$, below which energy extraction via magnetic reconnection can never succeed, no matter how high the magnetization of the bulk plasma is. This limit suggests that the spin-down of a black hole caused by successive magnetic reconnections would terminate once the spin decreases to approximately 0.8005.

</details>


### [81] [A new regime of the C-metric: Super accelerating black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25110)
*Francisco Silva*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the super-accelerating regime of the charged C-metric at large acceleration, revealing a restricted polar coordinate domain, a new symmetry axis, modified conical singularity structure, a simplified Komar mass formula $M_K = 1/(4A)$ when the south-pole defect is removed, and novel near-extremal limiting geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the geometric and physical properties of the charged C-metric in the previously unexplored large-acceleration (super-accelerating) regime, particularly how coordinate restrictions alter global structure, conical singularities, mass definitions, and extremal limits.

Method: Analytical investigation of the charged C-metric spacetime geometry: coordinate domain analysis, identification of symmetry axes, classification of conical singularities, computation of Komar mass with appropriate Killing vector normalization, and asymptotic analysis of near-extremal limits.

Result: 1) Polar coordinate restriction to a subinterval of $(0,\pi)$ introduces a new axis of symmetry; 2) Conical singularity structure—and thus string/strut interpretation—is altered; 3) Removing the south-pole conical defect yields Komar mass $M_K = 1/(4A)$ (up to time-Killing normalization); 4) New near-extremal limiting geometries are identified.

Conclusion: The super-accelerating regime exhibits qualitatively distinct global geometry and physical interpretation compared to standard accelerating black hole solutions; the simplified mass formula and novel extremal limits suggest deeper connections between acceleration, mass, and extremality in asymptotically non-flat spacetimes.

Abstract: We define and investigate the super accelerating regime of the charged C-metric, which arises at large acceleration. In this regime, the polar coordinate must be restricted to a smaller interval within $(0,\pi)$, and the resulting boundary defines a new axis of symmetry. This changes the conical singularity structure of the spacetime and, consequently, its string/strut interpretation. We further show that, when the conical defect at the south pole is removed, the Komar mass takes the simple form $M_K=1/(4A)$, up to the normalization of the time translation Killing vector. Finally, we explore the near extremal limits of this regime, identifying new limiting geometries.

</details>


### [82] [Optical constraints on rotating logarithmic- and exponential-entropy wormholes from strong-field lensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25132)
*A. Errehymy G. Yergaliyeva,S. K. Maurya,B. Turimov,I. Ibragimov,J. Rayimbaev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper addresses the degeneracy in black hole mimickers by introducing a 5-component optical fingerprint—rotational asymmetry, two strong-deflection lensing coefficients, Lyapunov exponent, and orbital frequency—to distinguish slowly rotating traversable wormholes from Kerr black holes and regular black hole models, achieving SNR ~3.7–3.9 and breaking shadow-diameter degeneracy at the 10% measurement level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The shadow diameter alone is insufficient to distinguish horizonless compact objects (e.g., wormholes) from black holes due to geometric degeneracy; a more robust multi-observable diagnostic is needed.

Method: Derives equatorial null geodesic observables for slowly rotating traversable wormholes using a novel interpolating lapse function; computes photon-sphere condition, strong-deflection lensing coefficients (\bar{a}, \bar{b}), Lyapunov exponent (\Lambda), orbital frequency (\Omega_\phi), and rotational asymmetry (A_rot) in closed form; constructs a 5-component fingerprint F = (A_rot, \bar{a}, \bar{b}, \Lambda, \Omega_\phi) and quantifies fractional deviations from Kerr and benchmark models.

Result: All wormhole models match Kerr shadow diameter within 5–11%; however, all five fingerprint components deviate from Kerr by ≥5%, especially \bar{b} and \Lambda; combined discrimination yields SNR ≈ 3.7–3.9 against Kerr and Kazakov–Solodukhin models, sufficient to break degeneracy at 10% observational precision.

Conclusion: Shadow diameter is inadequate for model discrimination; the proposed multi-parameter optical fingerprint provides statistically significant separation between horizonless wormholes and black holes—including Kerr and regular black hole alternatives—enabling future EHT-grade tests of horizon existence.

Abstract: The longstanding issue of whether horizonless compact objects can mimic black holes at horizon scales is addressed, a problem that persists because the shadow diameter alone is degenerate across a wide class of geometries. For slowly rotating traversable wormholes, we precisely formulate the optical appearance as a set of observables derived from equatorial null geodesics, yielding necessary and sufficient conditions for a multi-parameter fingerprint. Using a new lapse function that interpolates between exponential and rational redshift profiles, we obtain the photon-sphere condition, the strong-deflection lensing coefficients, and the Lyapunov exponent in closed form. The equations yield two families of wormhole solutions dependent on logarithmic and exponential entropy parameters. We compare the resulting quantum-modified spacetimes with Kerr and four regular black holes. The shadow diameter is shown to be tunable to within $5\%$ to $11\%$ of Kerr for all models considered, so that $|R_{\rm sh}^{\rm WH}/R_{\rm sh}^{\rm Kerr}-1| \lesssim 0.11$ and the shadow alone cannot separate the models. The degeneracy is lifted when the rotational asymmetry, lensing coefficients, Lyapunov exponent, and orbital frequency are combined: for the $5$-component fingerprint $\mathbf{F}=(\Arot,\abar,\bbar, \Lya,\Oph)$, the fractional deviation $\Delta_i=(F_i^{\rm WH}-F_i^{\rm Kerr})/ F_i^{\rm Kerr}$ satisfies $|\Delta_i|\gtrsim 0.05$ for every component, with the largest departures in the second lensing coefficient and the Lyapunov exponent. The combined discrimination signal reaches a signal-to-noise ratio of about $3.8$ to $3.9$ against Kerr and about $3.7$ against the Kazakov--Solodukhin benchmark, which is sufficient to break the shadow-diameter degeneracy at the $10\%$ measurement level.

</details>


### [83] [Photon production at Kepler escape threshold](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25160)
*Michael R.R. Good*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives exact closed-form expressions for total photon count, angularly resolved photon spectrum, and radiated energy/momentum from a relativistic charged particle moving along a Newtonian Kepler parabola; the photon count is finite and scale-independent, grows linearly with rapidity η, and exhibits hardening radiation, contrasting with IR divergence in hyperbolic cases and distinct scaling versus elliptic orbits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the infrared (IR) behavior and quantify radiation characteristics—especially photon count and spectral hardness—at the parabolic threshold of unbound gravitational orbits, where relativistic effects and quantum emission become significant despite classical trajectory assumptions.

Method: Fully relativistic classical electrodynamics applied to a point charge following an exact Newtonian Kepler parabolic orbit; analytic computation of Liénard–Wiechert fields, Fourier decomposition of acceleration, and integration over frequency and solid angle to obtain photon number via quantum correspondence (N = ∫ dω dΩ d²N/dωdΩ).

Result: Exact closed-form total photon count N, angularly resolved photon spectrum d²N/dωdΩ, total radiated energy E and momenta; N is finite, scale-independent, analytic in subluminal speed at periastron, asymptoting as N ∼ (7α/√6)η for large η; E ∝ e^{3η} at fixed passage timescale; hyperbolic case remains IR divergent near threshold, while parabolic case is finite.

Conclusion: The parabolic Kepler orbit constitutes a critical, finite-radiation threshold separating IR-divergent hyperbolic emission from bounded elliptic emission; its finiteness reflects cancellation of soft-photon divergences due to exact zero-binding-energy kinematics, offering a clean benchmark for strong-field radiation reaction and quantum-classical correspondence in gravitational-wave analogs.

Abstract: We obtain an exact closed-form total photon count for a prescribed Newtonian Kepler parabola, with a fully relativistic treatment of its radiation. We also obtain closed expressions for the angularly resolved photon spectrum and elementary expressions for the total radiated energy and momenta. The count is finite, scale-independent, and analytic in subluminal speed at closest approach. At large maximum rapidity $\eta$, $N\sim(7\alpha/\sqrt6)\eta$, while $E\propto e^{3\eta}$ at fixed passage timescale: the radiation chiefly hardens. For slow motion at fixed coupling and closest distance, a nearby ellipse emits fewer photons but more energy and angular momentum per orbit. The hyperbolic photon count remains IR divergent arbitrarily close to threshold, while the count at the exact parabolic threshold is finite.

</details>


### [84] [Moving Beyond Quasinormal Modes and Reinterpreting Direct Waves in Binary Black Hole Merger-Ringdowns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25176)
*Richard Dyer,Anuj Kankani,Gareth Arturo Marks,Sean T. McWilliams,Christopher J. Moore*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper challenges claims of a 'direct wave' originating near the event horizon during black hole ringdown, presenting three independent lines of evidence that instead link the early ringdown component to the light ring (photon sphere), not the horizon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To critically assess recent suggestions—based on black-hole perturbation theory and effective-one-body models—that a measurable 'direct wave' exists in the ringdown signal and originates from deep inside the light ring or near the event horizon.

Method: The authors employ three independent analytical and numerical approaches: (i) rigorous analysis of wave propagation in Schwarzschild and Kerr spacetimes, (ii) time-domain simulations of merger–ringdown waveforms with controlled initial data, and (iii) spectral decomposition and echo analysis to isolate contributions from different radial regions.

Result: No evidence supports the existence of a 'direct wave' as previously defined; the early-time non-quasinormal-mode ringdown component is shown to be causally connected to the light ring, not the event horizon; its amplitude, frequency, and damping are governed by light-ring physics.

Conclusion: The purported direct wave is a misinterpretation—the early ringdown features are fully consistent with null-geodesic (light-ring) dynamics and do not require or imply emission from within or near the horizon.

Abstract: Based on black-hole perturbation-theory calculations and an effective-one-body picture of binary black hole mergers, it has been suggested that a direct wave may be present and measurable in the ringdown. Furthermore, it has been suggested that this signal might originate from substantially inside the light ring (or photon sphere) of the remnant black hole, perhaps even from close to the event horizon. In this paper, we present three independent strands of evidence against such claims. In the process, we show that there is indeed an early-time part of the ringdown signal that cannot be directly modeled by quasinormal modes. However, there is no evidence that this is a direct wave as it has previously been defined, and we show that the properties of this component are related to the remnant's light ring rather than its horizon.

</details>


### [85] [Probing parity-breaking black-hole geometry with extreme-mass-ratio inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25291)
*Zhen-Bo Wu,Qing Ou,Peng-Cheng Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how breaking equatorial reflection symmetry in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) around parity-violating conformal Kerr black holes affects gravitational-wave signals, finding that second-order dynamical corrections induce measurable dephasing—especially at high black-hole spin—and yield a waveform mismatch of 0.01 at ε ≈ 10⁻³ for a representative one-year EMRI, though results depend on the adiabatic quadrupole approximation and require improved radiation modeling for robust constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe parity violation in gravity via equatorial reflection symmetry breaking in EMRIs around conformal Kerr black holes, complementing propagation-based tests (e.g., birefringence) with source-level signatures.

Method: Derives orbital dynamics to second order in the dimensionless deformation parameter ε for circular equatorial-like orbits; constructs adiabatic inspirals and gravitational-wave waveforms using a mass-quadrupole radiation model; computes noise-weighted waveform mismatches and performs single-parameter Fisher analysis for ε sensitivity.

Result: Second-order dynamical corrections cause cumulative dephasing; waveform mismatch ℳ = 0.01 is reached at ε ≈ 10⁻³ for a 10 M⊙–10⁶ M⊙ EMRI with a/M = 0.9 and ι = 2π/3 over one year; high spin enhances both dephasing and local Fisher sensitivity to ε.

Conclusion: Equatorial reflection symmetry breaking imprints detectable, spin-amplified phase effects in EMRI waveforms, offering a novel source-based test of parity violation in strong-field gravity—pending validation beyond the adiabatic quadrupole approximation and full Bayesian parameter estimation.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational-wave signatures of equatorial reflection symmetry breaking in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) around a parity-violating conformal Kerr black hole. For the circular-orbit branch connected to equatorial Kerr orbits, we derive the dynamics through second order in the dimensionless deformation parameter $\varepsilon$ and construct adiabatic inspirals and gravitational-wave signals using a mass-quadrupole prescription. Equatorial reflection maps $\varepsilon$ to $-\varepsilon$, making the polar displacement odd and the orbital frequency and energy even in the deformation. The induced first harmonic has a small direct contribution for the configurations studied, while second-order dynamical corrections accumulate over many cycles to produce appreciable dephasing. We find that rapid black-hole rotation enhances these phase effects and the associated noise-weighted waveform mismatch. For a one-year inspiral of a $10M_\odot$ object into a $10^6M_\odot$ black hole with $a/M=0.9$ and viewing inclination $\iota=2\pi/3$, the fixed-parameter mismatch reaches the comparison benchmark $\mathcal M=0.01$ at approximately $\varepsilon=10^{-3}$, without optimization over time, phase, or Kerr parameters. A single-parameter Fisher analysis also indicates improved local sensitivity to $\varepsilon$ at high spin. These results connect the reflection properties of the circular branch to accumulated source-phase effects and complement tests of propagation birefringence. The sensitivity estimates remain conditional on the adiabatic quadrupole model and the adopted comparison procedure; robust observational constraints require more accurate radiation modeling and a full parameter-estimation analysis.

</details>


### [86] [Scattering of scalar waves by scalarized black holes in Einstein-scalar-Gauss-Bonnet gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25316)
*Junyi Li,Yinrong Fang,Yang Huang,Qian Li,Junji Jia*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies scalar field scattering and absorption by scalarized black holes in Einstein-scalar-Gauss-Bonnet (EsGB) gravity, comparing quartic and exponential scalar couplings; it finds coupling-specific angular signatures in differential scattering—especially near the glory peak—that can diagnose the underlying coupling function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish between different scalar–Gauss-Bonnet coupling functions (quartic vs. exponential) via observable scattering/absorption features of scalarized black holes, beyond standard Schwarzschild predictions.

Method: Numerical construction of scalarized black hole backgrounds using Chebyshev pseudospectral method; partial-wave analysis of massive scalar field scattering/absorption; cross-validation with geodesic and glory approximations and Schwarzschild limit.

Result: Scalarization suppresses total absorption cross section relative to Schwarzschild; quartic coupling causes deviations mainly in intermediate angles (70°–140°), sparing forward angles and glory peak; exponential coupling modifies scattering across all angles and can enhance the glory peak beyond Schwarzschild value.

Conclusion: The angular structure of the differential scattering cross section—particularly glory peak behavior—provides a robust observational diagnostic to discriminate between EsGB coupling models.

Abstract: We systematically investigate the scattering and absorption of a massive scalar field by scalarized black holes in Einstein-scalar-Gauss-Bonnet gravity with quartic coupling $f(\phi)=\alpha(\phi^{2}+\beta\phi^{4})$ and exponential coupling $f(\phi)=\alpha(1-\operatorname{e}^{-\beta\phi^{2}})$. The background solutions are obtained numerically via a Chebyshev pseudospectral method, enabling a partial-wave analysis on purely numerical black hole backgrounds. The partial-wave results are cross-validated against geodesic and glory approximations and the Schwarzschild limit with excellent agreements. We find that scalarization suppresses the total absorption cross section relative to the Schwarzschild case, and produces distinctive signatures in the differential scattering cross section. A qualitative difference between the two coupling models is identified: for the quartic coupling model, deviations from Schwarzschild are concentrated in the intermediate-angle region $\sim(70^{\circ},140^{\circ})$, with comparatively small changes at forward angles and near the glory peak; for the exponential coupling model, deviations appear across the full angular range and substantially modify the glory peak, which in some parameter regimes can even exceed the Schwarzschild value. This distinctive angular pattern provides a potential diagnostic for distinguishing EsGB coupling functions from scattering observables.

</details>


### [87] [Searching for lensed gravitational waves with unlensed templates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26080)
*Digvijay Wadekar,Barak Zackay,Mark Ho-Yeuk Cheung,Tousif Islam,Javier Roulet,Ajit Kumar Mehta,Tejaswi Venumadhav,Jonathan Mushkin,Miguel Zumalacarregui,Matias Zaldarriaga*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a fast, efficient two-image method for detecting gravitationally lensed gravitational-wave signals from binary mergers, leveraging existing SNR time series to avoid costly matched filtering with explicit lensed templates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational lensing can produce overlapping or separated images of binary merger signals, which conventional unlensed-template searches miss or misclassify—leading to reduced sensitivity and false noise rejection. A computationally efficient and sensitive detection strategy for such lensed events is needed.

Method: The authors construct the matched-filter output for two-image lensed templates directly from the SNR time series generated by standard unlensed-template searches. They derive a marginalized detection statistic that coherently combines both images—applicable to both overlapping (geometric-optics regime) and separated image configurations—and incorporate signal-consistency restoration for overlapping cases.

Result: The method achieves >97% optimal SNR recovery for overlapping images under point-mass and singular isothermal lenses (vs. only ~67% for unlensed templates), restores consistency for signals previously down-ranked as noise, boosts sensitive volume by 2–4× for strongly down-ranked events, and increases sensitive volume by 1.8–2.6× over independent-detection thresholds (and up to 1.6× over targeted counterpart searches) near fold caustics for separated images. It is >150× faster than an explicit lensed template bank.

Conclusion: The proposed two-image method significantly enhances sensitivity to lensed gravitational-wave signals across both overlapping and separated regimes, with minimal computational overhead, and is broadly applicable to any matched-filtering GW search.

Abstract: Gravitational lensing can split the signal of a binary merger into two images, which either overlap and interfere or arrive as separate events. We show that the matched-filter output of every two-image template can be straightforwardly constructed from the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) time series that searches already compute with unlensed templates. This enables a marginalized detection statistic that increases search sensitivity without additional matched filtering and is more than 150 times faster than an explicit lensed template bank. For overlapping images in the geometric-optics regime, our two-image method recovers $\gtrsim97\%$ of the optimal SNR from point-mass and singular isothermal lenses, compared with as little as $67\%$ captured by unlensed templates, with substantial gains also for Chang-Refsdal and cored isothermal lenses. Overlapping lensed signals moreover fail the signal-consistency test that is used to down-rank noise transients. Our two-image method restores the consistency and increases the sensitive volume by roughly $2$-$4\times$ for the strongly down-ranked signals. For separated images, our method enables an efficient coherent search for pairs in which neither image is significant alone. Near fold caustics, this increases the sensitive volume by factors of 1.8-2.6 over requiring two independent detections, and up to 1.6 over a targeted counterpart search. Our method is applicable to any matched-filtering search.

</details>


### [88] [Outer trapped surface formation from gravitational waves collision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26320)
*Junbin Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper rigorously constructs outer trapped surface formation in smooth, asymmetric vacuum spacetimes via collision of two short-pulse gravitational waves; the surface is formed by smoothly joining two pieces with matched outer null normals, and future incompleteness of the maximal development is proven using an adaptation of Penrose’s argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a symmetry-free, rigorous demonstration of outer trapped surface formation—key to black hole formation—in vacuum general relativity, inspired by Penrose’s heuristic ultra-relativistic particle collision model.

Method: Constructs short-pulse, transversely localized, nearly plane-fronted gravitational wave initial data; joins two smooth spacetime regions along a curve with C⁰-matching of future-directed outer null normals; applies a geometric analysis of null geodesic congruences and adapts Penrose’s singularity theorem framework to establish future boundary incompleteness.

Result: Existence of a smooth outer trapped surface in a vacuum spacetime without symmetry assumptions; proof that the maximal future Cauchy development is future-incomplete (i.e., contains a singularity or null infinity boundary).

Conclusion: Trapped surface formation—and thus gravitational collapse leading to spacetime singularities—can occur robustly in generic (non-symmetric) vacuum settings, validating the physical relevance of Penrose’s cosmic censorship intuition beyond idealized models.

Abstract: We give a new rigorous construction of outer trapped surface formation in smooth vacuum spacetimes without symmetry assumptions. The outer trapped surface is produced by the collision of two transversely localized, nearly plane-fronted gravitational waves described by short pulse data. The outer trapped surface is obtained by joining two smooth pieces along a curve with their future-directed outer null normals matching continuously. The incompleteness of the maximal future development is then established by adapting Penrose's original argument. This construction is inspired by Penrose's argument in constructing a marginally outer trapped surface in the collision of two ultra-relativistic particles modeled by the Aichelburg--Sexl solution.

</details>


### [89] [Periodic orbits and gravitational-wave emission from extreme-mass-ratio inspirals around rotating charged black holes in nonlinear electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26113)
*Xi Luo,Meirong Tang,Zhaoyi Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies rotating, charged black holes in Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg nonlinear electrodynamics (EEH-NED), extending Kerr-Newman geometry; it analyzes timelike equatorial geodesics, ISCO/MBO, and gravitational-wave waveforms to probe deviations from the no-hair theorem and support LISA/TianQin tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the no-hair theorem and modified gravity via black-hole 'hair' induced by nonlinear electrodynamics, using observable EMRI dynamics and gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Analytical derivation of equatorial timelike geodesics and effective potential in EEH-NED spacetime (s=2); numerical computation of marginally-bound orbits (MBO) and innermost stable circular orbits (ISCO); orbit classification via (z,w,v) taxonomy; adiabatic quadrupole-based calculation of h+ and hx waveforms.

Result: Increasing spin a and NED charge q contract bound orbits inward, shift E-L phase-space to lower angular momentum, enhance zoom-whirl behavior, shorten waveform periods, and amplify signal amplitudes.

Conclusion: EEH-NED black holes produce distinctive, parameter-dependent EMRI dynamics and GW signatures—enabling space-based observatories like LISA and TianQin to observationally constrain NED extensions and test the no-hair theorem.

Abstract: Within general-relativistic descriptions, the no-hair theorem dictates that black-hole geometries possess just three intrinsic physical quantities: mass, angular momentum, and electric charge. Black holes in nonlinear electrodynamics (NED) are equivalent to those composed of anisotropic matter and can develop "hair" structures near the event horizon, serving as ideal testbeds for modified theories of gravity. Working within Einsteinian gravitational theory, we adopt a coupling construction between gravity and NED matter, focusing explicitly on the Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg (EEH) variant with the power-index parameter fixed at s = 2. In this framework, a rotating, charged NED black hole extends the Kerr-Newman geometry with extra correction terms. Treating spin a and NED charge q as primary control variables, we examine test-particle dynamics inside extreme-mass-ratio inspiral (EMRI) configurations. We derive equatorial timelike geodesics and the effective potential. Through numerical evaluation of marginally-bound orbits (MBO) and innermost stable circular orbits (ISCO), we explore how these parameters reshape the energy-angular-momentum (E-L) phase-space. Using the (z, w, v) integer-valued orbit taxonomy, we compute the two gravitational-wave polarization modes h+ and hx via the adiabatic approximation and quadrupole radiation prescription. Larger values for a and q drive inward contraction for bound trajectories while displacing the E-L phase-space toward smaller angular-momentum values. Periodic-orbit zoom-whirl signatures grow increasingly prominent, while larger parameter values shorten the waveform periods and enhance signal amplitudes. Our results provide theoretical support for space-borne gravitational-wave observatories including LISA and TianQin, enabling prospects for discriminating NED black-hole spacetimes and performing observational checks on the no-hair theorem.

</details>


### [90] [Local scale symmetry and black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26208)
*Malik Almatwi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole solutions coupled to a real scalar field under local scale symmetry, analyzing thermodynamic properties and scalar field distribution for both λ=0 and λ≠0 cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how local scale symmetry affects black hole solutions and scalar field dynamics, particularly regarding thermodynamics (temperature, entropy, effective mass) and spatial distribution of the scalar field.

Method: Analytical derivation of black hole solutions in a locally scale-invariant gravitational theory with a real scalar field; computation of horizon structure, temperature, entropy, and effective mass; qualitative analysis of scalar field localization based on black hole mass and coupling parameter λ.

Result: For λ=0: explicit black hole solutions obtained; temperature, entropy, and growing effective mass due to adiabatic scalar accretion. For λ≠0: horizon positions depend on black hole mass, scalar field strength, λ, and R_φ; two distinct regimes identified—scalar field localized far from BH (small mass) or inside BH (large mass).

Conclusion: Local scale symmetry introduces nontrivial coupling between black holes and scalar fields, leading to mass growth via adiabatic accretion and qualitatively different scalar field localization depending on black hole mass and self-interaction strength.

Abstract: We derive solutions for a black hole coupled to a real scalar field under considerations of local scale symmetry. Starting with the simple case $(\lambda=0)$, we find the solutions and derive the black hole's temperature, entropy, and its effective mass, and observe an adiabatic accretion of the scalar field onto the black hole. As a result, the effective mass of the black hole grows. In the general case $(\lambda\ne 0)$, the situation becomes more intricate; The horizons (the vanishing points of $g_{tt}$) depend on the mass of the black hole, scalar field strength, $\lambda$ and other positive parameter $R_\phi$. In this case, we propose two possible behaviors: When the black hole's mass is small enough, the field is mostly concentrated far from the black hole, whereas when the black hole's mass is sufficiently large, it is mostly concentrated inside the black hole.

</details>


### [91] [Analog regular black holes and black hole mimickers in a Bose-Einstein condensate: from the core to the light rings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26257)
*Valentin Pomakov,Stefano Liberati*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes an experimental analogue of regular black holes and mimickers in quasi-2D Bose–Einstein condensates, achieving exact horizon and surface gravity matching via acoustic metric engineering; it corrects light-ring shifts via tailored interaction profiles, derives fully realizable external potentials and interaction strengths, and provides experimentally feasible designs using 39K condensates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe inner-horizon and inner-light-ring instabilities of regular black holes and mimickers in a controlled laboratory setting using analogue gravity in Bose–Einstein condensates.

Method: Metric emulation via acoustic geometry in a quasi-two-dimensional BEC: matching gravitational and acoustic line elements on an equatorial disc to fix velocity-to-sound-speed ratio; correcting residual sound-speed-induced light-ring shifts with engineered interaction profiles; deriving closed-form external potential and interaction strength from continuity and Bernoulli equations; translating theoretical construction into experimental feasibility constraints (confinement, healing length, three-body loss, flow stability).

Result: Exact reproduction of horizon locations and surface gravities; analytical correction of light-ring position shift; closed-form, tunable expressions for external potential and interaction strength parameterized by mass M and regularization length ℓ; three experimentally viable designs using current 39K BEC platforms.

Conclusion: Spherically symmetric regular black-hole spacetimes with radius-dependent mass m(r) can be faithfully emulated in quasi-2D BECs, enabling laboratory investigation of inner-structure instabilities—provided the sound-speed profile is precisely engineered and experimental constraints (especially healing-length resolution and flow lifetime) are satisfied.

Abstract: We show how static, spherically symmetric regular black holes and black-hole mimickers with radius-dependent mass function $m(r)$ can be emulated in a quasi-two-dimensional Bose--Einstein condensate, with the aim of probing inner-horizon and inner-light-ring instabilities in the laboratory. Matching the acoustic and gravitational line elements on an equatorial disc fixes the ratio of radial flow velocity to sound speed and reproduces both horizon locations and surface gravities exactly. The analogue geometry nevertheless contains a residual radially varying sound-speed factor in $g_{tt}$, which shifts the light-ring positions and reduces the range of the regularization parameter $\ell/M$ for which a light-ring pair exists. We derive the interaction profile that removes this shift. Combining the metric correspondence with the continuity and Bernoulli equations, we then obtain closed-form expressions for the external potential and interaction strength realizing the full target spacetime, with $M$ and $\ell$ directly tunable through parameters of the potential. Finally, we translate the construction into experimental requirements (quasi-two-dimensional confinement, healing-length resolution, three-body lifetime, and stationary-flow lifetime) and present three fiducial designs achievable with present-day $^{39}\mathrm{K}$ platforms.

</details>


### [92] [Primordial black holes in braneworld gravity: A gateway to hidden dimensions and the dark Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26482)
*Itzi Aldecoa-Tamayo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This thesis explores primordial black holes (PBHs) in the Randall–Sundrum Type II braneworld, showing that higher-dimensional effects significantly enhance early accretion and modify evaporation, enabling PBHs below the 4D Planck mass to constitute all dark matter while satisfying current observational constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether PBHs in a higher-dimensional braneworld cosmology can serve as viable dark matter candidates, given that standard 4D treatments may misrepresent their early-time dynamics and observational signatures.

Method: 1) Fully relativistic PBH accretion modeling using Michel theory within the covariant Shiromizu–Maeda–Sasaki formalism for RS-II; 2) Reanalysis of observational constraints (Hawking radiation, gamma-ray background, microlensing, CMB energy injection) incorporating modified higher-dimensional evaporation and updated surveys.

Result: Higher-dimensional effects boost early accretion onto light PBHs, causing mass discrepancies scaling as t^0.33 and altering the initial-to-present mass mapping; revised constraints identify viable parameter space where PBHs < M_Pl(4D) can fully account for dark matter.

Conclusion: PBHs in the RS-II braneworld constitute a physically consistent, observationally allowed dark matter candidate with distinct formation, growth, and evaporation histories compared to 4D PBHs.

Abstract: This thesis investigates the cosmological dynamics of primordial black holes (PBHs) within the Randall--Sundrum Type II braneworld scenario. The work comprises a preliminary discussion on gravitational collapse in the small-black-hole limit at early cosmological epochs, and two principal studies. The first study presents a fully relativistic treatment of PBH accretion in the early Universe, employing Michel accretion theory within the covariant Shiromizu--Maeda--Sasaki formalism. The analysis reveals that higher-dimensional effects substantially enhance early-time accretion onto light PBHs, with discrepancies between our black hole masses and previous estimates growing with cosmic time as $t^{0.33}$ --- a difference that reaches several orders of magnitude by the end of the strong-accretion epoch. This finding fundamentally alters the mapping between the initial PBH mass spectrum and present-day populations. The second study reassesses the observational and cosmological constraints on higher-dimensional PBHs in Randall--Sundrum cosmologies. Constraints from direct Hawking radiation, unresolved diffuse gamma-ray backgrounds, microlensing surveys, and energy injection into the cosmic microwave background are reanalysed in light of the modified higher-dimensional evaporation dynamics and updated observational surveys. Taken together, these results identify viable regions of parameter space in which PBHs can account for the entirety of dark matter while remaining consistent with current observational bounds. Notably, due to the enhanced accretion epoch, PBHs capable of constituting a significant fraction of dark matter may originate from masses below the effective four-dimensional Planck scale. This thesis thus puts forward a dark matter candidate with a distinct evolutionary history and observational signatures today.

</details>


### [93] [GPU-accelerated Bayesian inference for diffracted gravitational wave signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26619)
*Zihao Lin,Jef Heynen,Mick Wright,Thomas C. K. Ng,Chris Van Den Broeck,Justin Janquart*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces Jim-lensing, a GPU-accelerated Bayesian inference framework for full-wave-optics gravitational lensing of gravitational waves, reducing parameter estimation runtime from >10 hours to ~10 minutes while maintaining accuracy and enabling robust significance testing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The growing rate of gravitational wave detections increases the likelihood of observing gravitationally lensed events; however, CPU-based wave-optics parameter estimation is prohibitively slow, hindering statistical assessment of lensing candidates.

Method: Implementation of a point-mass lens model within Jim—a high-performance GPU-based Bayesian parameter estimation pipeline—enabling end-to-end GPU-accelerated inference, including Bayes factor computation, without surrogate or training data.

Result: Jim-lensing achieves ~60× speedup over state-of-the-art CPU pipelines (from >10 h to ~10 min per full parameter estimation run), demonstrates numerical stability, and reproduces results consistent with the CPU baseline.

Conclusion: Jim-lensing enables scalable, accurate, and efficient Bayesian analysis of wave-optics lensing, facilitating broader exploration of lens models and robust frequentist significance estimates in upcoming gravitational-wave catalogs.

Abstract: As the rate of gravitational wave detections continues to grow, the probability of observing a gravitationally lensed gravitational wave also increases. When the Schwarzschild radius of the lens is comparable in size to the gravitational wave wavelength, wave-optics effects induce a frequency- dependent modulation on the signal. CPU-based parameter estimation analyses for wave-optics lensing are computationally intensive, limiting the scale of studies to determine the significance of any potential lensing candidate. In this work, we implement a point-mass lens model in Jim, a high-performance GPU-based parameter estimation pipeline. This enables, for the first time, GPU-accelerated Bayesian inference in full-wave optics, including the computation of Bayes factors, without the need for training data. We call this framework Jim-lensing. Using this framework, we are able to speed up a full parameter estimation run under the point-mass lens hypothesis from more than 10 hours to the order of 10 minutes compared to the current state-of-the-art CPU-based pipeline. We show that our implementation is stable and obtains comparable results to this state-of- the-art pipeline. The substantial speed gain over existing CPU pipelines paves the way for analyzing gravitational wave data under a broader range of lensing models with greater capacity to produce robust frequentist significance estimates.

</details>


### [94] [Spectroscopy as a Probe of Spacetime Foam](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26766)
*A. G. Kim,P. E. Nugent,L. Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using spectral distortions—specifically, temporal coherence loss and stochastic arrival-time jitter induced by quantum-gravitational spacetime foam—to constrain the foam exponent α, complementing imaging-based spatial constraints. It derives bounds from GRB 221009A MeV lines (α ≳ 0.63) and COBE/FIRAS CMB spectrum (α ≳ 0.52 at 95% CL), finding them consistent with the holographic value α = 2/3 and existing imaging limits; only a stringent TeV survival bound (α ≳ 0.74) would conflict with current observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a spectroscopic probe of the temporal variation of quantum-gravitational spacetime foam—complementary to existing imaging-based constraints on its spatial variation—by analyzing how foam-induced stochastic arrival-time jitter modifies first-order temporal coherence and spectral shape.

Method: Analyzes how time-varying spacetime foam induces achromatic, stochastic arrival-time jitter, modifying g^(1)(τ) and thus the observed spectrum; derives spectral signatures including coherent core suppression (e^(-σ_φ²)) and an incoherent pedestal; applies three observational probes—broadband flux survival, fractional line width, and chromatic continuum deficit—to GRB 221009A (MeV lines) and CMB (COBE/FIRAS); performs statistical fitting and forecasts for next-generation spectrometers.

Result: GRB 221009A yields α ≳ 0.63 (weak, lever-arm-limited); COBE/FIRAS gives α ≳ 0.52 (95% CL); forecasted next-gen CMB spectrometers reach only α ≳ 0.54–0.55 due to foam–temperature degeneracy; all bounds are floors below α = 2/3 and consistent with imaging constraints; only the conditional TeV survival bound (α ≳ 0.74, requiring near-Planck-refresh timescales τ_foam ≲ 4×10⁻²⁸ s) would exclude the current GRB-imaging window.

Conclusion: Spectral coherence analysis provides a viable, complementary channel to constrain dynamical spacetime foam; current bounds are consistent with holographic expectations and do not challenge existing imaging results; improved sensitivity requires breaking the σ_φ–T degeneracy in CMB spectroscopy or identifying robust broadband survival at TeV energies.

Abstract: Quantum-gravitational spacetime foam varying in space and in time imprints, respectively, a transverse phase decoherence and an achromatic, stochastic arrival-time jitter on light from distant sources. We show that the jitter modifies the first-order temporal coherence $g^{(1)}(\tau)$ and hence the observed spectrum, providing a spectroscopic probe of the temporal variation, complementary to existing imaging-channel constraints on the spatial variation. In the dynamical-foam limit, where the foam decorrelates within the light's coherence time, the spectrum splits into a coherent line core suppressed by $e^{-\sigma_\phi^2}$, with $\sigma_\phi^2$ the variance of the foam-induced phase jitter, and an incoherent pedestal, yielding a spectral shape-distortion bound on the foam exponent $\alpha$. Three probes follow: the outright survival of broadband flux, the fractional width of a spectral line, and a chromatic deficit across a broadband continuum. Applied to the MeV emission lines of GRB\,221009A we obtain $\alpha \gtrsim 0.63$, a weak, lever-arm-dominated floor. For the broadband CMB the foam induces an $e^{-\sigma_\phi^2(\nu)}$ deficit ($\sigma_\phi^2 \propto \nu^2$); a fit to the COBE/FIRAS monopole spectrum reaches $\alpha \gtrsim 0.52$ ($95\%$ CL), and next-generation spectrometers reach only $\alpha\gtrsim 0.54$--$0.55$ (forecast), limited by the foam--temperature degeneracy. The pedestal-independent line-width and CMB bounds are floors below the holographic value $\alpha = 2/3$, consistent with and excluding no part of the imaging window reported for GRB\,221009A; only the conditional TeV survival bound ($\alpha\gtrsim0.74$), which holds only for near-Planck-refresh foam ($\tau_{\rm foam}\lesssim4\times10^{-28}$\,s), would exclude it --- and, with it, the window.

</details>


### [95] [Spherically symmetric solutions with dust and radiation sources in Schouten-Codazzi Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26775)
*Sebastián Nájera,Roberto A. Sussman,Juan Carlos Hidalgo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies exact solutions of Schouten-Codazzi gravity (SCG), a second-order metric theory extending GR by adding a Codazzi tensor to the Einstein equations. It analyzes spherically symmetric Vaidya and LTB spacetimes, showing that Codazzi constraints introduce two integration functions—yielding an effective cosmological constant and extra dynamical degree of freedom—modifying mass definitions and enabling accelerated, ever-expanding cosmologies without dark energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether Schouten-Codazzi gravity—a geometric extension of GR incorporating a Codazzi tensor—can naturally produce cosmic acceleration and rich cosmological dynamics without invoking dark energy.

Method: Exact analytical solution of SCG field equations for two spherically symmetric ansätze: generalized Vaidya (null radiation) and Lemaître-Tolman-Bondi (inhomogeneous dust); imposition of Codazzi compatibility conditions; derivation and analysis of modified Misner-Sharp mass and cubic evolution equation for LTB scale factor.

Result: Codazzi tensor contributions generate an effective cosmological constant and an extra dynamical degree of freedom; Vaidya geometry exhibits modified Misner-Sharp mass; LTB evolution obeys a cubic ODE whose coefficients depend on Codazzi integration functions; resulting models support accelerated expansion and eternal expansion without dark energy.

Conclusion: Schouten-Codazzi gravity provides a viable, geometrically motivated alternative framework for cosmology, capable of reproducing key observational features (e.g., cosmic acceleration) without dark energy, broadening the landscape of gravitational theories beyond GR.

Abstract: We examine in this paper exact solutions of Schouten-Codazzi gravity (SCG), a second order metric theory in which a Codazzi tensor is added to the Einstein field equations. We consider two spherically symmetric geometries that describe null radiation and inhomogeneous dust, corresponding to generalized Vaidya and Lemaître-Tolman-Bondi (LTB) spacetimes. The Codazzi tensor has to comply the Codazzi equations, this imposes restrictions on the geometry of spacetime that are determined up to two integration functions that naturally generate an effective ``cosmological constant'' and an additional degree of freedom. We show that for the Vaidya geometry, these Codazzi contributions modify the Misner-Sharp mass, while the LTB models have as an evolution equation a cubic polynomial whose coefficients depend on the Codazzi integration functions. This leads to a broader class of dynamics than those of General Relativity, and allows for an accelerated expansion and ever-expanding models without the introduction of ``dark energy''. Overall, our analysis suggests that Schouten-Codazzi gravity provides an appealing framework for applications in cosmology.

</details>

### [96] [Light meson decays at BESIII](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24472)
*Kailu Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent BESIII results on η and η′ physics using the world's largest J/ψ sample, focusing on radiative decays, decay dynamics, electromagnetic form factors, and rare lepton-pair decays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The large J/ψ data sample collected by BESIII enables high-precision studies of η and η′ mesons via J/ψ → γη/η′, offering unique sensitivity to light-meson spectroscopy, QCD anomalies, chiral symmetry breaking, and potential new physics in rare decays.

Method: Analysis of high-statistics J/ψ radiative decays (J/ψ → γη/η′) at BESIII; reconstruction and amplitude analysis of η/η′ decays including η′ → π⁰π⁰η, η → 3π, η′ → 4π, η/η′ → γe⁺e⁻, η′ → π⁺π⁻l⁺l⁻, and searches for η → l⁺l⁻ and η/η′ → l⁺l⁻l⁺l⁻ (l = e, μ).

Result: Evidence for a cusp effect in η′ → π⁰π⁰η; improved precision on η → 3π and η′ → 4π decay mechanisms; first or most precise measurements of η/η′ transition form factors via γe⁺e⁻ and π⁺π⁻l⁺l⁻; stringent upper limits on rare decays η → l⁺l⁻ and η/η′ → l⁺l⁻l⁺l⁻.

Conclusion: BESIII’s J/ψ dataset provides unprecedented access to η and η′ properties, yielding insights into low-energy QCD, anomaly-induced dynamics, and testing SM predictions — particularly in rare decays where deviations could signal new physics.

Abstract: The world's largest sample of $J/\psi$ events accumulated with the BESIII detector offers a unique opportunity to investigate $\eta$ and $\eta'$ physics via the radiative transition processes $J/\psi \to \gamma\eta/\eta'$. A selection of recent highlights in light meson spectroscopy achieved at BESIII is reviewed in these proceedings, including evidence for the cusp effect in $\eta' \to \pi^0\pi^0\eta$, precision studies of the decay mechanisms of $\eta \to 3\pi$ and $\eta' \to 4\pi$, electromagnetic transition form factor measurements with $\eta/\eta' \to \gamma e^+e^-$ and $\eta' \to \pi^+\pi^-l^+l^-$, as well as searches for rare decays of $\eta$ and $\eta'$ into lepton pairs, including $\eta \to l^+l^-$ and $\eta/\eta' \to l^+l^-l^+l^-$~($l = e, \mu$).

</details>


### [97] [ceci n'est pas un tagger, can an LLM flavour-tag a jet?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24954)
*Andrea Coccaro,Andrea Milici,Lucio Derin*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether large language models (LLMs) can perform quantitative scientific inference—specifically, hadronic jet flavour classification—using only pretrained knowledge and raw jet constituent data, without coding or conventional algorithm implementation; it demonstrates non-trivial discrimination performance and interpretable, physics-grounded natural-language rationales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether LLMs' pretrained scientific knowledge supports quantitative inference on real physical data—beyond current descriptive or code-generation use cases—in high-energy physics workflows.

Method: An LLM is prompted to assign flavour probabilities (b, c, light, τ) to jets from a public ATLAS flavour-tagging dataset using only reconstructed constituents; constraints prohibit code generation or implementation of standard algorithms, forcing direct inference from pretrained knowledge and input tokens.

Result: The LLM achieves non-trivial flavour discrimination (quantitatively comparable to recent ATLAS taggers) and generates natural-language rationales that invoke physically meaningful concepts correlated with true jet flavour—e.g., secondary vertices for b-jets, soft leptons for c-jets, and narrow collimation for τ-jets.

Conclusion: LLMs can perform domain-specific quantitative inference grounded in scientific knowledge, suggesting their potential as context-providing 'scientific co-pilots' that augment task-specific algorithms with interpretable, physics-aware reasoning.

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly capable of reasoning about scientific problems, effectively mastering and synthesizing entire fields of research and providing, for the first time, access to a distilled and queryable form of scientific knowledge. As the integration of LLMs rapidly spreads across the large collaborations of high-energy physics, aiming to re-think and optimize analysis workflows, it remains unclear whether the knowledge acquired during pretraining can support quantitative inference on physical data. Moving beyond the applications currently pursued by the community, we pose a simple question: can an LLM classify the flavour of a hadronic jet? An LLM is therefore asked to assign flavour probabilities (b, c, light, $\tau$) to jets from a public ATLAS flavour-tagging sample, using only reconstructed constituents. Under a small set of constraints, the model is not allowed to write code or implement conventional algorithms and must instead perform the inference directly from its pretrained knowledge and the available inputs. We report two complementary results and make publicly available, through a GitHub repository, all the code required to reproduce the experiment and obtain the quantitative results presented in this work. First, the model achieves non-trivial discrimination between flavours, which we compare with recent ATLAS flavour-tagging algorithms. Second, the model accompanies each prediction with a natural language rationale. The physical concepts invoked in these rationales correlate with the truth flavour and reproduce the features traditionally associated with each jet category. Flavour tagging thus provides a laboratory for broader questions: how can we actively leverage the corpus of scientific knowledge accumulated over decades of research? Could LLMs serve as a general source of scientific context for task-specific algorithms?

</details>


### [98] [Search for new physics with charm data at BESIII](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25753)
*Xinyu Chai*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent BESIII results probing beyond-Standard-Model physics—specifically lepton/baryon number violation, charged lepton flavor violation, and charmonium weak decays—using the world’s largest $J/\psi$ and $\psi(3686)$ data samples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address unresolved SM issues—matter-antimatter asymmetry, neutrino mass, oscillations, and hierarchy—which suggest new physics requiring experimental probes of rare or forbidden processes.

Method: Analysis of high-statistics $J/\psi$ and $\psi(3686)$ resonance data collected by the BESIII experiment to search for LNV/BNV, CLFV, and charmonium weak decays.

Result: Presents recent published upper limits and null observations for LNV/BNV and CLFV processes, and first measurements or improved constraints on charmonium weak decays.

Conclusion: BESIII provides stringent tests of SM extensions; no significant signals observed so far, but sensitivity is competitive and complementary to other experiments (e.g., LHCb, Belle II), guiding theoretical model-building.

Abstract: Up to now, the Standard Model (SM) remains confronted with several outstanding issues, including the matter-antimatter asymmetry in the universe, as well as the masses, oscillations, and hierarchical structure of neutrinos. These challenges are closely tied to the naturalness and stability of the SM, and have motivated numerous proposals for new physics beyond the SM. The BESIII experiment has accumulated the world's largest data samples at the $J/\psi$ and $\psi(3686)$ resonances, providing an ideal environment for probing signatures of physics beyond the SM. This proceeding presents a summary of recent results published by the BESIII Collaboration on the searches for lepton/baryon number violation (LNV/BNV), charged lepton flavor violation (CLFV), and charmonium weak decays.

</details>


### [99] [Low-Latency Beam-Background Suppression for the BESIII Trigger System Using Machine Learning Trained on Experimental Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26148)
*Hanlin Zhang,Minggang Zhao,Junhao Yin,Boping Chen,Jingzhou Zhao,Wenxuan Gong,Ke Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a data-driven machine-learning method using compact neural networks to suppress beam-induced backgrounds at the BESIII experiment, achieving 20–60% reduction in false-positive background rate at matched hadron efficiency, with FPGA-compatible design for low-latency trigger-level deployment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Beam-induced backgrounds increase trigger and DAQ rates at high-luminosity e+e− colliders; their complex dependence on beam/machine conditions necessitates data-driven approaches complementing simulation.

Method: Compact neural-network classifiers trained on BESIII experimental data using 48 binary Level-1 trigger conditions; two configurations evaluated—tracking + TOF only, and EMC-only—distinguishing hadron events from beam-background events at trigger level.

Result: 20–60% reduction in beam-background false-positive rate vs. established trigger selection at matched hadron efficiency; stable performance validated on random-trigger data; hardware synthesis confirms FPGA feasibility for low-latency implementation.

Conclusion: The proposed ML-based trigger-level suppression method is effective and deployable for BESIII trigger upgrades and serves as a reference for future high-luminosity e+e− colliders.

Abstract: Beam-induced backgrounds contribute to the trigger and data-acquisition rate at high-luminosity electron-positron colliders. Their dependence on beam and machine conditions motivates data-driven studies alongside simulation. A data-driven machine-learning method for trigger-level beam-background suppression is developed using experimental data recorded by BESIII. Compact neural-network classifiers are trained and evaluated for two trigger configurations: one based only on tracking and time-of-flight information, and one based only on electromagnetic-calorimeter information. Each classifier uses 48 binary Level-1 trigger conditions of the BESIII detector. The models distinguish hadron events from beam-background events and are evaluated against the established trigger selection at matched hadron-event efficiency. Relative to the established trigger selection, the beam-background false-positive rate is reduced by 20-60% across the studied configurations and center-of-mass energies, with stable suppression performance as tested using random-trigger data. Hardware-oriented synthesis indicates that the compact network is suitable for low-latency FPGA implementation. The proposed method can reduce the fraction of beam-background events in the trigger stream in future BESIII trigger upgrades. It also provides a reference for trigger optimization at future high-luminosity electron-positron colliders.

</details>


### [100] [Measurements of time-dependent b-hadron properties in LHCb](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26188)
*Lucas Foreman*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents three key LHCb Run 2 measurements of time-dependent CP violation in b-hadron decays: (1) improved determination of the CKM phase φₛ in Bₛ⁰ → J/ψπ⁺π⁻, (2) first observation of CP violation in B⁰ → J/ψρ⁰(770) and constraint on penguin pollution Δφₛ, and (3) first time-dependent CPV measurement in the rare b → sℓ⁺ℓ⁻ decay Bₛ⁰ → Kₛμ⁺μ⁻.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To precisely test the Standard Model via CP-violating observables in b-hadron decays, particularly to constrain new physics through deviations in CKM phases (φₛ, 2β) and penguin pollution, and to extend such tests to rare flavor-changing neutral current processes like b → sℓ⁺ℓ⁻.

Method: Time-dependent amplitude analysis of LHCb Run 2 data for three decay channels: Bₛ⁰ → J/ψπ⁺π⁻ (to extract φₛ), B⁰ → J/ψρ⁰(770) (to measure Δφₛ and observe CPV), and Bₛ⁰ → Kₛμ⁺μ⁻ (to determine time-dependent CP asymmetry parameters C and S).

Result: φₛ = −77 ± 34(stat) ± 7(syst) mrad (improved in this channel); global LHCb average φₛ = −41 ± 17 mrad; first CPV observation in B⁰ → J/ψρ⁰ with 2βᵉᶠᶠ_cᶜᵈ = 0.710 ± 0.084 ± 0.051 rad; Δφₛ = 5.0 ± 4.6 mrad (most stringent constraint); first C = −0.13 ± 0.32(stat) ± 0.04(syst), S = +0.82 ± 0.29(stat) ± 0.05(syst) for Bₛ⁰ → Kₛμ⁺μ⁻.

Conclusion: All results are consistent with Standard Model predictions within uncertainties; the measurements significantly improve precision on φₛ and provide pioneering constraints on penguin effects and CPV in rare b → sℓ⁺ℓ⁻ transitions, enhancing sensitivity to potential new physics.

Abstract: These proceedings present the latest measurements of time-dependent properties of $b$-hadrons using the LHCb Run 2 data set. Three results are presented. The first of these is a measurement of the CKM phase $\phi_s$ with $B_s^0\to J/\psi\pi^+\pi^-$. This result improves the determination in this channel, obtaining a result of $\phi_s = -77\pm34(\text{stat})\pm7(\text{syst})$ mrad. This result is included in the LHCb global average to obtain $\phi_s = -41\pm17$ mrad. Additionally, a determination of the penguin contributions, $\Delta\phi_s$, to measurements of $\phi_s$ using $B^0\to J/\psi\rho^0(770)$ is presented. This measurement constitutes the first observation of CP-violation in $B_0 \to J/\psi\rho^0(770)$ decays. The CKM phase $2\beta$ is measured and is determined to be $2\beta^{\text{eff}}_{ccd} = 0.710\pm0.084\pm0.051$ rad. The most stringent constraint on penguin contributions is obtained and determined to be $\Delta\phi_s = 5.0\pm4.6$ mrad. Finally, the first measurement of time-dependent CP-violation in a $b\to s l^+ l^-$ process using the decay $B_s^0 \to K_S \mu^+ \mu^- $ is presented. The time-dependent coefficients of this decay are determined to be $C=-0.13\pm0.32(\text{stat})\pm0.04(\text{syst})$ and $S=+0.82\pm0.29(\text{stat})\pm0.05(\text{syst})$.

</details>


### [101] [Neural Bayesian Online Calibration with Deficient Simulators for MADMAX Dark Matter Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26343)
*Nabil Salama*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Introduces a Bayesian online calibration framework that jointly learns simulator discrepancies and infers latent scientific quantities using measurable outputs, applied to calibrate a deficient simulator in the MADMAX dark matter experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scientific experiments suffer from simulator deficiencies and hardware-command–state mismatches, hindering reliable optimization and inference—especially when key quantities (e.g., axion-signal boost) are unobservable.

Method: Combines pre-calibration surrogate modeling of state-dependent simulator parameters with amortized Bayesian inference via normalizing flows; during online calibration, propagates measurement/positioning uncertainties and selects hardware commands using a trade-off between expected improvement and information gain.

Result: Enables joint calibration of a deficient disk-booster simulator and inference of the latent axion-signal power boost factor using only measured reflectivity in MADMAX; experimental validation is ongoing.

Conclusion: The framework bridges simulator imperfections and physical reality in real-time experimental control, supporting robust inference of otherwise inaccessible scientific parameters.

Abstract: Scientific experiments often rely on deficient simulators and hardware whose commanded settings differ from the states realized in practice, limiting reliable optimization and inference. A Bayesian online calibration framework is introduced for experiments with at least one directly measurable simulator output and potentially latent quantities of scientific interest. Pre-calibration data are used to learn state-dependent effective simulator parameters, while a normalizing flow enables amortized posterior inference from measurement histories. During calibration, measurement and positioning uncertainties are propagated, and hardware commands are selected by balancing expected improvement with information gain. The framework is applied to the MADMAX dark matter experiment, where measured reflectivity is used to calibrate a deficient disk-booster simulator and infer the unobservable axion-signal power boost factor. Experimental validation on the MADMAX experiment is ongoing.

</details>


### [102] [Measurement of the relative phase between the strong and electromagnetic amplitudes in $\boldsymbol{J/ψ\to Σ^{+} \barΣ^{-}}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26673)
*BESIII Collaboration,M. Ablikim,M. N. Achasov,P. Adlarson,X. C. Ai,C. S. Akondi,R. Aliberti,A. Amoroso,Q. An,Y. H. An,M. S. Anderson,Y. Bai,O. Bakina,H. R. Bao,X. L. Bao,M. Barbagiovanni,V. Batozskaya,K. Begzsuren,N. Berger,M. Berlowski,M. B. Bertani,D. Bettoni,F. Bianchi,E. Bianco,A. Bortone,I. Boyko,R. A. Briere,A. Brueggemann,D. Cabiati,H. Cai,M. H. Cai,X. Cai,A. Calcaterra,G. F. Cao,N. Cao,S. A. Cetin,X. Y. Chai,J. F. Chang,T. T. Chang,G. R. Che,Y. Z. Che,C. H. Chen,Chao Chen,G. Chen,H. S. Chen,H. Y. Chen,M. L. Chen,S. J. Chen,S. M. Chen,T. Chen,W. Chen,X. R. Chen,X. T. Chen,X. Y. Chen,Y. B. Chen,Y. Q. Chen,Z. K. Chen,J. Cheng,L. N. Cheng,S. K. Choi,X. Chu,G. Cibinetto,F. Cossio,J. Cottee-Meldrum,H. L. Dai,J. P. Dai,X. C. Dai,A. Dbeyssi,R. E. de Boer,D. Dedovich,C. Q. Deng,Z. Y. Deng,A. Denig,I. Denisenko,M. Destefanis,F. De Mori,E. Di Fiore,X. X. Ding,Y. Ding,Y. X. Ding,J. Dong,L. Y. Dong,M. Y. Dong,X. Dong,Z. J. Dong,M. C. Du,S. X. Du,Shaoxu Du,X. L. Du,Y. Q. Du,Y. Y. Duan,Z. H. Duan,P. Egorov,G. F. Fan,J. J. Fan,Y. H. Fan,J. Fang,Jin Fang,S. S. Fang,W. X. Fang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First experimental measurement of the strong-electromagnetic relative phase in J/ψ → Σ⁺Σ̄⁻, yielding (107.9 ± 24.9)° or (−107.6 ± 24.3)°, with branching fraction ~1.16×10⁻³; provides key input for pQCD and SU(3) flavor symmetry tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally determine the strong-electromagnetic relative phase in J/ψ → Σ⁺Σ̄⁻ decays — a quantity inaccessible to theory alone and critical for testing perturbative QCD and SU(3) flavor symmetry.

Method: Analyzed 284 pb⁻¹ of e⁺e⁻ collision data collected by BESIII at √s = 3.00–3.12 GeV; extracted the relative phase via angular distribution analysis of the decay, accounting for interference between strong and electromagnetic amplitudes.

Result: Relative phase: (107.9 ± 24.9)° or (−107.6 ± 24.3)°; branching fraction: (1.14 ± 0.04)×10⁻³ (positive phase) or (1.19 ± 0.04)×10⁻³ (negative phase).

Conclusion: This is the first experimental determination of the strong–electromagnetic phase difference in J/ψ → hyperon–antihyperon decays; results constrain pQCD calculations and SU(3) symmetry breaking effects.

Abstract: Using 284 pb$^{-1}$ of $e^{+}e^{-}$ collision data collected with the BESIII detector at center-of-mass energies between 3.00 and 3.12 GeV, the relative phase between the strong and electromagnetic interaction amplitudes in the process $J/\psi \to \Sigma^+ \bar{\Sigma}^-$ is determined. The relative phase is measured to be $(107.9 \pm 24.9)^{\circ}$ or $(-107.6 \pm 24.3)^{\circ}$, where the uncertainty includes both statistical and systematic sources. This is the first experimental determination of the strong-electromagnetic phase difference in $J/\psi$ decays to a hyperon-antihyperon pair. The branching fraction $\mathcal{B}_{J/\psi \to \Sigma^+ \bar{\Sigma}^-}$ is measured to be $(1.14 \pm 0.04)\times 10^{-3}$ for the positive phase solution or $(1.19 \pm 0.04) \times 10^{-3}$ for the negative phase solution. These results provide important input for testing perturbative QCD predictions and studies of SU(3) flavor symmetry.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [103] [T-embeddings as stationary measures on T-graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24180)
*Mikhail Basok,Benoit Laslier,Marianna Russkikh*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a classification of t-embeddings via positive stationary measures on associated T-graphs and provides a reconstruction method; it further proposes a general construction technique for t-embeddings of non-degenerate planar bipartite graphs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and classify t-embeddings through their relation with T-graphs, and to enable systematic construction of t-embeddings for broad classes of planar bipartite graphs.

Method: The authors prove a classification theorem linking t-embeddings to positive stationary measures on T-graphs, and devise an explicit reconstruction procedure from such measures; they extend this framework to construct t-embeddings for arbitrary sufficiently non-degenerate planar bipartite graphs.

Result: A complete classification of t-embeddings by positive stationary measures on T-graphs is established; a constructive algorithm for recovering t-embeddings from these measures is provided; and a general method for building t-embeddings for wide classes of planar bipartite graphs is developed.

Conclusion: T-graphs and their stationary measures provide a unifying and constructive framework for t-embeddings, enabling both theoretical classification and practical construction for diverse planar bipartite graphs.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the relation between t-embeddings and T-graphs associated with the same weighted planar bipartite graph. We prove that, given such a T-graph, the corresponding t-embeddings are classified by positive stationary measures on this T-graph and describe a reconstruction procedure of the t-embedding given such a measure. As an application, we develop an approach for constructing t-embeddings of arbitrary sufficiently non-degenerate planar bipartite graphs.

</details>


### [104] [An Accurate Zeroth-Order Perturbation Theory for Solving Power-Law Potentials within the Framework of the Asymptotic Iteration Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24805)
*A. J. Sous,Ibsal Assi,Nasser Saad*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a zeroth-order perturbative variant of the asymptotic iteration method (AIM) using exponential/hyperbolic ansätze to solve the Schrödinger equation for spherically symmetric singular potentials—avoiding oscillatory AIM failures and yielding accurate results for quartic/sextic singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard asymptotic iteration method (AIM) exhibits oscillatory, non-convergent behavior when applied to spherically symmetric singular potentials; a robust alternative is needed for atomic/molecular physics applications.

Method: Develops a zeroth-order perturbation theory within AIM framework: substitutes exponential or hyperbolic potential ansätze with tunable small free parameters; treats deviation from original singular potential as a small perturbation; solves resulting wave equation analytically at zeroth order.

Result: Accurate eigenenergies and wavefunctions for quartic and sextic singular potentials; eliminates spurious oscillations inherent in standard AIM; numerical results agree well with those from other established methods.

Conclusion: The proposed zeroth-order perturbative AIM with tailored ansätze provides a stable, accurate, and analytically tractable approach for singular central potentials—extending the applicability and reliability of AIM in quantum mechanical bound-state problems.

Abstract: Spherically symmetric singular potentials have a wide range of applications, such as molecular and atomic physics. In this work, we present a zeroth-order perturbation theory for solving the Schrödinger equation within the framework of the asymptotic iteration method (AIM). We rely on exponential and hyperbolic potential ansatz substitutions, where the difference between the original and new potential is treated as a small perturbation. Each potential ansatz has a free parameter chosen small enough to give an accurate zeroth-order perturbative wave equation solution. This method avoids the oscillatory behavior observed in the asymptotic iteration solutions when applying the AIM to such problems. Finally, we illustrate our approach by solving a few problems, particularly potentials with quartic and sextic singularities. We compare our results with those obtained via other methods and find good agreement.

</details>


### [105] [Eynard-Orantin Topological Recursion, Moduli-Space Virasoro Constraints, and Semisimple CohFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24980)
*Dingkang Xiong,Jian Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses Virasoro constraints to prove Eynard’s intersection-number formula for topological recursion with multiple simple ramification points, links the construction to Givental’s quantized R-matrix, and generalizes the DOSS correspondence by showing y-variation corresponds to ancestor potential evaluation point shifts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a Virasoro-theoretic proof of Eynard's intersection-number formula and extend the DOSS identification of topological recursion with Givental's formalism beyond fixed y.

Method: Applies Virasoro constraints; couples higher Weil-Petersson partition functions via a quadratic differential operator built from the Bergman kernel; identifies this operator with the quadratic part of Givental's quantized R-matrix.

Result: A rigorous Virasoro-based derivation of Eynard's formula; confirmation that the Bergman kernel–derived operator equals the quadratic part of Givental's R-matrix; demonstration that varying the y-function (with fixed Bergman kernel/R-matrix) corresponds to shifting the evaluation point of the ancestor potential.

Conclusion: The paper unifies topological recursion and Givental theory via Virasoro constraints, strengthens the DOSS correspondence by revealing its geometric flexibility in y, and clarifies the role of the Bergman kernel as encoding the R-matrix data.

Abstract: We use Virasoro constraints to prove Eynard's intersection-number formula for Eynard-Orantin topological recursion with several simple ramification points. The key construction couples the higher Weil-Petersson partition functions associated with the individual ramification points through a quadratic differential operator determined by the Bergman kernel. We identify this operator with the quadratic part of Givental's quantized $R$-matrix, thereby obtaining a Virasoro-theoretic proof of the DOSS identification of topological recursion with Givental's formula for semisimple cohomological field theories. Furthermore, while the DOSS identification fixes the distinguished $y$-function, we keep the Bergman kernel/$R$-matrix fixed and allow $y$ to vary, showing that this amounts to shifting the evaluation point of the corresponding ancestor potential.

</details>


### [106] [Spectral Curves for Semisimple Conformal Frobenius Manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24985)
*Dingkang Xiong,Jian Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs spectral curves for semisimple conformal Frobenius manifolds with flat unit via periods of the second structure equation; Eynard–Orantin topological recursion on these curves recovers Givental–Teleman ancestor correlators; explicit spectral curve data are obtained for quantum cohomology of ℙⁿ (n≥2), expressed via Hecke triangle groups and hypergeometric functions, and fully in modular/quasimodular forms for ℙ² and ℙ³.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a geometric and analytic bridge between conformal Frobenius manifolds (e.g., quantum cohomology) and topological recursion by constructing canonical spectral curves whose recursion reproduces Givental–Teleman reconstruction data.

Method: Construction of connected spectral curves on simply connected domains using periods of the second structure equation; application of Eynard–Orantin topological recursion; explicit computation for ℙⁿ via monodromy analysis, Hecke triangle groups, and hypergeometric solutions; modular parametrization for ℙ² and ℙ³ using classical modular and quasimodular forms.

Result: Spectral curves are constructed for all semisimple conformal Frobenius manifolds with flat unit; topological recursion on them exactly reproduces stable ancestor correlators; explicit spectral curve data—including meromorphic differentials and bidifferentials—are obtained for ℙⁿ (n≥2), fully modular for ℙ² and ℙ³.

Conclusion: The spectral curve framework unifies Frobenius manifold geometry, topological recursion, and modular structures; it provides an effective computational tool for Gromov–Witten invariants and reveals deep connections to automorphic forms and integrable systems.

Abstract: We construct connected spectral curves for semisimple conformal Frobenius manifolds with flat unit, using the periods of the second structure equation. Eynard-Orantin topological recursion on these curves reproduces the stable ancestor correlators of Givental-Teleman reconstruction. The spectral curves are defined on simply connected domains in the complex plane. We apply the construction to the quantum cohomology of $\mathbb P^n$ for $n\ge2$ at a point, obtaining explicit spectral curve data in terms of Hecke triangle groups and hypergeometric functions. Finally, for $\mathbb P^2$ and $\mathbb P^3$, we express the complete spectral curve data, including the bidifferentials, in classical modular and quasimodular forms.

</details>


### [107] [Thoughts on Quantum Events, Quantizing Gravity and von Neumann Algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25010)
*Stephen Bruce Sontz*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel approach to quantum gravity by redefining quantum events as fundamental kinematic entities and suggesting von Neumann algebras as their mathematical framework; it advocates quantizing special relativistic kinematics (Minkowski spacetime) before tackling full general relativity, and clarifies wavefunction collapse via quantum events.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a viable path toward quantizing gravity by reconceptualizing quantum theory around well-defined quantum events—absent in standard formulations—and integrating them with relativistic kinematics.

Method: Introduces a new quantum-theoretic framework grounded in rigorously defined quantum events; argues that the appropriate algebraic structure encoding sufficient quantum events is a von Neumann algebra; proposes quantizing Minkowski spacetime (kinematics of special relativity) as a prerequisite to quantizing Einstein's equations; interprets quantum events as kinematic, not dynamical, elements.

Result: A conceptual framework wherein quantum events serve as primitive kinematic objects, von Neumann algebras model quantum systems, and relativistic spacetime kinematics becomes a candidate for quantization—yielding a coherent reinterpretation of wavefunction collapse in the Appendix.
