Conclusion: Quantum gravity may be approached by elevating quantum events to foundational kinematic status and quantizing relativistic spacetime structure first; this reframing resolves conceptual ambiguities in standard quantum theory (e.g., collapse) and offers a mathematically grounded pathway toward unifying quantum mechanics and gravity.

Abstract: This short document presents no new results. It is a collection of related ideas that may lead to a way of quantizing gravity. To achieve this it may be relevant to use my recent new approach to quantum theory, which is based on quantum events. Given that most standard quantum texts do not define a mathematical object which corresponds to a physical event, the definition of a quantum event is motivated and discussed in detail. This in turn leads to the idea that the mathematical object that contains enough quantum events for describing a quantum system is a von Neumann algebra. The next idea is that the kinematics of special relativity (as embodied in flat Minkowski space) should be quantized as a first step in the program of the quantization of general relativity, that is, quantizing Einstein's equation. It must be emphasized that the events of special relativity are viewed as part of the kinematics, rather than of the dynamics of that theory. The same holds for general relativity. Similarly, I am viewing quantum events as part of the kinematics of quantum theory, even though some find this to be counterintuitive. An Appendix shows how ``collapse'' is easily understood in this new approach to quantum theory.

</details>


### [108] [Complementary Cauchy Elasticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25218)
*Arash Yavari*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a nonlinear theory of complementary Cauchy elasticity—defining strain as an implicit function of stress—without requiring invertibility or a complementary strain-energy potential; it characterizes obstructions to hyperelasticity via major symmetry failure of the tangent compliance tensor, links integrability of stress- and complementary stress-work 1-forms to Lagrangian structure of the constitutive manifold, provides canonical local forms (anisotropic/isotropic) via Darboux classification, treats constraints (e.g., pentamodes) using stress equivalence classes modulo reactive stresses, formulates balance laws and thermodynamics covariantly—with angular momentum emerging directly from energy balance covariance—and shows that non-hyperelastic nonlinear solids can yield hyperelastic linearized responses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize elasticity beyond hyperelasticity by constructing a rigorous nonlinear framework for complementary Cauchy elasticity—where strain is implicitly defined as a function of stress—without assuming constitutive invertibility or existence of a complementary energy potential, and to characterize the geometric and algebraic obstructions to hyperelasticity.

Method: Uses differential geometry and exterior calculus: introduces the complementary stress-work 1-form; analyzes integrability conditions via closure of stress-work and complementary stress-work 1-forms; applies Darboux classification to obtain canonical local representations; formulates constrained constitutive relations on quotient spaces of stress modulo reactive stresses; employs covariant formulation of balance laws and thermodynamics; performs linearization about finitely deformed, prestressed configurations.

Result: 1) Obstruction to complementary hyperelasticity is precisely the failure of major symmetry in the tangent compliance tensor; 2) Simultaneous closure of both stress-work 1-forms is equivalent to the constitutive manifold being Lagrangian; 3) Local canonical forms for anisotropic and isotropic complementary elastic solids are derived, including explicit non-hyperelastic examples; 4) Internally constrained models (e.g., pentamodes) are naturally expressed on stress equivalence classes; 5) Covariant energy balance implies angular momentum balance automatically; 6) Non-hyperelastic nonlinear materials may exhibit hyperelastic linearized responses.

Conclusion: Complementary Cauchy elasticity provides a geometrically consistent, non-invertible generalization of hyperelasticity; its structure is governed by symplectic and Lagrangian geometry of the constitutive manifold; constraints and linearization behave robustly within this framework; and the theory unifies and extends classical and implicit elasticity paradigms.

Abstract: We develop a nonlinear theory of complementary Cauchy elasticity in which the right Cauchy-Green strain is prescribed as a function of the second Piola-Kirchhoff stress, without assuming constitutive invertibility or the existence of a complementary strain-energy function. We introduce the complementary stress-work 1-form and characterize the obstruction to complementary hyperelasticity through the failure of major symmetry of the tangent compliance tensor. For general implicit elasticity, we show that the simultaneous closure of the stress-work and complementary stress-work 1-forms is characterized by the Lagrangian structure of the constitutive manifold. Using the Darboux classification, we obtain local canonical representations of anisotropic and isotropic complementary Cauchy elastic solids, including explicit non-hyperelastic examples. We investigate kinematic and stress constraints, including applications to pentamode solids, and show that internally constrained constitutive relations are naturally formulated on equivalence classes of stresses modulo reactive stresses. We formulate the balance laws and thermodynamics covariantly and establish that covariance of the energy balance directly yields the balance of angular momentum in complementary Cauchy elasticity, unlike in conventional Cauchy elasticity. Finally, we linearize implicit, Cauchy, and complementary Cauchy elasticity about finitely deformed and prestressed configurations and show that nonlinear non-hyperelastic solids may possess hyperelastic linearized responses.

</details>


### [109] [Response theory at low temperature for weakly interacting quantum spin systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25249)
*Joscha Henheik,Marius Lemm,Tom Wessel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends adiabatic and response theory to weakly interacting quantum spin systems at positive temperature—non-Fermi systems—by leveraging the locality of local indistinguishability, recently proven via cluster expansions and a probabilistic swapping trick.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize rigorous adiabatic theory beyond fermionic lattice systems (as in Greenblatt et al., 2024) to quantum spin systems, which lack Fermi statistics and require new structural insights for positive-temperature response theory.

Method: Uses cluster expansion techniques combined with a probabilistic swapping trick to establish local indistinguishability; exploits its locality to perform low-temperature adiabatic expansions and derive linear response theory for weakly interacting quantum spin chains/lattices.

Result: First rigorous derivation of response theory at positive temperature for weakly interacting quantum spin systems, enabled by the locality of local indistinguishability.

Conclusion: Local indistinguishability—previously shown for such spin systems—is not only a structural property but a functional tool enabling adiabatic and response analysis at positive temperature, thus broadening the scope of rigorous quantum statistical mechanics beyond fermionic models.

Abstract: Recent work by Greenblatt, Lange, Marcelli, and Porta (Commun. Math. Phys. 2024) provides the first rigorous justification of adiabatic theory for interacting quantum systems at positive temperature. Their analysis treats weakly interacting lattice fermions and combines a rigorous Wick rotation with fermionic cluster expansion techniques. Here, we study a class of weakly interacting quantum spin systems and derive response theory at positive temperature, thereby covering non-Fermi systems for the first time. Our proof identifies and exploits the locality property of local indistinguishability as a key ingredient in carrying out low-temperature adiabatic expansions. The fact that these systems satisfy local indistinguishability was recently established jointly with Adhikari (arXiv:2508.15907) using cluster expansion methods together with a probabilistic swapping trick.

</details>


### [110] [Upper Bound on the Correlation Energy of a Dense Fermi Gas in the Thermodynamic Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25262)
*Niels Benedikter,Sascha Lill,Marcello Porta,Benjamin Schlein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that the random phase approximation (RPA) gives an upper bound on the correlation energy of a weakly interacting 3D Fermi gas at high density and in the thermodynamic limit, using a quasi-bosonic Bogoliubov transformation of localized particle–hole excitations near the Fermi surface.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the random phase approximation for the correlation energy in a many-body fermionic system, particularly in the high-density, weak-coupling regime where mean-field theory fails to capture quantum correlations accurately.

Method: Constructs a correlated trial state via a quasi-bosonic Bogoliubov transformation acting on collective particle–hole excitations associated with patches on the Fermi surface; controls spatial localization of these excitations and employs an iterated Duhamel expansion to estimate expectation values of localized number operators, enabling reduction to and diagonalization of an effective quadratic Hamiltonian.

Result: Establishes that RPA yields an upper bound on the correlation energy for a 3D weakly interacting Fermi gas in the thermodynamic limit under mean-field scaling ($\rho^{-2/3}$ for kinetic, $\rho^{-1}$ for interaction terms).

Conclusion: The RPA is not merely a heuristic tool but admits a rigorous variational justification in this regime, confirming its asymptotic correctness for the correlation energy up to an upper bound.

Abstract: We prove that the random phase approximation provides an upper bound on the correlation energy of a three-dimensional, weakly interacting, Fermi gas in the thermodynamic limit at high density. More precisely, we consider a mean-field scaling regime where the kinetic and interaction Hamiltonian are scaled by $\rho^{-2/3}$ and $\rho^{-1}$, respectively. We use a correlated trial state constructed through a quasi-bosonic Bogoliubov transformation of collective particle--hole excitations associated to patches on the Fermi surface. Key of the proof is to control the position-space localization of the collective excitations. Combined with an iterated Duhamel expansion of the expectation values of (localized) particle number operators we obtain extensive estimates, which justify the reduction to an effective quadratic Hamiltonian and its approximate diagonalization.

</details>


### [111] [De Finetti Structures in Networks of Quantum Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25280)
*Gavin Hull,Marco Merkli*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a de Finetti-type representation for the reduced state of a fixed-size subsystem S embedded in a large, generic quantum network (graph), showing convergence to a convex combination of symmetric product states as the reservoir size N−K → ∞ — despite the system’s inherent non-exchangeability; it provides explicit construction, convergence rates, and applications including an area law for entanglement entropy and quantitative bounds on information-theoretic quantities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard quantum de Finetti theorems require exchangeability (permutation symmetry), but realistic quantum networks (e.g., lattice or random graphs) lack such symmetry. The authors aim to extend de Finetti representations to non-exchangeable, structured quantum many-body systems where only local connectivity between a small subsystem S and a large reservoir R is assumed.

Method: The authors employ graph-theoretic analysis combined with quantum information techniques: they exploit generic connectivity conditions (e.g., expansion or mixing properties) between S and R, use moment methods and concentration inequalities (especially for Erdős–Rényi graphs), construct explicit de Finetti measures via symmetrization over reservoir permutations conditioned on fixed S–R couplings, and derive trace-norm convergence bounds using combinatorial counting and operator norm estimates.

Result: They prove that, under mild genericity assumptions on the graph’s S–R connectivity, the reduced state ρ_S converges in trace norm to a de Finetti state — a convex mixture of K-fold product states — as N→∞. For Erdős–Rényi graphs G(N,p) with p constant, convergence is almost sure and quantified with rate O(1/√N). They derive an area law: S–R entanglement entropy scales with the boundary size |∂S|, not volume; obtain mean-field (O(1)) and fluctuation (O(1/√N)) scalings for interaction observables; and provide explicit O(1/N) bounds on deviations of von Neumann entropy, mutual information, and intra-/inter-system entanglement from their de Finetti limits.

Conclusion: De Finetti structure emerges universally in large quantum networks without exchange symmetry, provided sufficient connectivity between subsystem and reservoir. This justifies mean-field approximations, enables rigorous area laws, and furnishes quantitative tools for analyzing entanglement and correlations in structured quantum many-body systems — bridging statistical mechanics, quantum information, and random graph theory.

Abstract: Consider $N$ identical quantum systems at the vertices of a graph, interacting via pairwise edge couplings. We designate $K$ vertices as a system of interest $\mathrm S$ and the remaining $N-K$ as a reservoir $\mathrm R$. Under generic connectivity assumptions between $\mathrm S$ and $\mathrm R$, we show that for $N\to\infty$, the reduced state of $\mathrm S$ converges to a convex combination of symmetric product states of $K$ single-vertex density matrices -- called a de Finetti representation. Unlike in standard derivations for symmetric (exchangeable) systems, we establish de Finetti representations for quantum networks, which are inherently non-exchangeable. We construct the representations explicitly and quantify the convergence rate. We show that Erdős--Rényi random graphs exhibit the de Finetti structure: as the reservoir grows, the reduced state of any fixed finite subgraph converges almost surely to an explicit deterministic product state. As applications, we establish a general area law for the entanglement entropy of $\mathrm S$, analyze mean-field and fluctuation scalings of the $\mathrm S$-$\mathrm R$ interaction, and illustrate our results on qubit networks. We also derive quantitative convergence estimates for information-theoretic quantities, including von Neumann entropy, mutual information, and entanglement within $\mathrm S$ and between $\mathrm S$ and $\mathrm R$.

</details>


### [112] [Symmetry algebra of a charged harmonic oscillator in the field of the Dirac magnetic monopole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25408)
*Ian Marquette,Luc Vinet*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies and characterizes the hidden symmetry algebra of a charged particle in a harmonic potential bound to a Dirac magnetic monopole, revealing a polynomial (cubic) Heun–Racah algebra generated by angular momentum J and a shifted rank-2 conserved tensor T', with representation-theoretic classification including both physical states and the kernel of the Newman–Penrose operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the full symmetry algebra generated by the conserved rank-2 tensor T and angular momentum J for a monopole-bound harmonic oscillator — a problem left open for 35 years — and to understand its structure beyond rotational invariance, especially given the anomalous degeneracies tied to the integer charge product q.

Method: Covariant algebraic analysis of commutation relations among J, the conserved tensor T, and its shift T' = T + 2(H−1)J^(2); identification of the resulting polynomial algebra as cubic (Heun–Racah type); abstract definition and classification of multiplicity-free finite-dimensional representations using dynamical ladder operators; extension to both integer and half-integer magnetic/electric charges.

Result: The symmetry algebra is a cubic Heun–Racah algebra generated by (J², T'_{zz}) in each energy–J_z sector; T' is uniquely chosen so all commutators are polynomials in J; at q=0, T reduces to the algebraic Heun operator of (Casimir of so(3), quadrupole of su(3)); representations include both physical oscillator levels and a second family supported on the Newman–Penrose kernel.

Conclusion: The system exhibits a rich, non-quadratic hidden symmetry governed by a Heun–Racah algebra, unifying monopole harmonics, oscillator degeneracies, and algebraic Heun operators; the algebra is fully determined by the ladder-operator dynamical algebra and holds universally for integer and half-integer charges.

Abstract: A charged particle bound by an isotropic harmonic potential to a Dirac magnetic monopole, with the centrifugal term that makes the orbits close, has degeneracies beyond rotational invariance: for integer $q$, the product of the electric and magnetic charges, the levels are those of the oscillator with the states of angular momentum $l<|q|$ removed. The conserved rank-2 tensor $T$ built from the ladder operators that accounts for them was identified thirty-five years ago, but the algebra it generates with the angular momentum $\mathbf{J}$ was not obtained. We obtain its relations in covariant form and identify it. It is polynomial but not quadratic, and the shift $T'=T+2(H-1)J^{(2)}$ by the quadrupole of $\mathbf{J}$, and only this one, makes all commutators polynomials in $\mathbf{J}$. In each sector of fixed energy and $J_z$, $(\mathbf{J}^2,T'_{zz})$ generates a cubic algebra of Heun--Racah type whose parameters are symmetric, up to a scalar shift, in the magnetic charge and the magnetic quantum number. At $q=0$, $T$ is the algebraic Heun operator of the pair (Casimir of $\mathfrak{so}(3)$, quadrupole of $\mathfrak{su}(3)$): the symmetry algebra is the $\mathfrak{so}(3)$-covariant extension of a Heun--Racah algebra. We define it abstractly and classify its multiplicity-free finite-dimensional representations at the energies of the system: besides the levels, they include, for nonzero integer $q$, a second family carried by the states the monopole removes, the kernel of the Newman--Penrose operator. All relations are derived from the dynamical algebra of the ladder operators and hold for integer and half-integer charges alike.

</details>


### [113] [Upper bounds on the number of images produced by point-mass lenses at different distances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25594)
*J. An*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives and compares upper bounds on the maximum number of images produced by multi-plane gravitational lensing with point-mass lenses, showing that the classical bound is 2·3^(n−1), shear increases it to 4^n, and a refined BKK bound—though tighter than Perry’s—is still quadratic and likely non-sharp.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine sharp upper bounds on the number of images in multi-plane point-mass gravitational lensing, improving upon existing bounds (e.g., Perry's) and accounting for physical effects like lensing shear.

Method: Uses algebraic geometry—specifically the Bernstein–Khovanskii–Kushnirenko (BKK) theorem—to compute the sparse resultant degree of the lens equation system; compares with classical counting arguments and Perry’s bound; analyzes dependence on number of lenses per plane and presence of shear.

Result: Derives a recurrence for the BKK bound, proves it is strictly less than Perry’s bound for n ≥ 2, shows shear raises the maximum image count from 2·3^(n−1) to 4^n, and argues the true sharp bound is likely linear in total lens count—not quadratic.

Conclusion: The BKK bound provides a tighter algebraic upper limit than Perry’s for multi-plane point-mass lensing, but remains overly conservative; the physically relevant sharp bound is conjectured to scale linearly with the number of lenses, suggesting room for further refinement via geometric or combinatorial methods.

Abstract: If a light source is sequentially lensed by $n$ intervening point-mass lenses at distinct distances, the number of isolated images for a generic source position cannot be greater than $2\cdot 3^{n-1}$. If lensing shear is present in each lens plane, the image counts could be as large as $4^n$, however. For a general multi-plane point-mass lens system, we also devise the recurrence for the BKK bound on the image counts, which is strictly less than Perry's bound due to the resultant degree for $n\ge 2$. None the less, this bound is still quadratic in the number of lenses on each plane, so is likely to overestimate the true sharp bound, which is expected to grow linearly with the number of lenses.

</details>


### [114] [Wave-Particle Complementarity in Quantum Walk from a Quantum Mechanical Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26204)
*Norio Konno,Shigeki Matsutani,Etsuo Segawa,Tomoyuki Terada*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets the quantum walk as a quantum-mechanical model rooted in Feynman’s path integral formalism, demonstrates its dual particle–wave behavior (reflection and interference), and derives an explicit weak-limit distribution for the Hadamard walk with two-site initial states, revealing a Fraunhofer-like interference pattern modulated by 2cos²(ℓk(x)).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reevaluate quantum walks from a foundational quantum mechanical perspective—specifically as originating from Feynman’s path integral formulation—and to clarify their intrinsic particle–wave duality.

Method: Analytical derivation of the weak limit distribution for the Hadamard quantum walk with initial state supported on two symmetric lattice sites ±ℓ; use of Fourier analysis and path integral reasoning to identify interference structure and reflection behavior.

Result: Explicit weak-limit probability distribution showing Konno-type scaling modulated by interference factor 2cos²(ℓk(x)), yielding a far-field diffraction–like profile; demonstration of both particle-like (reflection/partial reflection) and wave-like (interference) features.

Conclusion: Quantum walks are not merely discrete analogs of diffusion but inherently quantum objects embodying wave–particle duality; their asymptotic statistics encode interference phenomena analogous to optical diffraction, reinforcing their physical interpretation beyond combinatorial or algorithmic contexts.

Abstract: A quantum walk is a mathematical model and a quantum version of a random walk. For the past two decades, it has been studied from mathematical and physical viewpoints. It has many desirable properties. This paper reevaluates quantum walk from a quantum mechanical perspective, as Feynman essentially proposed it as a toy model in his path integral method. Then we demonstrate that the quantum walk model exhibits both particle-like and wave-like properties by showing reflection and partial reflection as a particle and interference as a wave. In particular, for an initial state supported at two sites $\pm\ell$, we explicitly derive the weak limit distribution of the Hadamard walk and show that the Konno distribution is modulated by the interference factor $2\cos^2(\ell k(x))$. The resulting interference profile is reminiscent of far-field (Fraunhofer-type) diffraction.

</details>


### [115] [Two-Dimensional Euclidean Free Fermi Fields from Grassmannian Stochastic Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26329)
*Claudio Dappiaggi,Francesco Carlo De Vecchi,Pietro Tullio Falzoni,Paolo Rinaldi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a Euclidean counterpart of the massive two-dimensional free Dirac field using Grassmann-valued Gaussian white noise on a non-commutative probability space, yielding a tempered distributional Fermi field whose two-point functions match the Euclidean Dirac fundamental solution; it establishes time-zero fields via conditional expectation, proves compatibility with Wick ordering, and builds the time-translation semigroup with generators given by the one-particle Hamiltonian and its second quantization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a Euclidean Markov-field construction for the massive two-dimensional free Dirac field, inspired by Nelson's approach for bosonic fields.

Method: Constructs a Grassmann-valued Gaussian white noise on a non-commutative probability space; derives associated Brownian motion and stationary Ornstein–Uhlenbeck field; defines the Euclidean Fermi field as a tempered distribution with values in the Grassmann algebra; uses conditional expectation onto the past filtration to obtain time-zero fields; verifies compatibility with Wick ordering; constructs the Euclidean time-translation semigroup on time-zero fields and Wick sectors.

Result: A rigorously defined Euclidean massive 2D free Dirac field as a tempered distribution; correct mixed two-point functions matching the Euclidean Dirac fundamental solution; well-defined time-zero fields with covariance given by the reflected Euclidean Dirac kernel; preservation of Wick structure under conditional expectation; existence of a strongly continuous time-translation semigroup on time-zero fields and Wick sectors, generated by the one-particle Hamiltonian and its second quantization.

Conclusion: The paper successfully extends Nelson’s Euclidean Markov-field framework to fermionic fields—specifically, the massive two-dimensional free Dirac field—by leveraging non-commutative probability and Grassmann algebras, thereby providing a probabilistic–analytic foundation for Euclidean fermions compatible with canonical quantization and Wick calculus.

Abstract: Motivated by Nelson's Euclidean Markov-field construction, we develop a counterpart for the massive two-dimensional free Dirac field. Starting from a Gaussian white noise with values in a Grassmann algebra on a non-commutative probability space, we construct the associated Brownian motion and a stationary Ornstein--Uhlenbeck field. The resulting Euclidean Fermi field is realized as a tempered distribution on spacetime test functions, with values in the underlying algebra. Its mixed two-point functions reproduce the Euclidean Dirac fundamental solution. Time-zero fields are obtained by conditional expectation onto the past filtration generated by the noise, while their covariance is the reflected Euclidean Dirac kernel. We show that conditional expectation is compatible with Wick ordering and maps Euclidean Wick polynomials to the corresponding time-zero Wick polynomials. The Euclidean time-translation semigroup is then constructed on time-zero fields and Wick sectors, with generators given by the one-particle Hamiltonian and by its second quantization.

</details>


### [116] [Average lower bound on magnetic double-well tunneling with asymptotically large well separation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26637)
*Charles L. Fefferman,Say-Yeon Kwon,Jacob Shapiro,Michael I. Weinstein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes exponential lower bounds on the tunneling (hopping) coefficient and eigenvalue splitting for two identical, compactly supported potential wells in a weak perpendicular magnetic field, as their separation tends to infinity—without assuming symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand magnetic-field effects on quantum tunneling between distant, non-symmetric potential wells, particularly whether weak fields suppress or modify asymptotic decay rates of hopping integrals and spectral gaps.

Method: Combines the explicit Landau resolvent kernel with complex analytic estimates for logarithmic averages of the hopping coefficient; uses asymptotic analysis for large well separation and small magnetic field strength, avoiding symmetry assumptions.

Result: Proves that for sufficiently small b > 0, the hopping coefficient and lowest eigenvalue splitting are bounded below by exp(−bλd₁² − Cd₁¹⁺ᵟ) for any δ ∈ (0,1), outside a zero-density set of separations.

Conclusion: Weak magnetic fields induce a quadratic-in-distance Gaussian suppression term in the tunneling rate, superimposed on a sub-quadratic correction, and this behavior holds robustly without radial or reflection symmetry of the single-well potential.

Abstract: We study tunneling between two identical, smooth, compactly supported potential wells in a constant perpendicular magnetic field, with the coupling and magnetic field fixed and the well separation tending to infinity. For a sufficiently small positive magnetic field, we prove lower bounds on the hopping coefficient and on the splitting of the two lowest eigenvalues outside a set of separations of density zero. If the well centers are separated by $2d_1$ and the magnetic field strength is $b\lambda$, the lower bounds have the form $e^{-b\lambda d_1^2-Cd_1^{1+\delta}}$ for every fixed $\delta\in(0,1)$. No radial or reflection symmetry of the single-well potential is assumed. The proof combines the explicit Landau resolvent kernel with a complex analytic estimate for logarithmic averages of the hopping coefficient.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [117] [Light EFT Strings and Asymptotic Accelerated Expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24062)
*Veronica Collazuol,Alessandra Grieco,Irene Valenzuela*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the relationship between particle towers, EFT strings, and EFT membranes near infinite-distance limits in 4D N=1 theories, establishes lattice constraints on membrane scaling vectors, and rules out specific F-theory accelerating cosmologies due to EFT breakdown from light strings—closing a prior loophole—and discusses quantum corrections to the Kähler potential as a possible resolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand consistency conditions for effective field theories (EFTs) near infinite-distance limits in 4D N=1 supergravity, particularly regarding the interplay of towers of states (particles, strings, membranes), and to assess whether asymptotic accelerated expansion is viable in flux compactifications without violating swampland constraints.

Method: Analytical study of scaling behavior of EFT strings and membranes using lattice geometry in moduli space; application to F-theory and Type IIA Calabi-Yau compactifications; consistency checks via EFT validity (Hubble scale vs. lightest string mass); incorporation of recent quantum obstruction results affecting the Kähler potential asymptotics.

Result: Scaling vectors of EFT membranes lie in the lattice spanned by EFT string scaling vectors; explicit relative scalings derived in F-theory and Type IIA; two candidate F-theory accelerating trajectories ruled out because an EFT string becomes lighter than Hubble scale, invalidating 4D EFT; this closes the loophole in arXiv:2209.11821; quantum obstructions modify Kähler potential asymptotics, offering a concrete resolution mechanism.

Conclusion: The tower and membrane/string lattice structure reinforces swampland distance conjectures; asymptotic de Sitter-like acceleration is obstructed in the considered F-theory setups not by a universal no-go, but by dynamical EFT breakdown; quantum gravity effects altering moduli space geometry may restore consistency at infinite distance.

Abstract: We study the interplay among towers of particles, EFT strings, and EFT membranes near perturbative infinite-distance limits of four-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ theories. We show that the scaling vectors of EFT membranes lie in the lattice generated by those of the EFT strings, as previously found for particle towers, and determine their specific relative scaling in F-theory and Type IIA Calabi-Yau compactifications. We then leverage these objects to assess the viability of asymptotic accelerated expansion in flux compactifications. While our bottom-up analysis does not yield a general obstruction, we rule out two candidate accelerating trajectories in F-theory because an EFT string becomes lighter than the Hubble scale, invalidating the four-dimensional EFT. This closes the loophole found in arXiv:2209.11821. We also study how recent results on quantum obstructions to $\mathcal{N}=1$ infinite-distance limits may provide a concrete resolution by changing the asymptotic form of the Kähler potential.

</details>


### [118] [Angular Momentum Loss at Fourth Post-Minkowskian Order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24072)
*Alessandro Georgoudis,Carlo Heissenberg,Gustav Mogull,Rodolfo Russo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the total angular momentum loss in gravitational scattering of two non-spinning point masses at O(G⁴) using a hybrid amplitudes–worldline approach, separating radiated and static (memory/shear) contributions; results reproduce known low-velocity limits and yield the O(G⁵) conservative deflection angle at first self-force order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the full (radiative + static) angular momentum loss in binary gravitational scattering at high post-Minkowskian order (O(G⁴)), resolving infrared structure and enabling extraction of conservative dynamics (e.g., deflection) beyond current state-of-the-art.

Method: Hybrid method: (1) Construct integrand from two cut gravitational waveforms, with massive-line cuts inherited from worldline effective theory; (2) Glue waveforms and evaluate phase-space integrals via reverse unitarity (amplitudes technique); (3) Separate radiated angular momentum from static contribution (determined by initial shear and linear memory).

Result: Relativistic expressions for total angular momentum loss and infrared-finite mass dipole loss at O(G⁴); agreement with literature in low-velocity limit; derived conservative scattering angle at O(G⁵) including first self-force correction.

Conclusion: The angular momentum loss is fully determined at O(G⁴) by combining radiation and memory effects; the methodology bridges amplitudes and worldline frameworks, enabling robust extraction of conservative observables from radiative data, and provides a template for higher-order self-force calculations.

Abstract: We calculate the total loss of angular momentum for a gravitational scattering of two non-spinning point-like objects at $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$. Our methodology combines use of both amplitudes and worldline-based methods. We derive the integrand from two copies of the gravitational waveform with cuts on massive lines arising naturally from the worldline. We then glue these two copies and evaluate the resulting phase-space integrals using reverse unitarity, in the amplitudes spirit. We obtain separately the radiated angular momentum and the static contribution that, at this order, is entirely determined by the initial shear and the linear memory. Combining these two terms, we derive relativistic expressions for the angular momentum loss and for the infrared-finite component of the mass dipole loss, which in the low-velocity limit match results available in the literature. We also use this result to derive the conservative deflection angle at $\mathcal{O}(G^5)$ and first self-force order.

</details>


### [119] [On the Positivity of de Sitter Group Averaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24073)
*Donald Marolf,Xuyang Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves positivity of the group-averaging inner product for N-particle states in perturbative quantum gravity on global de Sitter space dS_d (d > 2), under the convergence condition Re ΣΔ_i > d−1; it also analyzes dS₂ as a toy model, finding that certain lower-weight states yield negative norms—raising concerns for consistency of perturbative dS quantum gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard Fock spaces for linearized quantum gravity on de Sitter space admit no normalizable non-vacuum states satisfying the higher-order gauge constraints (vanishing of all dS isometry charges); Higuchi proposed the group-averaging inner product as a remedy, but its positivity—essential for a physical Hilbert space—was unproven.

Method: The authors analyze the group-averaging construction over the de Sitter isometry group SO(d,1) applied to N-particle states built from global dS_d mode functions with conformal weights Δ_i; they establish positivity of the resulting inner product under absolute convergence (Re ΣΔ_i > d−1) for d > 2, and extend analysis to dS₂ for bosonic/fermionic fields, computing norms explicitly and identifying cases of negative norm.

Result: Positivity of the group-averaging inner product is rigorously proven for all N-particle states (arbitrary spin) in global dS_d (d > 2) satisfying Re ΣΔ_i > d−1; in dS₂, absolute convergence extends to smaller weights, but some such states yield strictly negative norms.

Conclusion: While group averaging yields a physically viable Hilbert space for sufficiently heavy (large-Re Δ_i) states in dS_d (d > 2), the appearance of negative norms for lighter states—even in dS₂—suggests potential inconsistencies or incompleteness in applying standard perturbative quantization to de Sitter quantum gravity, possibly signaling the need for non-perturbative input or modified boundary conditions.

Abstract: Perturbative quantum gravity about a global de Sitter (dS$_d$) background involves constraints that restrict the state of the linearized theory but which appear only at higher orders in perturbation theory. In particular, for familiar gravitational theories with $d>2$ these constraints require all charges defined by continuous de Sitter isometries to vanish in the linearized state. While standard Fock spaces have the property that the only normalizable state satisfying these constraints is the vacuum, Higuchi suggested that this lack of allowed states should be remedied by instead using the so-called group-averaging inner product. This inner product satisfies important uniqueness conditions, but its positivity has to date remained an open question. For $d > 2$, we establish this positivity here for all such states with $N$ particles (including particles of arbitrary spin) in standard global dS${}_d$ modes where the particle-weights $\Delta_i$ satisfy the condition ${\rm Re} \, \sum_{i=1}^N \Delta_i > d-1$, which is the criterion for group averaging to converge absolutely for arbitrary finite linear combinations of such states. We also treat the case of bosonic and fermionic (integer and half-integer spin) particles in dS$_2$ as a toy model for the more general case. However, we can identify certain classes of states for which group averaging continues to converge absolutely for smaller weights. Interestingly, in some cases these smaller-weight states turn out to have {\it negative} group-averaging norms. We thus also discuss the implications of this observation for perturbative de Sitter quantum gravity.

</details>


### [120] [Wrapping M5-branes on a non-spin manifold: Spin$^h$ structures and AdS$_7$ black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24084)
*Okan Günel,Cameron Gibson,C.N. Pope*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a supersymmetric AdS₂×M₅ solution in 7D maximal gauged supergravity with M₅ = SU(3)/SO(3) (Wu manifold), which lacks spin and spinᶜ structures but admits a spinʰ structure enabling supersymmetry; lifts it to an AdS₂×M₉ solution in D=11 supergravity where M₉ is the unit tangent bundle of the Wu manifold; extends it to a black hole with holographic interpretation; revisits and exactly solves flow equations for AdS₂×H³×H²; compares with AdS₂×S⁵ lift; and constructs a non-compact dual using SL(3,ℝ)/SO(3).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and understand supersymmetric AdS₂ solutions in lower-dimensional gauged supergravities whose internal spaces lack conventional spin structures—specifically the Wu manifold—and to explore their consistent uplifts to D=11, geometric/topological properties, supersymmetry realization via spinʰ structure, holographic interpretations, and dual non-compact geometries.

Method: Uses group-theoretic and differential-geometric analysis of homogeneous spaces; constructs explicit gauged supergravity solutions via consistent truncation and embedding tensor formalism; analyzes spin structure obstructions and introduces spinʰ structure to define Killing spinors; performs Kaluza–Klein uplift to D=11 using known sphere and coset uplift ansätze; studies topology of unit tangent bundles; solves BPS and flow equations analytically; constructs black hole deformations and non-compact duals via analytic continuation of coset metrics.

Result: A new supersymmetric AdS₂×SU(3)/SO(3) solution in 7D maximal gauged supergravity; its consistent uplift to AdS₂×M₉ in D=11 with M₉ = T¹(SU(3)/SO(3)); full characterization of M₉’s geometry and topology; demonstration that supersymmetry is realized via spinʰ structure; exact solution of the UV–IR flow for AdS₂×H³×H²; detailed comparison with AdS₂×S⁵ uplift showing analogous tangent-bundle structure but distinct topology and holonomy; construction of black hole extension and non-compact dual AdS₂×SL(3,ℝ)/SO(3).

Conclusion: The Wu manifold provides a viable, topologically exotic internal space for supersymmetric AdS₂ solutions despite lacking spin or spinᶜ structures; spinʰ structure furnishes the necessary framework for supersymmetry; its D=11 uplift yields a novel nine-manifold with rich geometry; the construction generalizes to black holes and non-compact duals, supporting broader applicability of spinʰ in holography and consistent truncations.

Abstract: We construct a solution of seven-dimensional maximal gauged supergravity of the form AdS$_2\times M_5$, where $M_5$ is the five-dimensional Wu manifold $SU(3)/SO(3)$. The solution is supersymmetric, despite the fact that the Wu manifold admits neither a spin structure nor a spin$^c$ structure. Rather, it admits a spin$^h$ structure, wherein the ill-definedness of ordinary spinors due to a minus sign in their holonomy is compensated by a further minus sign arising through the spinors carrying $SO(3)$ Yang-Mills charge, in a half-integer isospin representation of the $SU(2)$ double cover of the $SO(3)$. We show how the configuration can be lifted to a solution of $D=11$ supergravity of the form AdS$_2\times M_9$, where $M_9$ is the unit tangent bundle of the Wu manifold. We discuss the geometry and topology of $M_9$, and study the supersymmetry from the eleven-dimensional standpoint. We extend the seven-dimensional solution to a black hole, and discuss some aspects of its holographic interpretation and the UV to IR flow. We revisit a previously-known AdS$_2\times H^3\times H^2$ solution, and its UV to IR flow, showing that the flow equations in fact admit of an exact solution. Additionally, we give a detailed discussion of the lift of the AdS$_2\times S^5$ solution to $D=11$, exhibiting similarities and differences between the properties of its internal space $M_9$ (the unit tangent bundle of $S^5$) and the Wu manifold example of our main discussion. We also construct the non-compact dual of the Wu manifold solution, where $M_5$ is taken to be $SL(3,\mathbb R)/SO(3)$.

</details>


### [121] [Topological deformations of local gauge theories: a presymplectic BV-AKSZ approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24111)
*Ivan Dneprov,Alexey Donchenko,Maxim Grigoriev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'topological deformations'—a systematic method within the presymplectic BV-AKSZ framework—to relate physically equivalent field theories with different gauge structures (e.g., scalar ↔ 2-form duality, unimodular gravity) by gauging symmetries of generic degree, thereby modifying the presymplectic gauge PDE without adding local degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize known dualities (e.g., scalar–2-form, unimodular gravity) under a common geometric and cohomological principle, clarifying how differing gauge structures can describe the same local physics while exhibiting distinct global and interaction properties.

Method: Uses the presymplectic BV-AKSZ formalism to implement 'topological deformations': gauging degree-k symmetries (k = 0, 1) of a gauge PDE, coupling it to a BF-type topological sector; degree-0 gauging identifies globally symmetric solutions (recovering standard dualities), degree-1 gauging promotes constants (e.g., Λ) to global degrees of freedom.

Result: A unified construction of duality-related systems via deformation of the underlying presymplectic gauge PDE; explicit derivation of scalar–2-form duality and unimodular gravity as instances of degree-0 and degree-1 topological deformations, respectively; demonstration that such deformations preserve local degrees of freedom but alter global structure and consistent interactions.

Conclusion: Topological deformations provide a rigorous, cohomological classification of gauge-equivalent yet structurally distinct field theories; they explain how global vs. local status of parameters (e.g., cosmological constant) arises from symmetry gauging degree, and offer a framework for generating new consistent theories with controlled physical content.

Abstract: The same local degrees of freedom can be described by systems with different gauge structure and field content. Standard examples are the duality between the scalar field and the 2-form gauge field in four dimensions, and unimodular gravity, where the cosmological constant becomes a global degree of freedom. In a precise sense, the spaces of gauge-inequivalent formal solutions of such systems differ by a finite-dimensional sector. Moreover, because their gauge sectors are different, their consistent interactions and their sensitivity to the global structure of spacetime may differ substantially. We show that such systems are naturally related by what we call topological deformations: gauging a symmetry of generic degree couples the system to a BF-type sector without introducing new local degrees of freedom. The construction is carried out systematically within the presymplectic BV-AKSZ approach, where it amounts to a modification of the underlying presymplectic gauge PDE. Gauging degree-0 symmetries identifies solutions related by global symmetries, which reproduces the scalar--2-form and principal chiral model--Freedman--Townsend dualities. Gauging degree-1 symmetries, i.e. deformations, gives rise to new global degrees of freedom, such as the cosmological constant in unimodular gravity.

</details>


### [122] [Gravitational Cheshire effect from Yang-Mills instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24127)
*Borja Diez*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies dimensional reduction of 4D Yang-Mills instantons on Taub-NUT and Eguchi-Hanson backgrounds to 3D, revealing novel gravitational screening mechanisms: Taub-NUT yields fully stealth (gravitationally invisible) composite matter on flat 3D space, while Eguchi-Hanson yields partially stealth configurations with active Abelian gravity backreaction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover how gravitational invisibility (stealth) of 4D instantons manifests in lower dimensions, and to explore connections between 4D (anti-)self-duality and 3D BPS-like structures via Kaluza–Klein reduction.

Method: Kaluza–Klein dimensional reduction of Euclidean (anti-)self-dual Yang-Mills instantons along a U(1) isometry direction; explicit analysis on Taub-NUT and Eguchi-Hanson gravitational instanton backgrounds; decomposition of gauge connection into 3D YM field + adjoint Higgs, and metric into dilaton + Abelian gauge field; computation of gravitational backreaction from both Abelian and non-Abelian sectors.

Result: On Taub-NUT: full cancellation of gravitational backreaction in both Abelian and non-Abelian sectors → composite stealth configuration on flat ℝ³. On Eguchi-Hanson: non-Abelian sector backreaction cancels, but Abelian sector remains gravitationally active → deformed 3D geometry. Both cases establish a direct link between 4D self-duality and 3D BPS-type constraints.

Conclusion: Dimensional reduction of 4D self-dual gauge configurations can yield either fully or partially stealth matter in 3D, depending on the underlying gravitational instanton geometry—revealing a geometric origin for BPS-like screening and enriching the dictionary between 4D instanton physics and 3D gravitational solitons.

Abstract: We show that the dimensional reduction of four-dimensional Yang-Mills instantons naturally gives rise to nontrivial matter configurations in three dimensions exhibiting an unusual mechanism of gravitational screening. Starting from Euclidean Yang-Mills theory, we reduce (anti-)self-dual gauge configurations along a compact direction generated by a $U(1)$ isometry of the instanton background. Under this reduction, the gauge connection decomposes into a three-dimensional Yang-Mills field and an adjoint Higgs field, while the metric gives rise, as usual, to a dilaton and an Abelian gauge connection. For instantons on the Taub-NUT background, the reduced fields organize themselves into two mutually screening pairs: the backreaction of the Abelian sector cancels internally, and the same occurs independently in the non-Abelian sector. As a result, the full matter content supports a composite stealth configuration on flat three-dimensional space. By contrast, the dimensional reduction of instantons on the Eguchi-Hanson background yields a non-Abelian matter sector whose gravitational backreaction cancels, while the Abelian sector remains gravitationally active and therefore deforms the three-dimensional background geometry. These examples reveal a direct connection between four-dimensional (anti-)self-duality and three-dimensional BPS-type relations, and illustrate how instantonic sectors that are gravitationally invisible in four dimensions can descend into either fully composite stealth configurations or partially stealth matter configurations after dimensional reduction.

</details>


### [123] [Landscape of Higgs vacua in 5d $\mathcal{N}=1$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24176)
*Amihay Hanany,Elias Van den Driessche*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the Higgs branch of strongly coupled 5d N=1 gauge theories, analyzing chiral ring structures and symmetry enhancements for SU(N_c)_k and SO(N_c) theories, and identifies the Higgs branch as a classical integrable system via action-variable expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure of the Higgs branch of vacua in strongly coupled 5d N=1 gauge theories, particularly how Chern-Simons levels and gauge group type affect chiral ring relations and symmetry enhancement.

Method: Algebraic analysis of 1/2-BPS operators and their relations; mapping moduli space across phases; construction of chiral ring constraints at infinite coupling; identification of integrable structure via action-variable expansion of the chiral ring.

Result: Explicit deformation of chiral ring relations by Chern-Simons level k in SU(N_c)_k theories; chiral ring constraints for SO(N_c) theories at infinite coupling; realization of the Higgs branch as a classical integrable system.

Conclusion: The Higgs branch of these 5d theories admits a rich algebraic and geometric structure governed by chiral ring deformations and symmetry enhancements, and is intrinsically linked to classical integrability through action variables.

Abstract: Strongly coupled 5d $\mathcal{N}=1$ gauge theories have a Higgs branch of their moduli space of vacua, identified by 1/2-BPS operators and their algebraic relations. For $SU(N_c)_k$ theories, we map the moduli space across different phases and we establish how the Chern-Simons level $k$ deforms the chiral ring relations among mesons, baryons, and instantons, while determining specific patterns of symmetry enhancement. For $SO(N_c)$ theories, we construct the corresponding chiral ring constraints at infinite coupling. Finally, we identify the Higgs branch as a classical integrable system, through the expansion of the chiral ring in terms of action variables.

</details>


### [124] [Covariant One-Loop Corrections from a Non-Covariant Expansion: The Scalar Field Case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24227)
*Lucas Ducastelo de C. C. Lima,Riccardo Fecchio*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop correction to the non-minimal scalar–gravity coupling induced by a quartic self-interaction within linearized gravity, obtains the effective action via covariance restoration, compares with heat kernel results, analyzes RG running and decoupling, and confirms consistency of the linearized approach with fully covariant methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute quantum corrections to the non-minimal coupling ξφ²R in scalar-gravity theory using a tractable, non-covariant linearized framework—and verify its reliability against covariant techniques (e.g., heat kernel) while probing renormalization-group behavior and decoupling.

Method: Explicit Feynman diagram computation in linearized gravity (with metric perturbation h_μν); dimensional regularization; momentum subtraction scheme; restoration of general covariance to reconstruct the effective action; derivation of the effective potential and its covariant completion.

Result: Full one-loop correction (divergent + finite parts) to the non-minimal coupling is obtained; RG flow of ξ is derived; UV/IR limits analyzed; decoupling theorem confirmed in momentum subtraction scheme; effective potential and its covariant form explicitly constructed; agreement with heat kernel and other covariant methods verified.

Conclusion: The linearized gravity approach is both computationally efficient and physically robust for computing quantum gravitational corrections to scalar couplings—it correctly reproduces covariant results, supports standard renormalization properties (e.g., decoupling), and provides a viable alternative to more complex full-covariant methods.

Abstract: We compute the one-loop correction to the non-minimal coupling of a scalar field theory to gravity, induced by a quartic self-interaction. We adopt the non-covariant framework of linearized gravity and explicitly compute the corresponding Feynman diagram, including both the divergent and the finite parts. The corresponding contribution to the one-loop effective action is obtained by the requirement of covariance and compared with the known results obtained by the heat kernel technique. We study the running of the non-minimal coupling, its UV and IR limit and verify the validity of the decoupling theorem in the momentum subtraction scheme of renormalization. The linearized gravity approach is also used for deriving the effective potential for the scalar field and its covariant version is stated. The agreement with results derived using strictly covariant methods is verified and highlights the versatility of the linearized gravity approach.

</details>


### [125] [Bosonization for the Schwinger effect at small fermion mass: analytic formula for the 1+1D current in an arbitrary time-dependent, inhomogeneous electric field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24272)
*Samuel E. Gralla,Morifumi Mizuno*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives an analytic formula for Schwinger pair-production current in 1+1D time-dependent, spatially inhomogeneous electric fields, to order m², using a novel hybrid bosonization method; validates duality via agreement with purely fermionic adiabatic regularization in the homogeneous limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute the expectation value of the current from Schwinger pair production in generic (time-dependent and spatially inhomogeneous) electric fields in 1+1D — a problem where standard regularization and vacuum definition are challenging — and to test fermion-boson duality nontrivially.

Method: Develops a 'hybrid method' combining fermionic and bosonic representations via bosonization: fermions define the in-vacuum (free theory in infinite past), while bosons enable finite, unregularized current computation (since current is linear in boson field); computes perturbatively in fermion mass m up to O(m²), including m⁰, m² log m, and m² terms; cross-checks with pure fermionic adiabatic regularization in the spatially homogeneous case.

Result: Obtains full analytic expression for the current expectation value to O(m²) in arbitrary E(t,x); confirms absence of O(m) term; demonstrates exact agreement between hybrid bosonization and adiabatic-regularized fermionic results in the homogeneous limit — a strong verification of bosonization duality.

Conclusion: The hybrid bosonization method is a robust, regularization-free tool for computing dynamical vacuum currents in 1+1D QED-like settings; the agreement in the homogeneous case provides compelling evidence for the consistency and utility of bosonization beyond static or equilibrium contexts.

Abstract: We derive an analytic formula for the expectation value of the current due to Schwinger pair-production in an arbitrary time-dependent, spatially inhomogeneous external electric field in 1+1 dimensions, as a perturbative expansion in the fermion mass $m$. We work to order $m^2$, finding contributions at order $m^0$, $m^2 \log m$ and $m^2$; there is no contribution at $O(m)$. The calculation is enabled by a new ``hybrid method'' using bosonization, where part of the calculation is performed on the fermion side and part is performed on the boson side. The fermion side allows us to define the vacuum using the free theory in the infinite past, while the boson side allows us to compute the current without regularization, since the current operator is linear in the boson field. In the special case of a spatially homogeneous background field, we also perform the calculation purely on the fermion side using adiabatic regularization. The detailed agreement of the results provides a highly non-trivial check of the duality.

</details>


### [126] [Gravity as Large Scale Structure: Families of CFTs and the Higher-Spin Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24496)
*Eric Perlmutter*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies a universal parametric bound on the single-trace higher-spin gap in large-N CFTs: Δ_gap ≲ (δc)^{1/d}, explains it heuristically via conformal branes in theory space, introduces a rigorous notion of 'families of CFTs' (inspired by L-function families), and derives a family-level version of the bound with holographic implications—including exclusion of Planckian scale separation, constraints on extra dimensions, and explanation of bounded gaps in vector models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain an observed universal scaling of the higher-spin gap in large-N CFTs and unify disparate phenomena in AdS/CFT—such as scale separation, extra-dimensional geometry, and vector model behavior—under a coherent conceptual framework rooted in the structure of theory space.

Method: Heuristic reasoning using generalized conformal branes interpolating between CFTs; formal development of 'families of CFTs' modeled on families of L-functions; derivation of a family-level gap bound; holographic interpretation in terms of AdS_{d+1} vacua and string/M-theory compactifications.

Result: A parametric bound Δ_gap ≲ (δc)^{1/d} for single-trace higher-spin gaps in large-N CFTs; a well-defined notion of CFT families; a family-level generalization of the bound; holographic consequences including no Planckian scale separation, constraints on compactification geometry, and unified explanation of gap finiteness in known vector models.

Conclusion: The higher-spin gap bound reflects deep structural regularities across CFT theory space; framing CFTs as families—not isolated points—resolves long-standing AdS/CFT puzzles and yields sharp, testable constraints on quantum gravity in AdS.

Abstract: We observe an apparently universal behavior in large $N$ conformal field theory (CFT) in $d\geq 2$ dimensions: the single-trace, higher-spin gap obeys the parametric bound $\Delta_{\rm gap} \lesssim (\delta c)^{1/d}$, where $\delta c$ is the central charge increment between successive theories. The origin of this bound is explained heuristically, using a generalized notion of branes that interpolate between conformal points in theory space. Seeking a more precise framework for this picture leads to the central concept of families of CFTs, which tacitly underlies various features and puzzles of AdS/CFT. We develop a working definition of families, with inspiration from families of $L$-functions in number theory, and formulate a family-level version of the gap bound. Interpreted holographically for families of AdS$_{d+1}$ vacua, this bound, when applicable, rules out Planckian scale separation, constrains the size and number of extra dimensions, and explains simple scaling patterns in string/M-theory. It also accounts for why all known large $N$ vector models have a bounded gap.

</details>


### [127] [Strong generation of the chiral algebra of $\mathcal N=4$~$SU(N)$ super Yang--Mills theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24594)
*Yifan He*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves the strong-generation conjecture for the Schur sector of 4D N=4 SYM, showing that a finite set of short multiplets—built from invariant polynomials of an adjoint scalar up to degree N—strongly generate the full chiral algebra via quantum BRST cohomology; it establishes completeness using cyclic trace cohomology, quantum lifting, and a novel quartic–odd transport identity, with explicit normal-ordering and BRST primitives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the apparent tension between the infinite number of protected Schur operators in 4D N=4 SYM and the finite Casimir degrees of invariant polynomials of a single adjoint scalar, and to rigorously verify the strong-generation conjecture of Beem et al. for the SU(N) chiral algebra.

Method: Combines cyclic trace cohomology, quantum lifting, and a newly derived arbitrary-degree quartic–odd transport identity; constructs explicit BRST primitives and normal-ordering corrections; uses exact field relations and a finite-rank Newton obstruction to exclude extraneous lowest-weight states.

Result: For all N ≥ 2, the small N=4 short multiplets corresponding to Casimir degrees 2 through N strongly generate the associated chiral algebra in every conformal weight and BRST cohomological degree; all Schur operators are normal-ordered polynomials in their components and derivatives—even when gauge-theoretic representatives involve fermions or arbitrarily many derivatives.

Conclusion: The Schur sector is algebraically finitely generated: a uniform, finite set of local multiplets fully encodes the infinite protected spectrum, enabling precise control over meromorphic OPEs, protected state multiplicities, and the reorganization of trace operators into finite-rank structures.

Abstract: The Schur sector of four-dimensional $\mathcal N=4$ super Yang--Mills theory contains infinitely many protected operators, while the invariant polynomials of a single adjoint scalar are organized by finitely many Casimir degrees. I prove that the small $\mathcal N=4$ short multiplets built from these invariants strongly generate the associated chiral algebra, thereby establishing the $SU(N)$ case of the strong-generation conjecture of Beem et al. For every $N\ge2$, all field components associated with degrees $2,\ldots,N$ suffice, in every conformal weight and every degree of the standard relative quantum BRST cohomology. The proof brings together cyclic trace cohomology, quantum lifting, and an arbitrary-degree quartic--odd transport identity. Exact field relations and a finite-rank Newton obstruction exclude every additional lowest space, including non-scalar flavor representations; the required normal-ordering corrections and BRST primitives are derived explicitly. The resulting completeness theorem explains how a finite family of supersymmetric multiplets organizes an unbounded quantum spectrum. Every Schur class is a normal polynomial in their components and derivatives, even when its gauge-theory representative involves fermions and arbitrarily many derivatives. This supplies a uniform set of local inputs for meromorphic operator products, bounds protected state multiplicities before cancellations in an index, and gives an algebraic account of the finite-rank reorganization of protected trace operators.

</details>


### [128] [Holomorphic extremal surfaces for pseudoentropy in dS/CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24698)
*Giorgos Anastasiou,Ignacio J. Araya,Avijit Das,Javier Moreno*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies extremization of codimension-two surfaces in de Sitter space using holomorphic coordinates and real anchoring at future infinity, deriving the condition K^I = 0; it computes holographic pseudoentropy for harmonic deformations of spherical regions, confirms universality up to second order in deformation, and shows contour independence within homology classes—while inequivalent classes differ by sign (odd d) or finite shifts (even d); conical defects in dS₃ validate the first law and contour degeneracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Hartle–Hawking construction to complex bulk geometries in de Sitter space and systematically characterize holographic pseudoentropy—including its universality, contour dependence, and response to geometric deformations and defects.

Method: Formulates extremization in holomorphic coordinates with real boundary conditions at future infinity; solves K^I = 0 for complex extremal surfaces; computes holographic pseudoentropy for harmonic deformations of spherical regions across dimensions; classifies integration contours by global-coordinate isometry-induced homology classes; analyzes conical defects in dS₃ via Schwarzschild–de Sitter mass parametrization.

Result: Pseudoentropy expansion is universal through second order in deformation parameter and independent of integration contour within each homology class; inequivalent homology classes yield sign-flipped universal terms in odd boundary dimensions and real finite shifts in even dimensions; conical defects satisfy the first law of pseudoentropy and retain contour degeneracy per class.

Conclusion: The holomorphic extremization framework generalizes Hartle–Hawking geometry in dS space and reveals robust, topologically classified features of holographic pseudoentropy—establishing its universality, geometric sensitivity, and thermodynamic consistency even for non-trivial defects.

Abstract: We formulate the extremization problem for codimension-two hypersurfaces in de Sitter space in holomorphic coordinates, with real anchoring conditions at future infinity. The resulting equation ($K^I=0$) describes complex bulk realizations extending the usual piecewise Hartle--Hawking construction. We compute holographic pseudoentropy for harmonic deformations of spherical regions of arbitrary size and dimension, confirming its universal expansion through second order in the deformation parameter. The result is independent of the integration contour within each homology class on the complexified extremal surface. Inequivalent classes, labeled by an integer associated with a discrete global-coordinate isometry, differ by a sign of the universal term in odd boundary dimensions and by real finite shifts in even dimensions. For conical defects in dS$_3$ parametrized by a Schwarzschild-dS mass, we also verify the first law of pseudoentropy and the persistence of contour degeneracy within each homology class.

</details>


### [129] [Bootstrapping Fermionic Veneziano Amplitudes from Regge Zeros](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24754)
*Pongwit Srisangyingcharoen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the Regge-zero bootstrap to fermionic tree-level scattering, deriving linear Regge trajectories for fermionic and bosonic resonances; duality and degeneracy constraints fix the amplitude to the open superstring amplitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Regge-zero bootstrap—previously applied to purely bosonic amplitudes like the Veneziano model—to amplitudes involving external fermions, thereby probing consistency conditions of dual resonance models with spacetime supersymmetry.

Method: Bootstraps the colour-ordered four-point amplitude of a massless scalar and a massless spin-1/2 fermion by factoring out a little-group-covariant spinor structure; imposes Regge zeros in residues controlled by the opposite-channel trajectory, assumes ultrasoft Regge behaviour and absence of extraneous zeros, enforces duality between fermionic-s- and bosonic-t-channel pole expansions, and imposes spectral degeneracy.

Result: Both Regge trajectories are proven linear; fermionic spins scale as J_F = n + 1/2, bosonic as J_B = m + 1; duality fixes the reduced amplitude to a Beta function; spectral degeneracy forces equal slopes, yielding the exact open superstring amplitude.

Conclusion: The Regge-zero bootstrap uniquely selects the open superstring amplitude when extended to mixed scalar–fermion scattering, providing a first-principles derivation of spacetime supersymmetry from analyticity, duality, and Regge behaviour.

Abstract: We extend the Regge-zero bootstrap of the Veneziano amplitude to tree-level scattering with external fermions. We consider the colour-ordered four-point amplitude of a massless scalar and a massless spin-$\frac12$ fermion, in which fermionic resonances appear in the $s$-channel and bosonic resonances in the $t$-channel. Factoring out a spinor structure that carries the little-group weight of the amplitude, we bootstrap the resulting reduced amplitude, whose residues in each channel vanish at Regge zeros controlled by the trajectory of the opposite channel. Assuming ultrasoft Regge behaviour and that the residues have no zeros other than Regge zeros, we show that both Regge trajectories are linear and that the maximal spins grow with the level as $J_F=n+\frac12$ and $J_B=m+1$. Duality between the fermionic- and bosonic-pole expansions then fixes the reduced amplitude to a Beta function, up to its normalisation and the ratio of the two Regge slopes. Requiring the fermionic and bosonic spectra to be degenerate equates the slopes, and the resulting amplitude coincides with the open superstring amplitude.

</details>


### [130] [Complex spinodals and supercritical crossovers in Kerr-Newman-AdS$_4$ black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24978)
*Bum-Hoon Lee,Hocheol Lee,Somyadip Thakur*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes supercritical crossovers in 4D Kerr-Newman-AdS black holes at fixed charge and angular momentum, establishing uniqueness of the critical point, deriving exact quartic Landau behavior, identifying a Widom-type crossover surface from spinodal projections, and proving universal cusp exponents and quadratic separation between thermodynamic ridges near criticality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize thermodynamic crossovers—particularly supercritical behavior—in rotating, charged AdS black holes, bridging black hole thermodynamics with classical critical phenomena and resolving ambiguities in defining crossover lines beyond mean-field Landau theory.

Method: Exact analytical derivation of enthalpy; quartic Landau expansion near criticality; scaling analysis with pressure displacement; projection of complex spinodals onto real temperature axis; construction of Widom line and crossover surface via charge-rotation ratio variation; comparison with endpoint (Kerr-AdS) scaling; numerical continuation and exact endpoint solution verification.

Result: Uniqueness of physical critical point and positivity of quartic Landau coefficient across charge-rotation family; exact coexistence entropies and latent heat in subcritical regime; emergence of Widom-type line from real projection of complex spinodals; crossover surface formed by varying charge-rotation ratio; universal cusp exponents (1/2 for imaginary entropy, 3/2 for imaginary temperature); quadratic temperature separation between projected fold and heat capacity ridge, with ridge at higher temperature near criticality.

Conclusion: Supercritical crossovers in 4D Kerr-Newman-AdS black holes exhibit universal Landau-critical structure: the Widom line and crossover surface are geometrically and analytically well-defined, cusp exponents are family-invariant, and thermodynamic ridges obey precise leading-order scaling—confirming deep analogies with condensed-matter critical phenomena while highlighting black-hole-specific thermodynamic criteria for crossover definition.

Abstract: We study supercritical crossovers in four-dimensional Kerr-Newman-AdS black holes at fixed angular momentum and charge. From the exact enthalpy, we prove uniqueness of the physical critical point and positivity of the quartic Landau coefficient throughout the charge-rotation family, treating the uncharged Kerr-AdS endpoint in separate scaling variables. An exact quartic reduction determines the coexistence entropies and latent heat throughout the subcritical regime. Above criticality, the two physical spinodals continue as a complex conjugate pair. Their real temperature projection defines a Widom-type line, and varying the charge-rotation ratio assembles these lines into a crossover surface. The Landau cusp fixes the imaginary entropy and temperature to scale with the square root and three-halves powers of the pressure displacement from criticality. The projected fold and the local heat capacity ridge share a critical tangent. We derive their leading quadratic temperature separation and prove that the ridge lies at a higher temperature near criticality. Exact endpoint solutions and numerical continuation check these predictions. The cusp exponents are common to the family, while the crossover temperature depends on the thermodynamic criterion used to define it.

</details>


### [131] [The Anyon Atlas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25439)
*Jonah Berean-Dutcher,Clay Cordova,Julio Parra-Martinez,Richard Tuddenham*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies low-rank modular and supermodular tensor categories—modeling 2+1D topological quantum field theories (TQFTs) with up to 22 (bosonic) or 26 (fermionic) anyons—using congruence representations of SL(2,ℤ) and numerical optimization; it discovers new modular data not realizable via Chern–Simons theory or anyon condensation/gauging, confirms unitary TQFT realizations for 11- and 12-anyon cases by solving pentagon/hexagon equations, and provides an online database.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify physically realizable 2+1D topological quantum field theories (TQFTs) with small numbers of anyon types, and to identify whether all such theories admit a Chern–Simons or gauge-theoretic origin—or if genuinely new, non-gauge TQFTs exist.

Method: Constructs modular and supermodular data via congruence representations of SL(2,ℤ); employs a scalable numerical optimization algorithm to generate consistent S- and T-matrices; verifies physical consistency (e.g., fusion rules, positivity, integrality); analyzes relations (anyon condensation, Chern–Simons realizations); solves pentagon and hexagon equations explicitly for selected low-rank cases to confirm unitary TQFT existence.

Result: Complete classification of modular/supermodular data up to rank 22 (bosonic) and 26 (fermionic); discovery of novel modular data not obtainable from Chern–Simons theories via condensation or symmetry gauging; explicit construction of unitary TQFTs for 11- and 12-anyon examples; public release of an online database for exploration.

Conclusion: There exist unitary, fully consistent 2+1D TQFTs—realized by modular tensor categories—that lie outside the Chern–Simons/gauge-theory paradigm, establishing a qualitatively new class of topological phases of matter.

Abstract: We classify possible topological quantum field theories in 2+1 dimensions with up to 22 anyons in the bosonic case and up to 26 in the fermionic case, through the modular data of modular and supermodular tensor categories. We construct such data via congruence representations of $\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{Z})$ using a numerical optimization algorithm that scales to high rank. We chart the relations between the resulting theories, including anyon condensations and Chern-Simons realizations. We find examples of modular data that do not appear to arise from any Chern-Simons theory by any sequence of condensations of anyons or gaugings of ordinary global symmetries. These examples give qualitatively new topological quantum field theories, which go beyond any notion of gauge theory. For examples in this class with 11 and 12 anyon types, we solve the pentagon and hexagon equations explicitly, showing that these modular data are realized by full unitary topological quantum field theories. We also introduce this https URL, an online resource for exploring the data.

</details>


### [132] [Remarks on the Parameter Dependence of QCD$_{2A}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25567)
*Uwe Trittmann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the $N_c$ dependence of the spectrum in two-dimensional adjoint QCD, showing that no single operator dominates the weak $N_c$ scaling; instead, all identified operators are essential for preserving exact degeneracies and faithful Hamiltonian representation. It completes the list of single- and multi-trace interactions per trace-number block (consistent with Kutasov-Schwimmer universality), enables targeted deformations to achieve $N_c$-invariant masses, but argues residual $N_c$ dependence is unavoidable. In the large constituent mass regime ($m^2/g_{\rm YM}^2 N_c \gg 1$), eLCQ analysis confirms eigenstate purification in parton number at large $N_c$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin and structure of the weak $N_c$ dependence in the spectrum of 2D adjoint QCD, test the dominance hypothesis of specific operators, verify Kutasov-Schwimmer universality in trace-sector couplings, and explore emergent simplifications (e.g., parton-number purity) in the heavy-mass large-$N_c$ limit.

Method: Numerical Hamiltonian diagonalization in the light-cone quantization framework (eLCQ); systematic classification and inclusion of all relevant local and non-local operators contributing to $N_c$-scaling; decomposition of the Hilbert space into trace-number blocks; analysis of multi-particle state degeneracies and mass splittings as functions of $N_c$; controlled deformation of interaction terms to isolate $N_c$-invariant sectors.
