Result: No single operator dominates the $N_c$ dependence — all are required to maintain exact multi-particle degeneracies; full list of inter-trace and intra-trace interactions is constructed, confirming Kutasov-Schwimmer block structure; targeted deformations can eliminate $N_c$ dependence for specific states but not globally; residual $N_c$ dependence is argued to be intrinsic; in the $m^2/(g_{\rm YM}^2 N_c) \gg 1$ regime, eigenstates become increasingly pure in parton number.

Conclusion: The $N_c$ dependence in 2D adjoint QCD is collective and irreducible — it arises from the interplay of multiple operators across trace-number sectors, reflecting deep structural constraints rather than perturbative or accidental effects; while certain limits (e.g., large constituent mass) enhance simplifications like parton-number purity, full $N_c$-independence is unattainable, underscoring the theory's nontrivial large-$N_c$ dynamics.

Abstract: Recently it was shown that there are only a few operators that contribute to the (very weak) $N_c$ dependence of the spectrum of two-dimensional adjoint QCD. We provide numerical evidence that none of these operators is dominant. In other words, all operators are necessary to guarantee the faithful representation of the Hamiltonian, resulting in, for instance, exact degeneracies of certain multi-particle state masses. We provide a complete list of interactions between single- and multi-trace states, which differ by trace-number block, as expected from the Kutasov-Schwimmer universality. The completed list allows for a targeted deformation of the theory to render some masses $N_c$-invariant. Nonetheless, we argue that a small amount of $N_c$ dependence of the theory is unavoidable. We also investigate the large constituent mass regime $m^2/g^2_{\rm YM}N_c\gg 1$ of the theory at large $N_c$ in the eLCQ approach. We find, among other things, that as the constituent mass grows, eigenstates get purer in parton number, in line with expectations.

</details>


### [133] [Non-Riemannian Holography: Long Strings and Soft Hair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25669)
*Shaun D. Hampton,Hyun-Cheol Kim,Jae-Hyuk Oh,Jeong-Hyuck Park*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces an O(D,D)-covariant holographic dictionary for two bulk geometries dual to Gomis–Ooguri boundary data: Riemannian AdS₃ with NS–NS flux and a non-Riemannian string Newton–Cartan geometry; it identifies a covariant Codazzi identity as the origin of boundary conservation, distinguishes soft hair in the non-Riemannian branch, and reveals novel supersymmetric structures ('supersupersymmetry').


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a manifestly O(D,D)-covariant holographic dictionary accommodating both Riemannian and non-Riemannian bulk completions of the Gomis–Ooguri boundary theory, resolving how boundary conservation and soft degrees of freedom emerge covariantly in double field theory context.

Method: Uses O(D,D)-covariant double field theory framework; derives boundary conservation from an O(D,D)-covariant Codazzi identity; computes generalized-metric one-point functions and analyzes normalizable vs. non-normalizable modes; examines supersymmetry variations and Killing-spinor conditions on non-Riemannian saddles.

Result: Both bulk branches yield identical diagonal generalized-metric one-point functions; only the non-Riemannian branch hosts independent, normalizable soft hair decoupled from surface charges and renormalized on-shell action; supersymmetric non-Riemannian solutions support arbitrary chiral Killing-spinor profiles — dubbed 'supersupersymmetry'.

Conclusion: The O(D,D)-covariant holographic dictionary unifies Riemannian and non-Riemannian realizations of the same boundary data, revealing that non-Riemannian geometry hosts physically distinct soft sectors and enables enhanced supersymmetry not possible in standard Riemannian AdS₃/CFT₂.

Abstract: We propose an $\mathbf{O}(D,D)$-covariant holographic dictionary for two bulk completions of the Gomis--Ooguri boundary data: Riemannian $AdS_3$ supported by pure NS--NS flux and an everywhere non-Riemannian string Newton--Cartan geometry. An $\mathbf{O}(D,D)$-covariant Codazzi identity yields the boundary conservation law. Both branches share diagonal generalized-metric one-point functions, but only the non-Riemannian branch carries independent normalizable soft hair that couples to long strings without contributing to surface charges or the renormalized on-shell action. Supersymmetric non-Riemannian saddles admit arbitrary chiral Killing-spinor profiles, realizing "supersupersymmetry."

</details>


### [134] [Subsystem quantum Rényi-Bhattacharyya distance in 2D CFTs and its holographic applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25685)
*Yihao Wang,Jiaju Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops an OPE-based method to compute subsystem fidelity and Rényi fidelity in 2D CFTs, applying it to quantum Bhattacharyya and Rényi-Bhattacharyya distances; it distinguishes regimes where distinguishability is classical gravity–dominated (𝒪(c)) vs. quantum gravity–corrected (𝒪(1/c)), and reveals a striking ℓ⁸, 𝒪(1) leading BD contribution for heavy primary vs. thermal states—contrasting with 𝒪(c) RBD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically quantify state distinguishability in 2D CFTs via fidelity-based distances, bridging CFT techniques with holographic expectations—particularly clarifying when subsystem observables probe classical vs. quantum aspects of dual gravity.

Method: Uses twist operator OPE to compute subsystem fidelity and Rényi fidelity in short-interval expansion; applies to Bhattacharyya distance (BD) and Rényi-Bhattacharyya distance (RBD); benchmarks on free fermion/boson CFTs; extends to holographic CFTs with heavy primaries and thermal states.

Result: BD and RBD scale as 𝒪(c) when states differ in classical (order-c) data (e.g., different heavy primaries or temperatures); scale as 𝒪(1/c) when classical data match; for heavy primary vs. thermal state, BD starts at ℓ⁸ and is 𝒪(1), while RBD remains 𝒪(c), consistent with known Rényi entropy discrepancies.

Conclusion: Subsystem fidelity-based distances diagnose the gravitational sector probed: 𝒪(c) signals classical geometry differences, 𝒪(1/c) signals quantum fluctuations; the ℓ⁸ BD term reveals a universal, non-entanglement-entropy-based signature distinguishing heavy primaries from thermal states in holography.

Abstract: We develop a systematic method to compute the subsystem fidelity and Rényi fidelity in two-dimensional conformal field theories (CFTs) from the operator product expansion of twist operators, and apply it to the subsystem quantum Bhattacharyya distance (BD) and Rényi-Bhattacharyya distance (RBD). We validate the method against exact results in the free massless fermion and boson theories, and then use it to compute the BD and RBD in holographic CFTs between two heavy primary states, between two thermal states, and between a heavy primary state and a thermal state. When the two states differ in their classical, order-$c$ data, the distances are of order $c$, and the states are distinguishable already at the level of classical gravity; when the leading data coincide, the distances are of order $1/c$ at every order of the short interval expansion, so that the distinguishability becomes a perturbative quantum gravity effect. For a heavy primary state and its thermal counterpart, the leading contribution to the BD appears at order $\ell^8$ and is of order $c^0$, whereas the RBD is generically of order $c$, in line with the earlier finding that a heavy primary state and a thermal state share the same order-$c$ entanglement entropy but have different order-$c$ Rényi entropies in the short interval expansion.

</details>


### [135] [The long-range Ising CFT: perturbative and nonperturbative approaches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25940)
*Zechuan Zheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the long-range Ising model across 1D–3D using multiple complementary methods—perturbative expansion (up to eight loops), Hamiltonian truncation (with artifact correction), numerical renormalization group, and Monte Carlo—finding sub-percent agreement on the scaling dimension of the leading Z₂-even scalar, confirming universality in nonlocal conformal field theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the continuous family of interacting critical points in the long-range Ising model—a paradigmatic nonlocal CFT—and test universality across dimensions and methods.

Method: Combined high-order perturbative expansion (generalized free field and short-range limits), corrected Hamiltonian truncation for nonlocal Hamiltonians, numerical renormalization group, and Monte Carlo simulations.

Result: All methods agree within sub-percent on the scaling dimension of the leading Z₂-even scalar operator across 1D–3D; eight-loop expansion extends prior work; Hamiltonian truncation is validated for nonlocal theories after artifact correction.

Conclusion: The long-range Ising model’s critical family exhibits robust universality, and disparate theoretical and numerical techniques converge quantitatively, establishing reliability of nonlocal CFT descriptions beyond perturbation theory.

Abstract: The long-range Ising model is the prototype of criticality with power-law interactions. In each dimension its interacting critical points form a one-parameter family of nonlocal conformal field theories, weakly coupled at both ends but not in between. We study this family in one, two and three dimensions from several independent directions. We extend the expansion about the generalized free field from three to eight loops and expand about the short-range end. We show that Hamiltonian truncation applies to this nonlocal theory once a truncation artifact is corrected. Where they can be compared, the resummed expansions, Hamiltonian truncation, the numerical renormalization group and Monte Carlo simulations agree on the dimension of the leading $\mathbb{Z}_2$-even scalar at the sub-percent level, as universality requires.

</details>


### [136] [Higher-Dimensional Test of the Holographic Dual of Entanglement Cohomology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25969)
*R.C.Rashkov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the holographic test of entanglement cohomology from 2D to higher-dimensional CFTs (d=3,4), computing boundary relative entropy and bulk canonical energy for primary-operator perturbations of spherical entangling regions; results agree at first order with universal coefficients κ_d Δ²/C_T, confirming the modular Hamiltonian perturbation as the holographic avatar of the first entanglement cohomology class.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively verify the holographic dual of entanglement cohomology beyond d=2, testing whether modular Hamiltonian perturbations correctly represent the first entanglement cohomology class in AdS/CFT.

Method: Computes boundary relative entropy via conformal Ward identities for primary-operator perturbations of the vacuum modular Hamiltonian in d-dimensional CFTs with spherical entangling surfaces; matches to bulk canonical energy of a dual AdS_{d+1} scalar field using the JLMS relation.

Result: Exact agreement at first order in perturbation between boundary relative entropy and bulk canonical energy for d=3 and d=4, yielding coefficients κ_3 = π²/12 and κ_4 = 3π³/16, both scaling as Δ²/C_T.

Conclusion: The modular Hamiltonian perturbation is confirmed as the correct holographic representative of the first entanglement cohomology class in d ≥ 3, extending prior d=2 results and reinforcing the structural role of entanglement cohomology in holography.

Abstract: We extend the holographic test of entanglement cohomology from $d=2$ to higher-dimensional conformal field theories. For a $d$-dimensional CFT with a spherical entangling region $A$, the vacuum modular Hamiltonian is known explicitly, and the boundary relative entropy of a primary-operator perturbation can be computed via conformal Ward identities. On the bulk side, the perturbation is dual to a scalar field in AdS$_{d+1}$, and the bulk canonical energy is computed using the JLMS relation. We carry out the computation explicitly for $d=3$ and $d=4$ and find that the boundary and bulk results agree at first order in the perturbation, with identical coefficients $\kappa_d \Delta^2 / C_T$, where $\kappa_3 = \pi^2/12$ and $\kappa_4 = 3\pi^3/16$. The result extends the quantitative verification of the holographic dual of entanglement cohomology beyond the $d=2$ case treated in the companion paper and confirms that the modular Hamiltonian perturbation is the correct holographic image of the first entanglement cohomology class. We discuss the role of the conformal anomaly, the shape of the entanglement wedge, and an outlook on TsT-deformed backgrounds. \keywords{Entanglement entropy \and Cyclic cohomology \and von Neumann algebras \and Holography \and Modular Hamiltonian}

</details>


### [137] [One-loop unifying relations between Yang-Mills theory and the nonlinear sigma model from Berends-Giele currents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25972)
*Zhenhua Guo,Xin Yan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper unifies Yang-Mills theory and the nonlinear sigma model by extending tree-level amplitude relations—via differential operators—to off-shell multiparticle currents, and derives one-loop integrand relations by sewing two off-shell legs; the method potentially generalizes to higher loops.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover and formalize unifying relations between Yang-Mills theory and the nonlinear sigma model, particularly beyond on-shell amplitudes.

Method: Extends tree-level amplitude relations expressed via differential operators to off-shell multiparticle currents; constructs one-loop integrands by sewing two off-shell legs; proposes applicability to higher-loop integrands.

Result: Derives new off-shell current relations and explicit one-loop integrand relations bridging Yang-Mills and nonlinear sigma model; establishes a framework for potential higher-loop generalization.

Conclusion: A systematic off-shell approach successfully unifies aspects of Yang-Mills theory and the nonlinear sigma model at tree and one-loop levels, suggesting broader utility in quantum field theory amplitude computations.

Abstract: We study unifying relations between Yang-Mills theory and the nonlinear sigma model. We extend tree-level amplitude relations expressed through differential operators to off-shell multiparticle currents and obtain one-loop integrand relations by sewing two off-shell legs. This off-shell approach may also apply to higher-loop integrands.

</details>


### [138] [Hubble Susceptibility: Beyond Effective-Mass Spectroscopy in the Cosmological Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26014)
*Shuntaro Aoki,Masahide Yamaguchi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces Hubble susceptibility as a probe of Hubble-induced mass corrections to heavy fields during inflation, using squeezed-limit oscillations' scale dependence to distinguish them from intrinsic masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Heavy fields during inflation acquire Hubble-scale mass corrections; disentangling these from intrinsic masses is essential for accurate cosmological collider physics interpretation.

Method: Analyzes the scale dependence and drift of oscillation frequency in the squeezed limit of correlation functions at leading slow-roll and adiabatic order.

Result: The local oscillation frequency and its scale-dependent drift (Hubble susceptibility) cleanly separate Hubble-induced mass corrections from intrinsic field masses.

Conclusion: Hubble susceptibility provides a robust, observable signature to identify and quantify Hubble-scale mass effects in primordial non-Gaussianity.

Abstract: In this note, we introduce the Hubble susceptibility of cosmological-collider masses, encoded in the scale dependence of the squeezed-limit oscillation. Heavy fields generically receive Hubble-induced mass corrections during inflation, and we show that the local oscillation frequency and its drift can disentangle such corrections from an intrinsic mass contribution at leading slow-roll and adiabatic order.

</details>


### [139] [Millicharged particles and the degree-3 Prouhet-Tarry-Escott problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26092)
*Ben Gripaios*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses geometric methods to find all integer solutions to a system of three power-sum equations (for k=1,2,3) with m variables, a problem new for m ≥ 5; it applies these solutions to anomaly cancellation in particle physics models featuring millicharged fermions arising from kinetic mixing and chiral symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve a previously unsolved arithmetic problem—finding all integer solutions to simultaneous power-sum equalities for k = 1,2,3 and arbitrary m—with direct relevance to anomaly cancellation conditions in beyond-Standard-Model physics involving millicharged particles.

Method: Geometric approach to Diophantine analysis: reformulating the system of equations ∑x_i^k = ∑y_i^k (k=1,2,3) as constraints on symmetric varieties or moment maps, enabling classification of integer solution sets via algebraic geometry and lattice techniques.

Result: Complete classification of all integer solutions for any m; first explicit solution set for m ≥ 5; physical interpretation linking solutions to consistent, anomaly-free U(1) extensions with vector-like and chiral symmetries yielding light, millicharged fermions.

Conclusion: Geometry provides a powerful and novel tool for solving high-degree symmetric Diophantine systems; the resulting solutions fully characterize viable charge assignments for millicharged particle models satisfying quantum consistency (anomaly cancellation) in effective field theory.

Abstract: We use geometry to solve a problem in arithmetic relevant for physics. Namely, for any $m$ we find all integer solutions to the equations $\sum_{i=1}^m x_i^k = \sum_{i=1}^m y_i^k$, where $k \in \{1,2,3\}$; this seems to be new for $m \geq 5$. In physics, these are the anomaly cancellation conditions for models with $m$ fermions which can be naturally light and carry small electric charges (a.k.a. millicharged or minicharged particles). The small charges arise by kinetic mixing of the Standard Model $U(1)$ gauge symmetry with an unbroken non-chiral (a.k.a. vector-like) $U(1)$ and the masses are suppressed by a spontaneously-broken chiral $U(1)$.

</details>


### [140] [Quantum Noise from Massive Lorentz--Violating Gravitons: Interferometer Signatures in Very Special Linear Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26177)
*Jorge Alfaro,Alex Soto,Alessandro Santoni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates graviton signatures in classical gravitational detectors within the Very Special Linear Gravity framework, identifying dissipative terms and novel mass-squared corrections; it further reveals a phase-sign-dependent contribution in squeezed-state configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observable signatures of a gauge-invariant massive graviton with two polarizations in classical gravitational wave detectors, leveraging the Very Special Linear Gravity framework.

Method: Analysis within the Very Special Linear Gravity framework using the Vernon approach to derive detector response, including treatment of dissipative effects and squeezed quantum states.

Result: Derivation of dissipative terms consistent with prior literature plus new O(m_g^2) corrections; identification of a squeezing-phase-sign-dependent contribution unique to the model.

Conclusion: The Very Special Linear Gravity framework yields distinctive, testable features in classical detector responses—particularly mass-dependent corrections and phase-sensitive squeezed-state effects—that could distinguish it from standard massive gravity models.

Abstract: In this work we analyse under the Very Special Linear Gravity framework, which introduces a gauge invariant massive graviton with two polarizations, possible signatures of gravitons in a classical gravitational detector. Following the Vernon approach we found dissipative terms consistent with other works and extra terms of order of the graviton mass squared. The model also presents a characteristic feature when a squeezed state is considered. In such case, a contribution that distinguishes the sign of the squeezing phase is present.

</details>


### [141] [Quantum Relaxation Dynamics of Vacuum Energy from Pre-Geometric Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26197)
*Andrea Addazi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a quantum relaxation mechanism for vacuum energy in a MacDowell–Mansouri gravity framework with SO(1,4)→SO(1,3) spontaneous symmetry breaking, predicting discrete, quantized vacuum energy levels and monotonic cosmological constant decay to zero via tunneling across topologically distinct vacua.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the cosmological constant problem by providing a dynamical, quantum-mechanical mechanism for vacuum energy relaxation—rather than fine-tuning—via topological structure and symmetry breaking in pre-geometric gravity.

Method: Analyzes spontaneous symmetry breaking SO(1,4) → SO(1,3) induced by a Higgs field Φ in MacDowell–Mansouri gravity; constructs a periodic Higgs potential yielding infinitely many topologically distinct vacua; computes quantum tunneling probabilities between vacua; derives dualities and see-saw relations linking Planck mass, cosmological constant, Gauss–Bonnet coupling, and θ-term—all emergent post-SSB.

Result: Vacuum energy is quantized into discrete levels Λ_k; quantum tunneling probability is maximized along a strictly decreasing sequence Λ₁ > Λ₂ > … → Λ_∞ = 0; topological parameters (e.g., Gauss–Bonnet coupling, θ-term) emerge only after SSB and govern scale relations.

Conclusion: The model realizes a natural, geometrically grounded relaxation of the cosmological constant to zero through quantum dynamics on a topological vacuum landscape—offering a testable alternative to anthropic or fine-tuning explanations.

Abstract: We investigate a quantum relaxation mechanism for the vacuum energy density arising from the spontaneous symmetry breaking (SSB) $SO(1,4)\to SO(1,3)$, driven by a Higgs field $\Phi$ in a MacDowell--Mansouri pre-geometric gravity framework. The theory predicts a countably infinite set of vacua, corresponding to distinct topological sectors, for a periodic Higgs potential with intervening barriers. This structure gives rise to a series of dualities and see-saw relations connecting fundamental scales---the Planck mass and the Cosmological Constant---to topological parameters such as the Gauss-Bonnet coupling and the $\theta$-term, all of which emerge only after SSB. A striking consequence is the quantization of the vacuum energy into discrete levels. Starting from a vacuum expectation value $\langle \Phi\rangle = v_1$ associated with a large vacuum energy, the system evolves via quantum tunneling through the infinite landscape of minima. Crucially, we demonstrate that the transition probability is maximized for the ordered sequence $ \Lambda_1 > \cdots > \Lambda_k > \cdots$, where the Cosmological Constant decreases monotonically toward the asymptotic attractor $\Lambda_\infty = 0$.

</details>


### [142] [Boundary preparation of strong Mpemba states in holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26218)
*Yu Shi,Xinwu Liu,Liping Li,Chikun Ding,Yuebing Zhou,Changqing Liu,Sheng Long*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that finite boundary drives ending at the same final charge density can select distinct relaxation channels in an Abelian–Higgs model on a fixed AdS black brane; it identifies cubic slow-mode amplitudes arising from charge-density history and radial Maxwell feedback, achieves suppression of slow-mode contributions from O(ε³) to O(ε⁵) via compensating pulses, and shows tunable elimination of one or two slow modes—enabling strong Mpemba-like states and preserving time advantages even in charge-neutral controls.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how different preparation protocols (finite boundary drives) ending at identical final charge densities can lead to qualitatively distinct relaxation dynamics—particularly whether and how slow relaxation channels can be selectively suppressed or eliminated without altering the final equilibrium state or quasinormal-mode spectrum.

Method: Uses a probe Abelian–Higgs model on a fixed AdS black brane background with stable normal phase endpoint; analyzes linear and nonlinear evolution under time-dependent boundary drives; isolates contributions from density history and radial Maxwell feedback; constructs compensating pulses from low-order (cubic) amplitude calculations; validates via full nonlinear numerical evolution; performs local analysis for reachability of Mpemba states; compares charge-neutral control protocols including total control duration.

Result: Density-history term contributes 85–89% of total cubic slow-mode coefficient at ρ_f = 3; compensating pulse reduces source-amplitude scaling of slow dynamics from O(ε³) to O(ε⁵), achieving ~600× suppression at ε = 0.05; two- and three-pulse preparations fully suppress one and two slow modes respectively within numerical resolution; strong Mpemba states (vanishing selected asymptotic slow-mode coefficients) are finitely reachable; charge-neutral controls retain total-time advantage from shared input.

Conclusion: Relaxation channel selection is protocol-dependent and controllable via boundary drive design; slow-mode amplitudes are not solely determined by final state but encode memory of preparation history and dynamical feedback; cubic interference enables high-order cancellation; this provides a robust framework for engineering fast relaxation and realizing Mpemba-like phenomena in holographic quantum many-body systems.

Abstract: Finite boundary drives ending at the same charge density can select different relaxation channels. We demonstrate this in a probe Abelian--Higgs model on a fixed AdS black brane with a stable normal endpoint. Charge exchange during preparation modifies scalar propagation and source coupling. Density history and radial Maxwell feedback during and after the drive leave a cubic slow amplitude even after linear slow-mode cancellation. For the tested protocols at $\rho_f=3$, the density-history term interferes constructively with the net radial feedback; its modulus is 85--89\% of that of the total cubic coefficient. Calculated from the low-order equations alone, this total coefficient predicts the compensating pulse. Full nonlinear evolution confirms the reduction from $O(\epsilon^3)$ to $O(\epsilon^5)$ in the source amplitude $\epsilon$, with approximately 600-fold suppression for the representative protocol at $\epsilon=0.05$. Compensation extends the window of fast-channel relaxation without changing the final quasinormal-mode spectrum; early distance crossing already arises from linear interference. Fully tuned two- and three-pulse preparations suppress one and two slow modes, respectively, within numerical resolution. Local analysis establishes finite-pulse reachability of strong Mpemba states with vanishing selected asymptotic slow-mode coefficients. Charge-neutral controls retain a total-time advantage from a common input when the full subsequent control duration is included.

</details>


### [143] [Dispersion of three-dimensional oscillons: a standing-wave perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26281)
*I. V. Barashenkov,R. De Sousa,N. V. Alexeeva,A. Dika,E. V. Zemlyanaya*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper models an intermediate stage of the φ⁴ oscillon using a spherically symmetric standing wave in a finite ball with weak radiation exchange; it derives an energy-frequency relation via multiscale variational analysis and validates it numerically, finding excellent agreement near the continuous spectrum endpoint but degradation below ω ≈ 0.85ω₀ due to neglected radiation wings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model the intermediate adiabatic evolution stage of φ⁴ oscillons using a tractable yet physically informed standing-wave configuration that captures core-boundary radiative coupling.

Method: Multiscale variational framework applied to a spherically symmetric standing wave confined in a finite-radius ball, with weak radiative exchange between core and boundary; comparison against numerical solutions of the φ⁴ boundary value problem on a (time, radius) domain.

Result: An analytic dispersion curve for energy vs. frequency is obtained; numerical validation shows excellent agreement near the continuous spectrum endpoint (ω → ω₀), but error grows as ω decreases below 0.85ω₀.

Conclusion: The variational ansatz accurately captures the oscillon’s near-threshold behavior but fails at lower frequencies due to increasing significance of the radiation tail—highlighting the limitation of neglecting extended radiation wings in the trial function.

Abstract: A spherically symmetric standing wave confined within a finite-radius ball, with weak radiative exchange between its core and boundary, provides a model for an intermediate stage in the adiabatic evolution of the $\phi^4$ oscillon. We employ a multiscale variational framework to derive an approximate description of these standing waves and their energy-frequency relation. The resulting dispersion curve is compared with that obtained from numerical solutions of the boundary value problem for the $\phi^4$ equation on a two dimensional (time and radius) domain. The agreement between the numerical and variational results is excellent near the endpoint of the continuous spectrum but deteriorates as $\omega$ is reduced below $0.85 \omega_0$. We attribute this discrepancy to the growth of the radiation wing, which is neglected in the variational ansatz.

</details>


### [144] [U(1) Current Diffusion in Gauss-Bonnet Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26515)
*A. Naveena Kumara,Shreyas Punacha,Kartheek Hegde*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates U(1) charge transport holographically using a probe Maxwell field in a 5D Gauss-Bonnet-AdS black-brane background; it derives off-shell constitutive relations, computes diffusion and conductivities analytically (hydrodynamic regime) and numerically (generic frequency/momentum), and shows Gauss-Bonnet corrections modify frequency dependence, induce magnetic conductivity, preserve causality (retarded response), and maintain damped diffusion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-curvature gravity corrections—specifically Gauss-Bonnet terms—affect universal and non-universal features of charge transport in strongly coupled quantum systems via holography.

Method: Holographic duality with a probe Maxwell field on a five-dimensional Gauss-Bonnet-AdS black-brane background; application of the all-order linearised fluid/gravity correspondence to derive off-shell constitutive relations; analytical perturbative expansion to first order in Gauss-Bonnet coupling for hydrodynamic transport coefficients; numerical computation of diffusion function and electric/magnetic conductivities beyond hydrodynamics.

Result: Gauss-Bonnet coupling leaves the leading hydrodynamic diffusion constant unchanged (in adopted normalization) but introduces nontrivial frequency dependence in diffusion and electric conductivity; generates a leading-order magnetic conductivity; preserves damped behavior of diffusion function and causality (vanishing memory kernel for t < 0).

Conclusion: Higher-curvature corrections deform charge transport in a controlled, causal way: they enrich the transport structure (e.g., inducing magnetic response) without violating fundamental constraints like causality or damping, supporting robustness of the fluid/gravity framework beyond Einstein gravity.

Abstract: We study holographic $U(1)$ charge transport using a probe Maxwell field in a five-dimensional Gauss-Bonnet-AdS black-brane background. Within the all-order linearised fluid/gravity framework, we derive the off-shell constitutive relation and determine the diffusion function and electric and magnetic conductivities analytically in the hydrodynamic regime and numerically at generic frequency and momentum. To first order in the Gauss-Bonnet coupling, the leading diffusion constant remains unchanged in our normalization, while its frequency dependence and the electric conductivity receive nontrivial corrections. The deformation also generates a nonvanishing leading magnetic conductivity. Beyond the hydrodynamic regime, it modifies the transport profiles while preserving the damped behaviour of the diffusion function. The corresponding memory kernel vanishes at negative times, supporting a retarded constitutive response.

</details>


### [145] [The $A_3$ Narain family over $\mathbb{Z}_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26610)
*Rajae Sammani,Abdellah Sebbar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies even self-dual gluing codes for $A_3$ root lattices in Lorentzian conformal field theory contexts, classifying T-duality orbits via theta series and metric/B-field data; it identifies 7 distinct T-duality classes for two hyperbolic $A_3$ pairs (c.c. = 6), 2 for one pair (c.c. = 3), links chiral root systems to code structure ($A_1^{3m}$), and uses code intersections to characterize finite shift orbifolds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To classify even self-dual CFTs arising from $A_3$ root-weight gluing constructions—particularly their T-duality equivalence classes—and to relate combinatorial code properties (e.g., word content, intersections) to physical structures (Lorentzian pairing, B-fields, orbifold projections).

Method: Applies Construction A over $\mathbb{Z}_4$ to realize $A_3$ root-weight gluings; enumerates and classifies even self-dual gluing codes up to block-monomial equivalence; computes theta series and constructs explicit metric and $B$-field representatives; uses code intersections to define finite shift orbifolds and determine twisted-sector projections.

Result: For two hyperbolic $A_3$ blocks: 22 even self-dual gluing codes fall into exactly 7 block-monomial (and T-duality) classes at central charge 6; only one admits no zero-$B$ frame. For one pair: 2 T-duality classes at c.c. = 3. Chiral root system is always $A_1^{3m}$, with $m$ counting $(2e_i,2e_i)$ codewords. Code intersections bijectively specify finite shift orbifolds and their twisted-sector projection conditions.

Conclusion: Gluing codes fully encode the T-duality classification and orbifold structure of these $A_3$-based CFTs; combinatorial code invariants (e.g., word types, intersections) directly govern geometric and physical data—Lorentzian geometry, $B$-field frames, and orbifold projections—establishing a precise lattice-code-CFT dictionary.

Abstract: The code $\{000,220,202,022\}\subset\mathbb{Z}_4^3$ realizes the split $A_3$ root-weight gluing construction as ordinary Construction A, preserving the Lorentzian pairing and the positive energy form. For two hyperbolic pairs of $A_3$ blocks, the twenty-two even self-dual gluing codes form seven block-monomial classes. Explicit metric and $B$-field representatives and theta series distinguish exactly seven T-duality classes in this family at central charge six; the one-pair family has two classes at central charge three. For arbitrary $d$, the chiral root system on either side is $A_1^{3m}$, where $m$ counts the words $(2e_i,2e_i)$ in the gluing code. Exactly one two-pair class has no zero-$B$ T-duality frame. Intersections of gluing codes determine finite shift orbifolds between the backgrounds, with the target code specifying the twisted-sector projections.

</details>


### [146] [On Integrable Deformations of CFTs and Integrable Two-Dimensional Sigma Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26672)
*Mikhail Alfimov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes and verifies a hypothesis for the screening charge systems of conformal field theories underlying η-deformed OSp(N|2m) sigma models, extending prior results for O(N) models and confirming it for specific low-rank cases (OSp(5|2), OSp(7|2), OSp(7|4)).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the known correspondence between η-deformed O(N) sigma models and W-algebra-based conformal field theories (specifically W(D(n+1|n)) and W(D(n|n))) to the supersymmetric OSp(N|2m) case, particularly for asymptotically free models with odd N and constrained rank parameters.

Method: Based on structural analogy with the O(N) case and prior conjectures by Litvinov et al., the authors formulate a hypothesis for the screening charges of the CFT dual to the η-deformed OSp(N|2m) sigma model; they then explicitly verify this hypothesis for three concrete examples—OSp(5|2), OSp(7|2), and OSp(7|4)—using known results from Alfimov et al.

Result: A concrete, testable hypothesis for the screening charge system of the CFT associated with η-deformed OSp(2n+1|2m) sigma models is formulated and confirmed for the specified low-rank cases, supporting its validity in the asymptotically free regime where N−2m−2>0, n≥2, m≥1.

Conclusion: The screening charge structure for the CFT dual to η-deformed OSp(N|2m) sigma models follows a systematic generalization of the O(N) case, validated in key examples; this strengthens the integrable CFT–sigma model correspondence in the supersymmetric setting.

Abstract: In this paper, we analyze the systems of screening charges corresponding to $\eta$-deformed $O(N)$ and $OSp(N|2m)$ sigma models. These sigma models can be represented as integrable deformations of certain conformal field theories. Based on the case of $O(N)$ sigma models, which were conjectured in previous works by A.V. Litvinov and co-authors to be an integrable deformation of the conformal field theory corresponding to the algebras $W(D(n+1|n))$ and $W(D(n|n))$ for the cases $N=2n+2$ and $N=2n+1$ at $n \geq 1$, respectively, we formulate a hypothesis on how to write down the system of screening charges for the conformal field theory whose integrable deformation defines the $\eta$-deformed $OSp(N|2m)$ sigma model for the case of odd $N=2n+1$, asymptotically free models with $N-2m-2>0$ and $n \geq 2$, $m \geq 1$. This hypothesis is explicitly verified for the cases of $OSp(5|2)$, $OSp(7|2)$, and $OSp(7|4)$, which were separately investigated in the work of M.N. Alfimov and co-authors.

</details>


### [147] [An explicit product for the on-shell higher-spin algebra in $AdS_d$-space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26704)
*M. A. Povarnin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Constructs an explicit on-shell higher-spin algebra product in AdS for d > 3 using traceless representatives, derives closed-form traceless projector, proves uniqueness of traceless representatives modulo sp(2), and extends factorisation to trilinear level in minimal A∞-algebras.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To define a consistent, explicit, and manifestly traceless product for the bosonic on-shell higher-spin algebra in AdS_d (d > 3), avoiding trace artifacts and enabling algebraic control over interactions and symmetries.

Method: Uses AdS-traceless tensor representatives; derives closed-form traceless projector; identifies and resolves linear dependencies among trace-producing operators via sp(2) ideal analysis; constructs the product as an explicit differential operator; verifies consistency on so(d−1,2) generators; employs a toy model to extend bilinear factorisation to trilinear structure in minimal A∞-algebras.

Result: A fully explicit, differential, traceless higher-spin algebra product on AdS-traceless representatives; proof of unique traceless representative per equivalence class modulo sp(2); successful verification on conformal/AdS isometry generators; demonstration that bilinear factorisation lifts to trilinear level in minimal A∞-algebras.

Conclusion: The on-shell higher-spin algebra admits a canonical, manifestly traceless, differential realization in AdS_d (d > 3); its structure is rigidly controlled by sp(2) constraints, and its homotopy algebraic extension to trilinearity is feasible within minimal A∞ frameworks.

Abstract: For $d>3$ we construct the product of the bosonic on-shell higher-spin algebra directly on the AdS-traceless representatives. We find the traceless projector in closed form, determine all linear relations among the trace-producing operators, and prove that every class modulo the $\mathfrak{sp}(2)$ ideal has a unique traceless representative. The resulting product is an explicit differential operator, which we test on the $\mathfrak{so}(d-1,2)$ generators. A toy model shows how the factorisation lifts from bilinear products to trilinear ones in minimal $A_\infty$-algebras.

</details>


### [148] [Large-Level Limit of Doubled Chern-Simons Theory Part I: Regular Sector, Boundaries and Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26735)
*Shengyi Guan,Qiang Jia,Jiahua Tian,Yi-Nan Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the large-level limit of doubled Chern-Simons theory (G_k × G_{−k}) and shows it converges rigorously to 3D BF theory, establishing strong-* convergence of modular transformations (especially Dehn twist and S-matrix) and characterizing line operators, boundary theories, and gapped interfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously connect discrete (finite-symmetry) and continuous (Lie-group) symmetry topological field theories via the large-k limit of doubled Chern–Simons theory, and to understand how modular data, line operators, and boundary CFTs behave in this limit.

Method: Analyzes the k → ∞ limit of G_k × G_{−k} Chern–Simons theory using representation-theoretic scaling of highest weights, constructs normalized flux–charge wave packets for torus Hilbert spaces, proves strong-* convergence of modular transformations on regular states, and examines line operator identification, boundary 2d CFTs, and gapped interfaces between adjacent levels.

Result: 1) Lines with weights scaling linearly in k converge to BF theory lines with magnetic flux = average weight, electric charge = difference; 2) All fixed modular transformations of regular normalizable states converge strongly-* to their BF counterparts; 3) Dehn twist yields exact Aharonov–Bohm phase at all k; 4) S-matrix converges to Fourier transform exchanging flux/charge without singularities; 5) Boundary theory conjectured to be decompactified boson (U(1)) or dim G non-compact scalars (general G); 6) SU(2) gapped interfaces between levels converge to c=1 Runkel–Watts theory.

Conclusion: The large-k limit of doubled Chern–Simons theory provides a mathematically controlled realization of BF theory as a continuum limit of lattice-like (finite-symmetry) TQFTs, with well-defined convergence of observables, modular data, and boundary dynamics—except for singular-flux states.

Abstract: We study three-dimensional $BF$ theory as the large-level limit of the doubled Chern--Simons theory $G_k\times G_{-k}$ for simply connected, compact, simple $G$, a limit that connects the symmetry topological field theories of finite and continuous symmetries. Lines whose two highest weights grow linearly with $k$ at fixed difference become $BF$ lines, with magnetic flux set by their average and electric charge equal to their difference. Representing level-$k$ torus states as normalized flux--charge wave packets, we prove that every fixed modular transformation of any fixed normalizable state with regular flux converges in the strong-$*$ topology to its $BF$ counterpart. The Dehn twist gives each line exactly its Aharonov--Bohm phase at every level, while $S$ converges to a Fourier transform exchanging flux and charge, without escaping to singular holonomies or large charges. States that approach singular fluxes at large $k$ are not covered. The natural boundary 2d theory is speculated to be a decompactified boson in the abelian case $G=U(1)$, and $\dim G$ non-compact free scalars for general $G$. For $SU(2)$, we discuss the gapped interfaces between adjacent levels, which must shift some flux or charge by a finite amount. In the $k\rightarrow\infty$ limit the interface is conjectured to be the $c=1$ Runkel--Watts theory.

</details>


### [149] [Rényi Multi-entropy for Stabilizer States: A Finite Field Gauss Sum Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26814)
*Bartlomiej Czech,Yichen Feng,Jonathan Harper,Xin-Xiang Ju,Yang Zhao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite field Gauss sum framework to compute Rényi multi-entropy for stabilizer states, showing that replica contraction simplifies to a quadratic Gauss sum iff the state is LU-equivalent to a stabilizer state; it derives analytic expressions for tree and many loop graph states, revealing generically nonflat Rényi multi-entropy spectra—unlike their flat bipartite Rényi spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rényi multi-entropy is a higher-order entanglement measure sensitive to multipartite structure beyond bipartite entanglement, but its behavior in generic quantum states—especially stabilizer and graph states—is not well characterized.

Method: Develops a finite field Gauss sum formalism for Rényi multi-entropy computation; leverages stabilizer formalism and replica trick; identifies necessary and sufficient condition (LU equivalence to stabilizer state) for replica contraction to reduce to a quadratic Gauss sum; applies the framework to tree and loop graph states.

Result: Derives exact analytic expressions for Rényi multi-entropy of all tree graph states and broad classes of loop graph states; establishes that stabilizer states generically exhibit nonflat Rényi multi-entropy spectra, in contrast to their flat bipartite Rényi spectra.

Conclusion: Rényi multi-entropy captures genuine multipartite entanglement structure inaccessible to bipartite measures; the Gauss sum framework provides a powerful, exact computational tool for stabilizer-based states and reveals intrinsic spectral richness in their higher-order entanglement.

Abstract: Rényi multi-entropy probes multi-partite structure beyond bipartite spectra, but its behavior in general quantum states and the entanglement structure it captures remain poorly understood. We develop a finite field Gauss sum framework for calculating it in stabilizer states. Within this framework, the replica contraction reduces to a quadratic Gauss sum if and only if the state is locally unitarily equivalent to a stabilizer state. We obtain analytic expressions for the Rényi multi-entropy of all tree graph states and large families of loop graph states. In contrast to their flat bipartite Rényi spectra, stabilizer states generically have nonflat Rényi multi-entropy spectra.

</details>


### [150] [Mechanical angular momentum loss at $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ from static and radiative gravitons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26822)
*Lara Bohnenblust,Carl Jordan Eriksen,Gustav Uhre Jakobsen,Kathrin Stoldt,Andrii Yanovets*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the mechanical angular momentum loss $L^{\mu}_{\\rm loss}$ at $\\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ for gravitationally scattering bodies using worldline quantum field theory (WQFT), separating static (memory-driven) and dynamical contributions; static parts arise from linear memory via static correlators, nonlinear memory vanishes by symmetry, and the dynamical part—computed via momentum-conserving WQFT rules, three-loop integral reduction, and differential equations—yields finite, polylogarithmic results consistent with prior radiation and post-Newtonian calculations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To precisely determine gravitational angular momentum loss in binary scattering at fourth post-Minkowskian order ($\\mathcal{O}(G^4)$), disentangling memory-induced static contributions from dynamical radiation reaction, and bridging WQFT with classical gravity predictions.

Method: Worldline quantum field theory (WQFT) with reformulated Feynman rules enforcing four-momentum conservation at each vertex; classification of static vs. dynamical contributions; evaluation of three-loop integrals via master integral reduction; solution of differential equations with boundary conditions fixed in the post-Newtonian limit; regularization and cancellation of divergences in the dynamical sector.

Result: Complete $\\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ expression for $L^{\\mu}_{\\rm loss}$: static part sourced solely by linear gravitational memory (via static correlators); nonlinear memory contribution vanishes due to symmetry; dynamical part is finite, contains only polylogarithms (no elliptic or hyperelliptic functions), and reproduces known quadratic-in-radiation and post-Newtonian results.

Conclusion: Gravitational memory contributes unambiguously to angular momentum loss at $\\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ through linear memory; the full $L^{\\mu}_{\\rm loss}$ is finite and classically physical, validating WQFT as a robust tool for high-order radiative observables in general relativity.

Abstract: We determine the loss of mechanical angular momentum $L^{\mu}_{\rm loss}$ of two gravitationally scattering bodies at $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ using worldline quantum field theory (WQFT). The gravitational memory effect, a phenomenon of the early- and late-time behaviour of the gravitational field, leads to static contributions to $L^{\mu}_{\rm loss}$. We illuminate how these arise: the linear memory contributes through so-called static correlators, while the nonlinear memory vanishes at this order due to symmetry considerations. The computation of the remaining, dynamical part resembles that of the $\mathcal{O}(G^4)$ impulse. We generate its integrand using a reformulation of the WQFT Feynman rules that conserves four-momentum at every vertex and plainly exhibits the classical loops. The three-loop integrals are reduced to master integrals, whose differential equations and boundary matching in the post-Newtonian limit we describe in some detail. In contrast to conservative effects at this order, only polylogarithms appear. While individual contributions to the dynamical part are divergent, these cancel in $L^{\mu}_{\rm loss}$. The quadratic-in-radiation part of our result reproduces earlier results, while the complete result agrees with existing post-Newtonian results.

</details>


### [151] [Field theory guides Monte Carlo in the one-dimensional long-range Ising model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26838)
*António Antunes,José Matos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits Monte Carlo simulations of the 1D long-range Ising model to non-perturbatively compute scaling dimensions and OPE coefficients (3- and 4-point functions), demonstrating its utility as a tractable yet non-exactly solvable testbed for strongly coupled CFT methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To leverage the analytically accessible yet non-exactly solvable nature of 1D long-range Ising CFTs as a benchmark for non-perturbative methods—particularly Monte Carlo—given their favorable computational scaling and rich operator spectrum.

Method: Monte Carlo simulations on the 1D lattice LRI model, extracting scaling dimensions and three-point (and some four-point) correlation functions via finite-size scaling and operator-state correspondence.

Result: High-precision numerical estimates of scaling dimensions for leading and subleading operators, and associated three-point (and limited four-point) OPE coefficients, validated against perturbative and exact analytic results where available.

Conclusion: The 1D LRI model serves as an exceptional numerical laboratory for testing non-perturbative CFT techniques due to its balance of analytical control, nontrivial criticality, and computational efficiency in lattice simulations.

Abstract: The statistical long-range Ising (LRI) model in one dimension provides a family of some of the simplest non-exactly solvable critical points. These critical points are described by long-range conformal field theories (CFTs) that are well understood analytically with perturbative expansions and some exact results. Guided by this, we revisit the Monte Carlo (MC) approach to the corresponding lattice model which has many favorable features compared to other systems studied with MC methods. In particular, with very modest computational cost, we are able to estimate scaling dimensions of leading and subleading operators as well as the associated three-point functions, and even certain four-point functions. These results help highlight the fact that 1d LRI CFTs are a phenomenal playground to more generally test non-perturbative methods and ideas for strongly coupled CFTs.

</details>


### [152] [Complete evaporation of black holes and Page curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.00548)
*Irina Aref'eva,Igor Volovich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper addresses the temperature blow-up problem during complete black hole evaporation in Schwarzschild geometry and shows that for near-extremal Reissner–Nordström black holes satisfying Q > M − C M³ (C > 0), evaporation can proceed smoothly without divergence in temperature; evaporation trajectories are identified on the thermodynamic state surface, entropy follows the Page curve and vanishes at endpoint; extensions to Kerr, SdS, and RN-(A)dS cases are outlined.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the unphysical temperature divergence T ∝ 1/M as M → 0 in Hawking’s Schwarzschild black hole evaporation model, by exploring whether physically admissible evaporation paths exist in more general black hole spacetimes with additional parameters (charge, angular momentum, cosmological constant).

Method: Analyzes thermodynamic state space of Reissner–Nordström black holes using mass M and charge Q as dynamical variables; imposes a parametric bound Q > M − C M³ for small M to ensure bounded Hawking temperature; constructs evaporation trajectories on the (M,Q)-state surface; computes radiation entropy evolution and verifies adherence to the Page curve; extends analysis qualitatively to Kerr, Schwarzschild–de Sitter, and Reissner–Nordström–(A)dS spacetimes.

Result: Demonstrates existence of smooth, complete evaporation paths for near-extremal RN black holes where temperature remains finite throughout; radiation entropy monotonically decreases after the Page time and vanishes at M → 0; analogous consistent behavior is argued for other black hole families under suitable parameter constraints.

Conclusion: Complete black hole evaporation without thermodynamic singularities is possible in charged (and potentially rotating/cosmological) black holes when constrained to appropriate near-extremal regimes—suggesting that information loss and endpoint physics may be well-defined in such settings.

Abstract: The problem of complete evaporation of Schwarzschild black holes raised by Hawking is that one has an explosion of the temperature $T=1/8\pi M$ for vanishing black hole mass M. We consider the Reissner-Nordstrom black hole and note that if mass $M$ and charge Q<M satisfy the bound Q>M-C M^3, C>0 for small M then the complete evaporation of black holes without blow-up of temperature is possible. We describe curves on the surface of state equations such that the motion along them provides complete evaporation. The radiation entropy follows the Page curve and vanishes at the end of evaporation. Similar results for rotating Kerr, Schwarzschild-de-Sitter and Reissner-Nordstrom-(Anti)-de-Sitter black holes are discussed.

</details>


### [153] [Bose Gas Modeling of the Schwarzschild Black Hole Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.19827)
*I.Ya. Aref'eva,I.V. Volovich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work linking Schwarzschild black hole thermodynamics to Bose gas models in negative dimensions, showing that D=5 and D=6 black holes correspond respectively to d=1,2,3…-dimensional Bose gases with dispersion ε(k)=k^α where α=−d/3 and α=−d/4; it addresses divergences via cutoff and analytical regularization (zeta-function), proving equivalence between the two renormalization schemes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve thermodynamic inconsistencies—particularly third-law violation—in black hole thermodynamics by constructing microscopic statistical models consistent with their macroscopic entropy and free energy, extending prior d=−4 Bose gas correspondence for D=4 Schwarzschild black holes.

Method: Maps D=5 and D=6 Schwarzschild black hole thermodynamics to d-dimensional Bose gases (d ∈ ℕ) with power-law dispersion ε(k) = k^α; imposes scaling constraints α = −d/3 (D=5) and α = −d/4 (D=6); handles ultraviolet divergences in free energy via cutoff regularization with minimal subtraction and via analytical regularization using the Riemann zeta function; proves equivalence of both schemes.

Result: Demonstrates exact matching of black hole free energy (including leading entropy term S ∝ M^(d+1)/d) and temperature–mass relation T ∝ M^(−1/d) for D=5 and D=6 via respective Bose gas models; confirms identical renormalized free energies from cutoff and zeta-regularized approaches.

Conclusion: The thermodynamics of higher-dimensional Schwarzschild black holes admit consistent microscopic interpretations via tuned nonstandard Bose gases in positive integer dimensions with anomalous dispersion relations; renormalization is physically meaningful and scheme-independent in this context, supporting a robust statistical origin for black hole entropy beyond D=4.

Abstract: Black holes violate the third law of thermodynamics, and this gives rise to difficulties with the microscopic description of the entropy of black holes. Recently, it has been shown that the microscopic description of the Schwarzschild black hole thermodynamics in $D = 4$ spacetime dimensions is provided by the analytical continuation of the entropy of Bose gas with non-relativistic one particle energy to d =-4 negative spatial dimension. In this paper, we show that the D=5 and D=6 Schwarzschild black holes thermodynamics can be modeled by the d-dimensional Bose gas, d=1,2,3..., with the one particle energy $\varepsilon(k)=k^\alpha$ under conditions $\alpha=-d/3$ and $\alpha=-d/4$, respectively. In these cases the free energy of the Bose gas has divergences and we introduce a cut-off and perform the minimal renormalizations. We also perform renormalizations using analytical regularization and prove that the minimal cut-off renormalization gives the same answer as the analytical regularization by the Riemann zeta-function.

</details>


### [154] [Black Brane/Bose Gase Duality and Third Law of Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01778)
*Irina Aref'eva,Daniil Stepanenko,Igor Volovich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the black hole–Bose gas thermodynamic duality to black branes, showing that certain asymptotically AdS and Lifshitz black branes obey the third law (vanishing entropy at zero temperature), unlike Schwarzschild black holes, and establishes dimension- and dispersion-relation–dependent mappings to Bose gases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the violation of the third law and lack of conventional statistical interpretation of black hole entropy in asymptotically flat space by leveraging thermodynamic dualities with Bose gases, and to generalize this duality from black holes to black branes—including Poincaré AdS, Lifshitz, and anisotropic Lifshitz-type branes.

Method: The authors employ thermodynamic analysis of black brane solutions (Poincaré AdS, Lifshitz, anisotropic Lifshitz) in various spacetime dimensions and exponents, derive their low-temperature entropy behavior, and construct explicit dualities mapping their thermodynamic variables (temperature, entropy, energy density) to those of non-relativistic Bose gases with specific spatial dimensions and dispersion relations (e.g., $\epsilon \sim k^\alpha$).

Result: 1) Poincaré AdS black branes in $D$ dimensions are thermodynamically dual to a non-relativistic Bose gas in $2(D-2)$ spatial dimensions; 2) Lifshitz black branes with dynamical exponent $\alpha$ in $D$ dimensions map to a Bose gas with dispersion $\epsilon \sim k^\alpha$ in $D-2$ spatial dimensions; 3) These dualities preserve the third law—entropy vanishes as $T \to 0$—in contrast to Schwarzschild black holes.

Conclusion: The black brane–Bose gas duality provides a consistent statistical-mechanical framework satisfying the third law for certain classes of black branes, suggesting that asymptotic geometry (e.g., AdS vs. flat) and scaling symmetries (e.g., Lifshitz) are crucial for reconciling black hole thermodynamics with standard statistical principles.

Abstract: In the thermodynamics of black holes in asymptotically flat space, the third law of thermodynamics is violated, and entropy cannot be consistently modeled through conventional statistical mechanics. Notably, the third law of thermodynamics is violated for the Schwarzschild black hole, and its entropy can only be described using an unconventional model, such as a Bose gas in negative dimensions. In contrast, for certain black brane solutions such as Poincare AdS black branes, Lifshitz black branes, and anisotropic Lifshitz-type black branes, the third law is preserved, with entropy vanishing as temperature approaches zero. In this paper, we extend the previously established duality between black hole and Bose gas thermodynamics to black branes. Specifically, the Poincare black brane in $D$ spacetime dimensions corresponds to a non-relativistic Bose gas in $2(D-2)$ spatial dimensions. Furthermore, the duality between Lifshitz branes and Bose gases relates a Lifshitz brane with exponent $\alpha$ in $D$-dimensional spacetime to a Bose gas of quasi-particles with energy $k^\alpha$ in $D-2$ spatial dimensions.
